\ \ge\ p^7(1-p)^{17}\bigl(1-759r-2576r^5-759r^9-r^{17}\bigr)
\ >\ \tfrac15\,p^7(1-p)^{17},
\qquad r=\frac p{1-p}.
\tag{R.4.4c.2}
$$
Thus, for every such $p$, $\mathcal G_{24}$ is the unique minimizer of $P_{\mathrm{ud}}(\,\cdot\,;p)$ on the binary linear $[24,12]$ class up to coordinate permutation, and the undetected-error functional strictly separates $\mathcal G_{24}$ from the code $D$ that Theorem R.4.4b ties with it.

*Proof.* An error goes undetected exactly when it is a nonzero codeword, which gives (R.4.4c.1). The octads of $\mathcal G_{24}$ form the Steiner system $S(5,8,24)$ used in Proposition R.4.4a, so $A_8=\binom{24}{5}/\binom85=759$. The code is doubly even and contains the all-one word, so its nonzero weights lie in $\{8,12,16,24\}$ with $A_{16}=A_8$ and $A_{24}=1$, and $|\mathcal G_{24}|=2^{12}$ gives $A_{12}=4096-2-2\cdot759=2576$. Hence
$$
P_{\mathrm{ud}}(\mathcal G_{24};p)=p^7(1-p)^{17}\bigl(759r+2576r^5+759r^9+r^{17}\bigr).
$$
If $C$ is not equivalent to $\mathcal G_{24}$, the bound $d\le8$ and the classification of binary linear $[24,12,8]$ codes in Theorem Z.13 give $d:=d(C)\le7$. Then $A_d(C)\ge1$ and, since $0<r<1$,
$$
P_{\mathrm{ud}}(C;p)\ge p^d(1-p)^{24-d}=p^7(1-p)^{17}r^{d-7}\ge p^7(1-p)^{17}.
$$
Subtraction gives the first inequality in (R.4.4c.2). The bracket decreases in $r$, which increases in $p$; at $p=10^{-3}$, $r=1/999$, so $759r<0.7598$ and the remaining three terms are below $10^{-10}$. ∎

<a id="theorem-r44d-full-channel-physical-detection-objective-and-golay-selection"></a>**Theorem R.4.4d (Full-Channel Physical Detection Objective and Golay Selection).** On the supplied finite oscillator/control/readout branch of Proposition R.4.2.8e, with the same marked twenty-four dual-rail data coordinates, there is a complete finite code-program/source/detection/reward experiment for every binary linear $[24,12]$ code and every supplied real $0<p\le 10^{-3}$. Its source channel is the full iid binary symmetric channel on every data state and untouched reference. Its actual committed pure-syndrome binary Brier loss is the undetected-error probability of Proposition R.4.4c. An explicitly funded common source package, a physical correctness reward and a single complete physical window realize a PCE rate with that loss and a positive coefficient. The unique minimizer up to coordinate permutation is the marked extended Golay code, with strict rate gap greater than $Bp^7(1-p)^{17}/(5T_{\mathrm{run}})$.

All source errors, old quotient-charge/validity changes, off-ready failure branches, private archives, auxiliary records, control and preparation resources are retained. The comparison fixes the original pure-syndrome detector class and the supplied exact-source primitives; it does not optimize over arbitrary alarm policies or assert a new complexity minimum, autonomous manufacture or a uniform noisy-fabrication margin.

*Proof.*

**1. Original class, catalogue and task**

For every supplied real $p$ with $0<p\le 1/1000$, admit ALL binary linear $[24,12]$ codes, each indexed once by its unique binary reduced-row-echelon $12\times 24$ generator $G_{C}$. The exhaustive algorithm chooses 12 increasing pivot columns, sets their identity columns, and enumerates every binary assignment to nonpivot entries strictly to the right of each row's pivot. No code is removed by a support, Golay-validity, distance or favorable-performance filter. Its size is

$$
N=\prod_{i=0}^{11} (2^{24}-2^i)/(2^{12}-2^i)
$$

$$
=77184136346814161837268404381760884963259795.
$$

The final equivalence is exactly coordinate permutation. For each nonpivot $j$, the parity-check row has bit $j=1$ and bit $\mathrm{pivot}_i=G_C[i,j]$; these 12 rows form $H_{C}$, with $\ker H_C=C$. The RREF enumeration is the exact finite constructor. The catalogue is intentionally enormous, not claimed practically materialized.

Prepare $M$ uniform on $\mathbb{F}_2^{12}$ and independent $E$ with 24 iid $\operatorname{Ber}(p)$ bits. The actual clean data is $x=MG_C$, delivered data $Y=x+E$. The fixed pure-syndrome detector reports $d=\mathbf{1}[H_CY=0]$. Its hidden prediction target is $D=\mathbf{1}[E=0]$, meaning that the delivered block was uncorrupted. The source error mask, clean message/archive and $D$ are private until after $d$ is committed; no receiver coupling reads them. A post-report verifier can use them, but has no feedback wire to the reported response. The comparison varies the code, not the alarm policy or unrestricted probabilistic forecasting rule. This is the original pure-error-detection class.

For EVERY M,E,

$$
(d-D)^2=\mathbf{1}[E\in C\setminus \{0\}].
$$

Indeed $H_CY=H_CE$; $E=0$ gives $d=D=1$, and for $E\ne 0$ the binary loss is 1 exactly when $E$ lies in $C$. The expected actual binary Brier score is therefore

$$
L_C=\sum_{w=1}^{24} A_w(C)p^w(1-p)^{24-w}=P_{\mathrm{ud}}(C;p).
$$

This is an actual PU proper-score task under Definition 7, not an assigned distance penalty. No detected error is discarded or postselected away. Neither pure-syndrome optimality over other alarm policies nor an architecture-universal optimum is claimed; Definition 15 uses the specified admissible comparison class.

**2. Exact finite full-space programme, including every error**

Original tensor factors before compiler scratch are:

-24 data bits, on the SAME marked24 dual-rail registers;

-12 message bits and 12 private purification/archive bits;

-24 private error bits and 24 private purification/archive bits;

-12 syndrome bits, report bit d, private target bit D, private correctness bit A;

-one N-level catalogue register;

-one three-level reward register, labels bank/useful/dump.

Thus there are 111 two-level factors and two further factors, $r=113$ and $D_{\mathrm{dim}}=3N\,2^{111}$. All unused qubit factors start 0. The catalogue factor starts at the selected $C$; the reward starts at bank. Earlier records remain owned. The tensor cut between data, message, source mask, detector and verifier is fixed.

Four explicit unitaries on this FULL finite tensor product define the programme:

$U_1$: apply Hadamard on each message bit and CNOT to its archive; on each error bit apply the real rotation $|0\rangle\to\sqrt{1-p}\,|0\rangle+\sqrt{p}\,|1\rangle$, $|1\rangle\to-\sqrt{p}\,|0\rangle+\sqrt{1-p}\,|1\rangle$, then CNOT to its private archive. For each code-program basis state $C$, XOR $MG_C$ into the 24 data bits by the explicit controlled-CNOT truth table. The coherent block-diagonal programme is $\sum_C|C\rangle\langle C|\otimes U_{1,C}$ with those exact common phase conventions.

$U_2$: for each $i=1,\dots,24$ apply controlled $X$ to $\mathrm{DATA}_i$ with $\mathrm{ERROR}_i$ as control. Its ready-environment isometry on arbitrary data is

$$
V_p|\psi\rangle=\sum_e \sqrt{p^{|e|}(1-p)^{24-|e|}}\, X_e|\psi\rangle\otimes|e\rangle_E\otimes|e\rangle_{\mathrm{archive}}.
$$

It is an isometry on the entire $2^{24}$-dimensional data space, not only a Golay sector. Tracing the private error factors gives the complete BSC CP map. Retaining their labels gives its complete refined instrument. The identity is valid with every untouched quantum reference. No error mask is clipped or rejected.

$U_3$: XOR $H_CY$ into the 12 syndrome bits; XOR the predicate $[\text{syndrome}=0]$ into $d$. Read $d$ by the complete occupation instrument and seal its public output. The syndrome is retained; it can be included in the declared detector transcript without changing $d$. No target or clean-copy input is accessible to $U_3$.

$U_4$: after that commitment, XOR $[E=0]$ into $D$ and XOR $[d=D]$ into $A$. If $A=1$ swap bank and useful reward levels; if $A=0$ swap bank and dump. These are disjoint-transposition extensions on the WHOLE reward factor, not an overwrite of an occupied register. Measure the reward output with every level/outcome retained. These predicates and transpositions specify the exact basis action, including nonblank data/syndrome/record inputs; the quantum action is its linear extension, together with $U_1$'s explicitly stated rotations.

All predicates and controlled permutations above are finite explicit matrices, not free external algorithms. They are synthesized by the next section's actual finite compiler. Correctness/target processing occurs strictly after report release. Access to private archives as a terminating source audit does not provide a detector feedback port or a new eligible decision rule.

**3. Same oscillator substrate and positive physical compiler**

Use exactly R.4.2.8e.1: each $q$-level factor has $q$ separate bosonic rails, equal frequency $\Omega>0$, embedding $S_q|j\rangle=b_j^\dagger|\mathrm{vac}\rangle$, $H_q^0=\hbar\Omega\sum_j n_j$, and $T=2\pi/\Omega$. In particular $S_D=S_2^{\otimes 24}$ preserves the original physical coordinate support. Each original factor holds one excitation. The entire $2^{24}$ data-word sector is ready: neither Golay $P$ nor $P_{\mathrm{good}}$ is a source admission check. Non-Golay comparator codewords lying in old $P_{\mathrm{perp}}$ are legitimate inputs.

For the four concrete unitaries use the explicit Lemma R.4.2.8d.1 compiler: lexicographic complex Givens elimination, lexicographic Gray paths, reversible equality flags/conjunctions, controlled phases and their coherent uncomputation. Its exact bound is

$$
B(d,k)=[d(d-1)/2+d](2k+1)(84k+4).
$$

Set $s=2r-1=225$ scratch dual rails and $L=\max(2,B(D_{\mathrm{dim}},r))$. Pad each word to exactly $L$. This is an actual finite synthesis algorithm for the four specified matrices; arbitrary unitary universality alone is not the source map. All ready scratch is returned on every original input. Exact algebraic $p$, including $p=1/1000$, gives an effective algebraic construction. For arbitrary supplied real $p$ the same finite matrix/control source family is mathematical; no algorithm prints every arbitrary real coefficient.

To include the same physical stage/clock convention as Proposition R.4.2.8e, add L stage rails with one excitation, initially at 0. For each compiled elementary number-preserving endpoint $U_{j}$ use its bounded full-Fock oscillator extension tilde $U_{j}$, and the admitted positive assisted-hopping pulse

$$
F_j=a_{j+1}-\tilde U_j a_j,
$$

$$
H_j(t)=H_0+\hbar\, g(t)F_j^{\dagger}F_j,
$$

$$
g(t)=(\pi/(2T))6(t/T)(1-t/T),\quad 0\le t\le T.
$$

The stage edge indices are cyclic. On its two occupied stage positions the interaction matrix has blocks $\begin{bmatrix} I & -U_j^\dagger \\ -U_j & I \end{bmatrix}$, squares to twice itself, and integral $g=\pi/2$; its exact endpoint transfers the stage and applies $U_{j}$. Full-period free evolution is identity on the integer occupation sectors. Thus each $L$-edge cycle implements the desired unitary and returns the stage 0/scratch 0. It is positive on full Fock space, preserves every register's total occupation, and is exactly defined on off-code sectors as well. Self-adjoint evolution is the direct sum over fixed-total-occupation finite sectors. No uniform energy or finite preparation-cost bound is asserted for arbitrary unbounded-occupation input states; the finite resource ledger is for the declared supplied ready ensemble. The original local rotations/Kerr gates supply the bounded coefficients; their elementary controls are explicitly those of Lemma R.4.2.8d.1, with that proof's finite compiler, rather than an unimplemented matrix oracle.

Five complete readiness services occur initially and after the four returned cycles. They test stage 0, one-excitation occupation in every active AND supplied auxiliary register (including all fresh and retained flags, pointer/purifier factors, and the wallet total-occupation sector) and scratch 0; they DO NOT test the Golay charge or decoder validity of Theorem R.4.2.8d. Each uses its own fresh dual-rail flag with the original coarse-predicate positive pulse $H_0+\hbar g(t)R_0(I-S_{\mathrm{flag}})$, duration $T$, and complete pointer readout of positive duration $t_{m}$. Both readiness outcomes and conditional states are retained. Failure aborts subsequent data operations and retains the failure transcript; nominal supplied ready states have zero such probability. All remaining registers have their actual free evolution on failure branches, so the overall instrument is normalized on the full Fock carrier.

Read d after cycle3 and reward after cycle4, each with its actual complete projective occupation service, duration $t_{m}$. There are seven main detector services in total, and one funded-guard detector in the payment prefix. The concrete finite apparatus and extra record-copy slots are specified in Section 5. Previous flags and private environments stay isolated/retained. The scheduled source is externally timed as the substrate explicitly permits; pulse timing/controller hardware and detector services are owned and priced. No autonomous-clock theorem is asserted.

At every completed edge, tensor occupancy embedding, the retained stage index and the compiler's scratch identification give the exact implemented unitary. Composition and conditional pointer outcomes therefore identify the entire four-block instrument, every declared finite continuation and untouched reference. Intermediate-stage coherences are retained by the same $V_{j}$ change-of-frame, but no conservation of the quotient charge of Theorem R.4.2.8d is inferred: the closing-edge commutator in e.10 fails for the charged BSC, exactly as it should. Physical intermediate time is the displayed source Hamiltonian, not an ideal instantaneous circuit checkpoint.

**4. All-weight audit of the marked Golay charge**

Keep the original marked Golay matrix and projectors $P_h,P_{\mathrm{perp}}$ literally. If $z=Ge^{\mathsf T}$ has a radius-three leader $\ell(z)$, output on code input $c_0$ has decoded codeword $c_0+e+\ell(z)$, and charge shift $h(e+\ell(z))$; otherwise it lies in $P_{\mathrm{perp}}$. The good enumerator is

$$
G_{\mathrm{good}}(z)=\sum_w A_w \sum_{j=0}^3 \sum_i \binom{w}{i}\binom{24-w}{j-i}z^{w+j-2i},
$$

with $A_0,A_8,A_{12},A_{16},A_{24}=1,759,2576,759,1$. Neutral-good is $\sum_{j=0}^3\binom{24}{j}(z^j+z^{24-j})$; charged-good is the difference, and exceptional is $(1+z)^{24}-G_{\mathrm{good}}(z)$. These exhaust all $2^{24}$ masks, with totals 4650,9518550,7254016 respectively. Every weight 4 mask is exceptional. Codeword masks outside $\{0,1\}$ change $h$. Each has positive BSC probability, so data quotient-charge/validity conservation is false for this extension. Each rail-pair occupation and free oscillator energy is conserved by the actual noise coupling; those are different observables. The charge measurements and restricted recovery of Theorem R.4.2.8d retain their exact meaning when separately invoked; this theorem does not promise correction of all BSC masks.

The complete BSC isometry and the displayed disjoint leader/coset enumeration give the all-weight identities.

**5. Complete common inventory, paid boundary and clock**

The base compiled apparatus has

$$
N_{\mathrm{rails}}=222+N+3+2s+L,
$$

$$
E_0=(r+s+1)\hbar\Omega=339\hbar\Omega,
$$

five additional readiness flags (ten rails, five excitations), and the explicitly finite detector/payment apparatus below. The catalogue register, every compiled coefficient/program ROM, source preparation, all 225 scratch registers, all L stage rails, purification/archive factors and reward factor are included. No unused catalogue/program/control stock is unpriced. Identity padding and the same four blocks give a common clock for every C. Source loading has a declared positive duration $t_{\mathrm{load}}$ and includes every ready excitation/program setting and coefficient preparation; payment has duration $t_{\mathrm{pay}}>0$. The common release window is

$$
T_{\mathrm{run}}=t_{\mathrm{load}}+(4L+15)T+8t_m.
$$

Here payment uses a guard pulse, its record-copy pulse/readout and the transfer pulse; each of the seven main detector services uses its own additional record-copy pulse. Thus15 extra T slots are exactly 5 readiness pulses+2 payment guard/transfer pulses+8 detector record-copy pulses.

All early records remain held until that endpoint. The original code bound gives total gate action $\le 4\pi\hbar L$, peak interaction $\le 3\hbar\Omega/4$ on the code, absolute actuator-work bound $\le 12\pi\hbar L/T$. All 15 auxiliary pulses add $15\pi\hbar$ action and $45\pi\hbar/T$ absolute-work bound. Holding the base occupied 339 registers through the window and each readiness flag through its retained lifetime is explicit energy-time storage; the detector/ROM/controller holding has its own finite package entry. Signed drive work is zero between the equal-free-energy ready endpoints; the absolute-work bound is not relabeled as exact net expense. Preparation energy, bounded drive supply/recovery, detector operation and retained outputs are separate resource entries.

Register a finite positive supplied-package price $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}$ that covers this ENTIRE common stock and service vector, including all catalogue alternatives and padding, external clocks, readout, private-target computation, code-selection/preparation, compiler ROM and retention. It is one fixed paid package for all C at a fixed p. Before loading/activation, a spendable excitation of gap $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}$ is swapped into an inaccessible equal-gap supplier receipt, with the successful funded guard latched before delivery, by the explicit mechanism below. An unfunded reservation supplies no promised data service. The guard authorizes only this one scheduled programme; there is no unpaid preparation/control/readout interface or refund route. The finite source family is fully funded for each finite package; no infinite lifetime battery is claimed. Every imported energy, recovered drive energy, retained stock balance and dump is reconciled within that package, without charging purchased energy twice. This is an actual supplied-service expenditure boundary, not a claim that arbitrary supplier internals intrinsically dissipate $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}$. A common flat package can absorb code-dependent internal drive-exchange details because the entire finite catalogue/resources are supplied in advance and the actual debit is code-independent.

The reward stock $B$ is included ONCE in $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}$. Only the useful rail is released to the beneficiary; bank and dump remain provider-private and cannot be reclaimed or used to alter the detector. Take the reward quantum $B=\hbar\Omega>0$ already held in the three-level oscillator register. Its energy is unchanged by the correct/incorrect routing; useful delivered energy is $B$ if $d=D$, zero otherwise, and the remainder is in the inaccessible dump. Both routes, verdict apparatus and their costs are in $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}$. The actual mean source-boundary expense minus useful reward is $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}-B+BL_C$. The corresponding PCE rate is

$$
V_C=[E_{\mathrm{pkg}}-B+B P_{\mathrm{ud}}(C;p)]/T_{\mathrm{run}}.
$$

Thus the detection term is derived from the registered proper-score task, its actual physical correctness reward and full common prices. It is not assumed as a free code-dependent tariff. No double error penalty is added.

**Explicit finite detector and payment apparatus**

For a measured $q$-rail register, $q=2$ or 3, the finite coarse effects are $P_j=|\text{one excitation in rail }j\rangle\langle\text{one excitation in rail }j|$, $0\le j<q$, and $P_{\mathrm{fail}}=I-\sum_j P_j$. No occupation number is exported. Each service owns a $(q+1)$-rail pointer and a $(q+1)$-rail retained purifier, each initially one excitation in rail 0. Let $S_{a}$ exchange rail 0 with the outcome rail $a$ ($S_0=I$), on the full Fock space. With $W_a=S_a(\text{pointer})\otimes S_a(\text{purifier})$, use

$$
H_{\mathrm{record}}(t)=H_0+\hbar g(t)\sum_a P_a\otimes(I-W_a).
$$

The $P_{a}$ are orthogonal, the $W_{a}$ Hermitian unitaries, so the interaction is positive and has code norm $\le 2\hbar g$. Its $T$ endpoint is $\sum_a P_a\otimes W_a$ and copies the coarse label coherently to both owned records. The actual finite coarse-pointer occupation service has positive duration $t_{m}$; it retains the complete Lüders outcomes, including the complement-failure subspace. Equivalently the ready-apparatus CP leaves are $P_a\rho P_a$ with both record factors at $a$. Off-ready apparatus is one additional failure subevent, with its full state retained; it is not forced into a fictitious valid one-excitation record. Combining all source-failure subevents into one finite named outcome is allowed without discarding their conditional states. The same global occupation readiness/abort convention covers these auxiliary factors. The finite coarse readout includes the joint complement of its pointer/purifier one-excitation sectors as a failure subevent. Nominal source preparation supplies fresh rail 0 states. For arbitrary valid-occupation but nonblank apparatus inputs, the displayed full-space controlled permutations still define the complete response; the ready-apparatus Lüders identity is not falsely asserted for those states. Older retained records are never required to be blank, and data is never required to be Golay-valid. This is the original admitted complete coarse-occupation service with an explicit finite dilation, not a claim that infinitely many photon counts fit a finite pointer.

The seven main services are six binary-factor reads (five flags and $d$) with three outcomes each, and one reward read with four outcomes. Their pointers+purifiers use $2(6\cdot 3+4)=44$ rails and 14 ready excitations. Each record-copy pulse adds $T$, action $\le\pi\hbar$ and absolute actuator-work bound $\le 3\pi\hbar/T$; each primitive read adds $t_{m}$ and its fixed supplied detector/control holding charge. The primitive readout and coarse-predicate control are the explicit original source premises, rather than uncounted further ideal computers.

Choose an integer $K\ge 1$ large enough for the declared common finite package and set $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}=K\hbar\Omega$. The actual payment account is one two-rail donor/receipt register of frequency $K\Omega$, initially one excitation at donor. A fresh dual-rail funded guard at frequency $\Omega$ starts 0. The funded projector $Q$ selects exactly one donor excitation and zero receipt excitations. The positive coarse-predicate pulse $H_0+\hbar g(t)Q(I-S_{\mathrm{guard}})$ latches it in one $T$ slot; read it by its own three-outcome detector (one additional 3-rail pointer and 3-rail purifier, record-copy $T$ plus $t_m$). Successful delivery proceeds only on the funded result. The running guard is retained and never recomputed from the depleted wallet.

The transfer pulse is $H_0+\hbar g(t)P_{\mathrm{guard}1}(a_{\mathrm{receipt}}-a_{\mathrm{donor}})^\dagger(a_{\mathrm{receipt}}-a_{\mathrm{donor}})$, duration $T$. It is positive on full Fock space, preserves wallet total occupation and, on the funded sector, swaps donor and receipt exactly. Frequency $K\Omega$ gives a full-period free phase because $K$ is an integer. Thus actual root spendable energy decreases by $K\hbar\Omega$, provider receipt increases by the same amount, and no duplicate energy is created. The guard controls every subsequent scheduled service; failed funding supplies no promised data operations, but retains the normalized failure branch to the same final horizon. There is no reverse/refund interface. Payment adds ten rails (two wallet,two guard,six readout apparatus), one wallet excitation of energy $K\hbar\Omega$ and three excitations of energy $\hbar\Omega$.

Consequently the entire displayed auxiliary inventory adds 64 rails to the base rail count, and total ready excitation energy is $(K+361)\hbar\Omega$:339 base+5 readiness flags+14 main detector records+3 payment guard/records, plus the $K$-energy wallet. Detector controllers, timing apparatus and immutable compiler records are the explicitly supplied finite classical/pulse service hardware, whose finite capacities and positive hosting tariffs are part of $E_{\mathrm{pkg}}$; the underlying primitive service law is the same as the accepted original source. Every occupied auxiliary factor has its actual lifetime included in the holding sum. The payment wallet energy is the debit, not an additional second fee; the reward excitation is already among the base 339. No unknown occupancy is silently assigned zero cost on failure branches: those have their actual source state and retained failure outcome, while the fixed comparison task uses the supplied ready ensemble.

**6. Original selector and scope of completion**

Now import original R.4.4c, on its FULL class and original p interval. Every C not coordinate-permutation equivalent to marked extended Golay satisfies

$$
V_C-V_G > [B/(5T_{\mathrm{run}})]p^7(1-p)^{17}>0.
$$

For $p=1/1000$ the explicit rational integrated gap is $>B\,999^{17}/(5\cdot 1000^{24})$. At $p=0$ all losses tie, as the original result states. The selected code is the original marked $G_{24}$, so its encoder, all 24 noisy coordinates, syndrome, decoder/charge observables and code-to-lattice marking are exactly those of Theorem R.4.2.8d and Proposition R.4.2.8e. The physical catalogue of this theorem acts on that same data substrate and extends its noise law over the full BSC alphabet.

The source claim is the original finite oscillator/register physical presentation, with its admitted positive controls, exact calibrated pulses, complete occupation services and funded boundary. It makes no new $C_P$-minimality assertion and does not copy the ALOG cell descriptor. Original R04 requires the full channel/task/source map rather than a new cell-minimum: the substrate subclause supplied by Theorem R.4.2.8d and Proposition R.4.2.8e is consumed at its actual scope, while the full BSC, all-code detector and paid proper-score map of this theorem discharge the remaining conjunction. Perturbations would require the original error budget plus a margin small enough to preserve the strict gap; this exact source theorem makes no uniform tolerance claim beyond its stated model.

∎

#### R.4.2.1.3 Leech Lattice Construction via Golay Gluing

<a id="lemma-r45-conditional-leech-identification-from-a-registered-discriminant-form-gluing-datum"></a>**Lemma R.4.5 (Conditional Leech Identification from a Registered Discriminant-Form Gluing Datum).** Let
$$
L_0=(\sqrt2E_8)^3,
\qquad
A_{L_0}=L_0^*/L_0,
$$
with discriminant quadratic form $q_{L_0}$. A registered Golay gluing datum consists of:

1. an explicit isometry of finite quadratic modules
$$
\phi:(A_{L_0},q_{L_0})\longrightarrow(B,q_B),
$$
not merely a vector-space identification with $\mathbb F_2^{24}$;
2. a specified embedded copy $C\subset B$ of the extended binary Golay code such that $H:=\phi^{-1}(C)$ is maximal totally isotropic;
3. a certified nonzero-vector minimum ledger
$$
m_0^{\times}:=\min\{|x|^2:x\in L_0\setminus\{0\}\}=4,
$$
and, for every nonzero $h\in A_{L_0}$,
$$
m(h):=\min\{|x|^2:x\in L_0^*,\ [x]=h\},
$$
proving
$$
m_H^{\times}
:=
\min\left(\{m_0^{\times}\}\cup\{m(h):0\ne h\in H\}\right)
=4.
$$

Then
$$
L_H:=\bigcup_{h\in H}(g_h+L_0)
$$
is even, unimodular, and rootless of rank $24$, and hence is isometric to the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$.

*Proof.* Apply the even-quadratic-lattice overlattice correspondence (Chenevier and Lannes 2015, Proposition II.1.1) to the specified quadratic-module isometry and isotropic subgroup $H$. Its inverse image in $L_0^*$ is an even integral overlattice of index $|H|=2^{12}$, so its determinant is $\det L_0/|H|^2=2^{24}/2^{24}=1$. Every nonzero vector of $L_H$ lies either in $L_0\setminus\{0\}$ or in a nonzero glue coset; the assumed ledger $m_H^{\times}=4$ therefore gives minimum nonzero squared norm $4$ and rootlessness. The rank-$24$ positive-definite even unimodular rootless-lattice characterization (Chenevier and Lannes 2015, Theorem II.3.16; Conway 1969a) identifies its Euclidean-isometry class with $\Lambda_{24}$. An abstract isomorphism $A_{L_0}\cong\mathbb F_2^{24}$ without $\phi$, $q_B$, and the coset-minimum ledger is insufficient for this conclusion. ∎

**Explicit coordinate closure of the marked Golay branch.** Let $C=\mathcal G_{24}\subset\mathbb F_2^{24}$ be the doubly even self-dual extended binary $[24,12,8]$ Golay code and define
$$
K_C
:=
\left\{
x\in\mathbb Z^{24}:
x\bmod2\in C,\ 
\sum_{i=1}^{24}x_i\equiv0\pmod4
\right\},
\qquad
L_B(C):=\frac1{\sqrt2}K_C.
$$
Let
$$
v=(-3,1,\ldots,1),
\qquad
\nu=\frac v{\sqrt8},
\qquad
\Lambda_C:=L_B(C)\cup\bigl(\nu+L_B(C)\bigr).
$$
Then $\Lambda_C$ is an even unimodular positive-definite rank-$24$ lattice of minimum squared norm $4$, and therefore $\Lambda_C\cong\Lambda_{24}$.

*Coordinate proof.* Since $\dim C=12$, the parity condition $x\bmod2\in C$ has index $2^{12}$ in $\mathbb Z^{24}$. On that preimage, $x\mapsto\frac12\sum_i x_i\bmod2$ is a surjective homomorphism because $C$ is even and $2e_1$ maps to $1$. Hence
$$
[\mathbb Z^{24}:K_C]=2^{13},
\qquad
\operatorname{covol}L_B(C)=2^{13}2^{-12}=2.
$$
The code $C$ is doubly even, so for $x\in K_C$,
$$
x\cdot x\equiv\operatorname{wt}(x\bmod2)\equiv0\pmod4.
$$
Thus every vector $x/\sqrt2\in L_B(C)$ has even squared norm. If $x\bmod2=0$, then $x=2z$ with $\sum_i z_i$ even, and every nonzero such vector has $x\cdot x\ge8$. If $x\bmod2\ne0$, then the minimum distance $d(C)=8$ gives $x\cdot x\ge8$. The vector $2(e_1-e_2)/\sqrt2\in L_B(C)$ has squared norm $4$, so equality is attained. Therefore
$$
\min\{|y|^2:0\ne y\in L_B(C)\}=4.
$$

Because $C$ is even and self-dual, the all-one word belongs to $C$: it is orthogonal to every even-weight codeword and hence lies in $C^\perp=C$. Therefore $v\bmod2=\mathbf1\in C$ and $\sum_i v_i=20\equiv0\pmod4$, so $2\nu=v/\sqrt2\in L_B(C)$. For $x/\sqrt2\in L_B(C)$,
$$
\left\langle\nu,\frac{x}{\sqrt2}\right\rangle
=\frac{v\cdot x}{4}
=\frac{\sum_i x_i-4x_1}{4}
\in\mathbb Z.
$$
Hence $\nu\in L_B(C)^*$, while $\nu\notin L_B(C)$, and $\nu+L_B(C)$ is an order-two glue coset. For every $\ell\in L_B(C)$,
$$
|\nu+\ell|^2
=|\nu|^2+|\ell|^2+2\langle\nu,\ell\rangle
\in2\mathbb Z,
$$
so adjoining the coset preserves evenness. The index-two extension has covolume $2/2=1$, so $\Lambda_C$ is unimodular.

Finally, every vector in $\nu+L_B(C)$ has the form $(v+2x)/\sqrt8$ with all $24$ numerator coordinates odd. Its squared norm is at least $24/8=3$; because $\Lambda_C$ is even, that norm is an even integer and is therefore at least $4$. The vector $\nu$ attains $4$. Thus $\Lambda_C$ has minimum squared norm $4$, so it is rootless. The Niemeier classification identifies the resulting lattice with the Leech lattice. Coordinate permutations carry equivalent copies of $C$ to isometric lattices. ∎

<a id="proposition-r45a-coordinatewise-trio-realization-of-the-sqrt2e_83-gluing-datum"></a>**Proposition R.4.5a (Coordinatewise Trio Realization of the $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ Gluing Datum).** Let $C=\mathcal G_{24}$, $K_C$, $L_B(C)$, $\nu$ and $\Lambda_C$ be as in the explicit coordinate construction above. For $S\subset\{1,\ldots,24\}$ write $\mathbb R^S$ and $\mathbb F_2^S$ for the coordinate subspaces and $\mathbf 1_S$ for the indicator word, and for an octad $O$ put
$$
E_8(O)=\Bigl\{y\in\mathbb Z^O\cup(\mathbb Z+\tfrac12)^O:\ \sum_{i\in O}y_i\in2\mathbb Z\Bigr\}.
$$

1. For every octad $O_1$, exactly $30$ octads are disjoint from $O_1$, and they form $15$ complementary pairs in $\{1,\ldots,24\}\setminus O_1$. Hence $C$ has trios $T=\{O_1,O_2,O_3\}$ of pairwise disjoint octads covering all $24$ coordinates.
2. For every octad $O$, $\Lambda_C\cap\mathbb R^O=\tfrac1{\sqrt2}\,2E_8(O)\cong\sqrt2E_8$. For a trio $T$ the coordinatewise base lattice
$$
L_0^T:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^3\bigl(\Lambda_C\cap\mathbb R^{O_i}\bigr)\cong(\sqrt2E_8)^3
\tag{R.4.5a.1}
$$
has $m_0^\times=4$.
3. The map
$$
\phi_T:\ (L_0^T)^*/L_0^T\longrightarrow B_T:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^3A_i,
\qquad A_i:=E_8(O_i)/2E_8(O_i),
\qquad \Bigl(\frac{y_i}{\sqrt2}\Bigr)_{i}\longmapsto\bigl(y_i\bmod2E_8(O_i)\bigr)_{i},
$$
is an isometry of finite quadratic modules onto $(B_T,q_B)$ with $q_B(y)=\sum_i|y_i|^2/2\bmod2$. Each $A_i$ consists of the zero class, $120$ nonsingular classes, each containing exactly one pair of roots $\pm r$, and $135$ nonzero singular classes, each containing exactly $16$ vectors of squared norm $4$ that form an orthogonal frame. Their minimal squared norms in $(L_0^T)^*$ are $0$, $1$ and $2$; call these component types $0$, $\mathrm N$ and $\mathrm S$.
4. $H_T:=\phi_T(\Lambda_C/L_0^T)$ is a maximal totally singular subspace of $B_T$ of order $2^{12}$, and the overlattice of Lemma R.4.5 attached to $H_T$ is $\Lambda_C$. Every nonzero $h\in H_T$ has component types, up to order, $(\mathrm S,\mathrm S,0)$, $(\mathrm S,\mathrm N,\mathrm N)$ or $(\mathrm S,\mathrm S,\mathrm S)$; these occur $45$, $2880$ and $1170$ times, and
$$
m(h)=4\ \ \text{for the first two types},\qquad m(h)=6\ \ \text{for the third}.
\tag{R.4.5a.2}
$$
Thus $m_H^\times=4$, and items 1 and 3 of Lemma R.4.5 hold for $(L_0^T,\phi_T)$. For every $\mathbb F_2$-linear isomorphism $\psi:B_T\to\mathbb F_2^{24}$ with $\psi(H_T)=C$, the isometry $\psi\circ\phi_T$ onto $(\mathbb F_2^{24},q_B\circ\psi^{-1})$ satisfies items 1–3 of Lemma R.4.5 with $(\psi\circ\phi_T)^{-1}(C)=\Lambda_C/L_0^T$; by item 5, no such $\psi$ maps every $A_i$ onto $\mathbb F_2^{O_i}$. The minimal vectors number $256$ in each $(\mathrm S,\mathrm S,0)$ coset and $64$ in each $(\mathrm S,\mathrm N,\mathrm N)$ coset, so $3\cdot240+45\cdot256+2880\cdot64=196560$.
5. Let $H_B:=\phi_T(L_B(C)/L_0^T)$, and let $H_\Sigma\subset H_B$ be the image of the vectors $x/\sqrt2$ with $x\in K_C$ and $x\bmod2\in\langle\mathbf 1_{O_1},\mathbf 1_{O_2},\mathbf 1_{O_3}\rangle$. Then $H_T=H_B\sqcup\bigl(\phi_T(\nu)+H_B\bigr)$, $H_\Sigma\cong\mathbb F_2^2$, and reduction modulo $2$ induces an isomorphism
$$
H_B/H_\Sigma\ \xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\ C/\langle\mathbf 1_{O_1},\mathbf 1_{O_2},\mathbf 1_{O_3}\rangle\cong\mathbb F_2^9.
\tag{R.4.5a.3}
$$
No $\mathbb F_2$-linear isomorphism $\psi:B_T\to\mathbb F_2^{24}$ with $\psi(A_i)=\mathbb F_2^{O_i}$ for $i=1,2,3$ carries $H_T$ onto $C$.

*Proof.* (1) A nonzero codeword supported in an octad $O$ has weight at least $8$ and therefore equals $\mathbf 1_O$, so $C\cap\mathbb F_2^O=\langle\mathbf 1_O\rangle$. For any $S$, the orthogonal complement in $\mathbb F_2^S$ of the coordinate projection $\pi_S(C)$ is $C^\perp\cap\mathbb F_2^S=C\cap\mathbb F_2^S$. Hence $\dim\pi_{O_1}(C)=7$, and $C\cap\mathbb F_2^{O_1^c}=\ker\pi_{O_1}$ has dimension $5$. Its $32$ words are doubly even of weight at most $16$; the weight-$16$ word is $\mathbf 1+\mathbf 1_{O_1}$, and a weight-$12$ word $c$ would give the weight-$4$ codeword $c+\mathbf 1+\mathbf 1_{O_1}$. The other $30$ words are therefore octads, and $O\mapsto O_1^c\setminus O$ pairs them.

(2) Every vector of $\nu+L_B(C)$ has all $24$ numerator coordinates odd and so lies in no $\mathbb R^O$. A vector $x/\sqrt2\in L_B(C)$ supported in $O$ has $x\bmod2\in C\cap\mathbb F_2^O=\{0,\mathbf 1_O\}$ and $\sum_ix_i\equiv0\pmod4$; conversely every such $x$ lies in $K_C$. Writing $x=2y$, these conditions say $y\in\mathbb Z^O\cup(\mathbb Z+\tfrac12)^O$ and $\sum_iy_i\in2\mathbb Z$, that is, $x\in2E_8(O)$. The minimum squared norm of $\sqrt2E_8$ is $4$.

(3) Since $E_8$ is unimodular, $(\sqrt2E_8(O_i))^*=\tfrac1{\sqrt2}E_8(O_i)$, so $\phi_T$ is a group isomorphism, and $|y_i/\sqrt2|^2=|y_i|^2/2$ gives the quadratic form. The lattice $E_8$ has $240$ roots and $2160$ vectors of squared norm $4$, the coefficients $240\sigma_3(1)$ and $240\sigma_3(2)$ of its $\theta$ series. Nonzero vectors of $2E_8$ have squared norm in $8\mathbb Z_{>0}$. Roots $r\ne\pm r'$ satisfy $|r-r'|^2=4-2r\cdot r'\le6$, so a root class contains exactly $\pm r$. Two norm-$4$ vectors in one class satisfy $|v-v'|^2=8-2v\cdot v'\in\{0,8,16\}$, so they are equal, orthogonal or opposite, and a class contains at most $16$ of them. Roots and norm-$4$ vectors lie in classes with $q_B=1$ and $q_B=0$ respectively. These counts give at least $1+240/2+2160/16=256=|A_i|$ classes, so every count is exact.

(4) The covolume of $\sqrt2E_8$ is $2^4$ and $\Lambda_C$ is unimodular, so $|\Lambda_C/L_0^T|=2^{12}$. Evenness of $\Lambda_C$ makes $q_B$ vanish on $H_T$, and $|H_T|^2=2^{24}=|B_T|$ makes $H_T$ maximal with $H_T^\perp=H_T$; its inverse image in $(L_0^T)^*$ is $\Lambda_C$. For $h=(h_1,h_2,h_3)\ne0$ the coset $h+L_0^T$ is a product of blockwise cosets, so $m(h)$ is the sum of the three component minima. The coset lies in $\Lambda_C\setminus\{0\}$, so $m(h)\ge4$, and $q_B(h)=0$ makes the number of $\mathrm N$ components even. These two conditions leave exactly the three listed types. In particular $H_T\cap A_k=0$, so, since $A_k^\perp=A_i\oplus A_j$ for $\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}$, the subspace $H_T\cap(A_i\oplus A_j)=(H_T+A_k)^\perp$ has dimension $24-20=4$; these three subspaces meet only in $0$ and contain the $45$ elements of type $(\mathrm S,\mathrm S,0)$. The projection $H_T\to A_k$ is onto, because the orthogonal of its image in $A_k$ is $A_k\cap H_T^\perp=0$; its fibers have $16$ elements, so exactly $120\cdot16=1920$ elements have an $\mathrm N$ component in position $k$. Each $(\mathrm S,\mathrm N,\mathrm N)$ element has two $\mathrm N$ components, so there are $3\cdot1920/2=2880$ of them, and $4095-45-2880=1170$ elements have type $(\mathrm S,\mathrm S,\mathrm S)$. The minima and minimal-vector counts follow from item 3. Isomorphisms $\psi$ with $\psi(H_T)=C$ exist because both subspaces have dimension $12$, and $q_B\circ\psi^{-1}$ makes $\psi\circ\phi_T$ an isometry, so items 1–3 of Lemma R.4.5 transfer from $\phi_T$ to $\psi\circ\phi_T$.

(5) Item 2 gives $L_0^T\subset L_B(C)$, while $\nu\notin L_B(C)$ and $2\nu\in L_B(C)$; hence $H_B$ has index $2$ in $H_T$ with the displayed coset decomposition. Reduction $x\mapsto(x\bmod2)+\langle\mathbf 1_{O_1},\mathbf 1_{O_2},\mathbf 1_{O_3}\rangle$ maps $K_C$ onto $C/\langle\mathbf 1_{O_1},\mathbf 1_{O_2},\mathbf 1_{O_3}\rangle$, and its kernel $K_\Sigma$ contains the numerator lattice $\bigoplus_i2E_8(O_i)$ of $L_0^T$; this gives (R.4.5a.3), and the three disjoint octad words are independent, so the quotient has dimension $9$. A vector $x\in K_\Sigma$ has constant parity on each $O_i$, so its block sums $a_i$ are even with $a_1+a_2+a_3\equiv0\pmod4$. The map $x\mapsto(a_i/2\bmod2)_i$ sends $K_\Sigma$ onto the even-weight plane of $\mathbb F_2^3$, since $2e_j-2e_k$ with $j\in O_1$ and $k\in O_2$ maps to $(1,1,0)$ and the analogous vector with $k\in O_3$ maps to $(1,0,1)$, and its kernel is $\bigoplus_i2E_8(O_i)$. Hence $H_\Sigma\cong\mathbb F_2^2$. Finally $\psi(H_T)\cap\mathbb F_2^{O_1}=\psi(H_T\cap A_1)=0$, whereas $\mathbf 1_{O_1}\in C\cap\mathbb F_2^{O_1}$. ∎

### R.4.5b A Stable Quantum Rotor on the Marked Gluing

<a id="theorem-r45b-stable-vacuum-and-full-finite-response-on-the-marked-leech-quotient"></a>**Theorem R.4.5b (Stable Vacuum and Full Finite Response on the Marked Leech Quotient).** Retain the particular marked coordinate lattice, trio base, discriminant map and glue subgroup of Proposition R.4.5a. Adopt the ideal compact quantum rotor, independently calibrated positive generalized inertia and Planck action, supplied preparation, clock, bounded controls and finite record instruments specified below. Its isolated observation Hamiltonian has one strictly lowest-energy ray, excitation gap $8\pi^2\hbar^2/I_{\mathrm{rot}}$ and first-shell multiplicity 196560. Every bounded self-adjoint perturbation of norm $\epsilon$ below half that gap retains a simple ground and gap at least $\Delta-2\epsilon$. The actual marked covering intertwines the full finite source, instruments, unnormalized histories and arbitrary retained references, with every declared resource retained.

Here strict vacuum selection means strict Hamiltonian ground-energy minimization on the already fixed marked realization. The rotor configuration has24 coordinates; no physical24-dimensional spacetime is asserted. Preparation and ideal services are constitutive supplied resources. The theorem neither prepares a ground state from an arbitrary thermal input nor compares vacuum energies between different lattice geometries.

**Source and independently fixed units.**

Retain the accepted **specific** $\Lambda=\Lambda_C$, the trio base $L_0=L_0^T$, and every marked map from R.4.5a. Let one dimensionless configuration coordinate be $q$ in $T=\mathbb{R}^{24}/\Lambda$. This is a compact 24-dimensional configuration space, not claimed to be physical 24-dimensional spacetime. Choose an independently calibrated generalized inertia $I_{\mathrm{rot}}>0$ with units energy times time squared, Planck action $\hbar>0$ with units energy times time, and physical time $t$. Equivalently $I_{\mathrm{rot}}$ may be a separately calibrated mass times an independently fixed length squared; that length is not supplied by the normalized lattice minimum. None is tuned to a desired gap or ground state. The kinetic action is

$$
S[q]=\int (I_{\mathrm{rot}}/2)|dq/dt|^2\, dt.
$$

Canonical momenta and periodic wavefunctions give the Hamiltonian $H_0=P^2/(2I_{\mathrm{rot}})=-\hbar^2\Delta/(2I_{\mathrm{rot}})$. The domain is $H^2(T)$, determined by the marked periodicity, and the norm is the accepted Euclidean norm. No desired vacuum projector enters this definition. The kinetic energy is positive quadratic work for velocity, an explicitly registered source law, not an inference from error-correcting code costs.

The unique observation-system ground is the constant function. The exact gap is $8\pi^2\hbar^2/I_{\mathrm{rot}}$ and its first-shell degeneracy is 196560. The Fourier and bounded-perturbation proof follows below, with $\kappa=\hbar^2/(2I_{\mathrm{rot}})$. The family works for every positive independently selected $I_{\mathrm{rot}}$ and $\hbar$, so selection is not achieved by fitting a target gap.

**Fourier spectrum and stability proof.**

Let $\Lambda$ denote precisely $\Lambda_{C}$ with its accepted Euclidean marking. Introduce the **new** quantum configuration space $T=\mathbb{R}^{24}/\Lambda$, Haar probability measure $dx$, Hilbert space $L^2(T,dx)$, and kinetic form

$$
q(f)=\kappa \int_T |\nabla f|^2\, dx,\quad \kappa>0,
$$

with form domain $H^1(T)$. The associated positive self-adjoint operator $H=-\kappa\Delta$ has domain $H^2(T)$. These are source additions, not conclusions of lattice gluing.

Characters $e_p(x)=\exp(2\pi i\,p\cdot x)$, $p\in\Lambda^*$, form an orthonormal basis. Since $\Lambda$ is unimodular and integral, $\Lambda^*=\Lambda$. Thus

$$
H e_p = 4 \pi^2 \kappa |p|^2 e_p.
$$

The kernel is exactly the constants: $p=0$ is the unique zero momentum and all nonzero $p$ have $|p|^2\ge 4$. The normalized ground is 1, and the exact gap is

$$
\Delta=16 \pi^2 \kappa.
$$

The first excited multiplicity is 196560 from the accepted minimum-vector count. This generator is a differential local operator on the 24-dimensional torus. It is not thereby local on the physical four-dimensional shell or a spatial many-site carrier.

For bounded self-adjoint $V$, with $\lVert V\rVert=\epsilon<\Delta/2$, min-max gives

$$
E_0(H+V) \le \epsilon,\qquad E_1(H+V) \ge \Delta-\epsilon,
$$

so its ground is simple and the gap is at least $\Delta-2\epsilon>0$. Compact resolvent is preserved. For normalized perturbed ground $\psi$, projecting its eigenvalue equation onto the complement $Q$ of constants yields

$$
\lVert Q\psi\rVert\le\epsilon/(\Delta-\epsilon).
$$

Here $H$ restricted to $Q$ has spectrum $\ge\Delta$ and $|E_0|\le\epsilon$. This proves controlled movement of the ground. It does not prove that the original constant vector remains exactly the ground under general $V$. A Hamiltonian scalar shift changes no spectral statement but any physical reserve implementing the shift must remain in the resource/source ledger.

**Exact marked carrier map.**

Let $T_0=\mathbb{R}^{24}/L_0$. The accepted lattice inclusion gives a degree-4096 covering

$$
\pi:T_0\to T,
$$

with deck translations $\Gamma=\Lambda/L_0$, identified with $H_{T}$ by the actual $\phi_{T}$. Equip both tori with normalized Haar measures. Define

$$
W:L^2(T)\to L^2(T_0),\quad Wf=f\circ\pi.
$$

Then W is an isometry because $\pi$ pushes normalized Haar to normalized Haar. Its range is exactly the Gamma-invariant subspace: a deck-invariant function is constant on each fiber and descends uniquely, up to null sets. Local isometry of the covering gives

$$
W H_0 = H_{0,\mathrm{base}} W
$$

on the Sobolev domains. The base Hamiltonian is the same local kinetic expression on $T_0$. A source observable $a(q)$ lifts to multiplication by $a\circ\pi$, and $Wa=a_{\mathrm{lift}}W$. Derivatives and actual marked periodic coefficients likewise intertwine on their domains. Hence every finite sequence of bounded source probes, their generated evolutions, and final bounded observations has identical matrix elements under $W$; with an untouched arbitrary reference, use $W\otimes I$. Equality of full finite response experiments follows, not merely equality of dimensions.

The invariant-sector restriction is a **geometric quotient already fixed by the gluing of Proposition R.4.5a**, not a penalty selecting the desired vacuum. The full base-torus source without the quotient has additional deck sectors and a different spectrum. One must not claim that physical gluing was dynamically generated by this intertwiner. The source lives on the glued geometry of Proposition R.4.5a.

This join preserves the actual $H_{T}$ and its Golay filtration. It does not use a false block-local identification $H_T=C$. No new $E_8$/Golay relabeling is performed.

**Actual24-channel response.**

Choose a marked lattice basis $p_1,\dots,p_{24}$ of $\Lambda^*=\Lambda$. They are distinct up to sign. Let $F_j(q)=\cos(2\pi\,p_j\cdot q)$, bounded local multiplication operators on configuration space. Their actual scalar-potential source is

$$
H(\theta)=H_0+\sum_j \theta_j F_j.
$$

Its bounded perturbation has norm at most $B=\sum_j|\theta_j|$. For $B<\text{gap}/2$, the unique ground and positive gap persist. The entire $\theta$-family and every probe lift through $W$, so no metric-QFI identity is assumed.

Starting from the unperturbed vacuum, switch on this potential for a fixed physical time $t$ and read any actual bounded observable. Define $\psi_\theta(t)=\exp(-itH(\theta)/\hbar)\,1$. This is a fully specified finite source-response family. At $\theta=0$, with $E_j=2\pi^2\hbar^2|p_j|^2/I_{\mathrm{rot}}$, its derivative is exactly

$$
\partial_j \psi = ((\exp(-i E_j t/\hbar)-1)/E_j) F_j.
$$

The zero averages and orthogonality $\int F_iF_j=\delta_{ij}/2$ give the pure-state QFI

$$
J_{ij}(t)=\delta_{ij}\, 8 \sin^2(E_j t/(2\hbar))/E_j^2.
$$

Choose any positive t avoiding the discrete resonance sets $E_{j}$ t/(2hbar) in $\pi$ Z. Then the QFI has rank 24. This is force/potential-probe response, explicitly distinct from the zero ground-state QFI for mere metric variations.

No instantaneous kick, nonphysical imaginary-time preparation, arbitrary projector, or gauge-only phase is used. $H(\theta)$ is a local second-order differential Hamiltonian plus bounded scalar potentials on the accepted marked torus. The pulse is a supplied-control change of a named scalar-potential source.

**Observation-source work.**

The ideal observation-source ledger is as follows; the complete finite readout extension below retains its separate clock/control premises:

- Rotor state space: one compact marked 24-coordinate degree of freedom, with unbounded momentum spectrum. This infinite source capacity is retained, not called finite memory.
- Vacuum preparation: one supplied pure ground resource. Its preparation is not derived by unitary evolution, not obtained freely from a finite-temperature state, and not hidden in the proof of unique ground. A relaxation claim would need a separate physical bath model.
- Physical time/generalized inertia/action: $I_{\mathrm{rot}},\hbar,t$ are independently calibrated parameters; a supplied clock and control waveform are retained external resources. An autonomous clock or driver is not claimed.
- Probe support: precisely the finite menu of 24 periodic scalar potentials $F_{j}$ and their physical amplitudes $\theta_{j}$. Configuration-space locality is proved; physical four-dimensional routing locality is not claimed.
- Observation Hamiltonian during the pulse: $H(\theta)$. Both switch-on and switch-off work are counted with the actual prepared state.
- Switch-on work from the constant vacuum is exactly expectation of $\sum\theta_jF_j$, which is zero. During the pulse the expectation of $H(\theta)$ remains its initial value zero. At switch-off the work is $-\mathbb{E}(\sum\theta_jF_j)$, exactly equal to the final nonnegative kinetic energy. It is between zero and $B$ because the potential norm is at most $B$. Thus a supplied available work reserve $B$ suffices for this cycle at the observation-system level. This is expected work, not a deterministic autonomous battery-shift theorem.
- Finite smooth switching instead has exact work integral $\int\sum_j\dot{\theta}_j\,\mathbb{E}(F_j)\,dt$ and absolute-work bound $\int\sum_j|\dot{\theta}_j|\,dt$. All lifted work expressions agree under $W$.
- If positive Hamiltonians are required at every setting, add the actual loaded reserve $RI$ with $R\ge B$ to the source, retained throughout. It changes neither the dynamics nor the gap but is not deleted from energy/source accounting.

**Finite instruments and retained records.**

For each of $N$ declared readout shots allocate a two-level meter and a two-level record, each with actual degenerate positive storage Hamiltonian $\epsilon_{A}I$ and $\epsilon_{R}I$, respectively. Both start in the supplied pure blank $|0\rangle$. Their positive storage energies and supplied blank purity are retained. No reset or erasure is performed. Allocating $N$ shots uses $2N$ two-level systems and $2N$ blank-purity resources, not an unlimited reusable free memory.

A shot for $F_{j}$ uses the actual total Hamiltonian

$$
H_{\mathrm{meter}}(t)=H_0 \otimes I + g(t) F_j \otimes Y_A + E_{\mathrm{storage}} I,
$$

for finite duration $s$, with a finite piecewise-constant bounded real $g(t)$. This is a bounded local scalar-potential interaction in rotor configuration space coupled to one finite meter. The exact propagator is used; no commutation of $H_0$ and $F_{j}$ is assumed. The meter is then read in the $X$ basis by a supplied single-meter Hadamard rotation and coherent copying of the resulting $Z$ bit to its record using a supplied two-qubit controlled-NOT pulse. The stored-record projectors define the classical outcomes. Equivalently the exact rotor instrument has Kraus operators

$$
K_b = \langle b_X| U_{\mathrm{meter}} |0_A\rangle,\qquad b \in \{+,-\},
$$

and $\sum K_b^*K_b=I$. One can retain both meter and record coherently instead of tracing them out; all outcome probabilities and conditional reference states are then obtained from the commuting record projections. A final record measurement is the declared standard quantum instrument postulate, not an implicit deletion of the record.

The H/CNOT gates act only on finite meters/records and have bounded explicit Hermitian logarithms. A unitary gate $U$ of duration $\tau$ may be implemented as $\exp(-i\tau K/\hbar)=U$ with $\lVert K\rVert\le\pi\hbar/\tau$ by a branch of its finite spectral logarithm. The gate generators, durations, and positive storage are additional named supplied controls, not inferred autonomous hardware. Rotor free evolution during these ancilla-only gates commutes with them and is retained exactly.

**Actual signal.**

The bounded observable $F_{j}$ has nonzero linear response to its own preparation potential. At the end of a probe pulse of duration t,

$$
\partial_{\theta_j} \langle F_j\rangle \text{ at } \theta=0 = (\cos(E_j t/\hbar)-1)/E_j.
$$

Other chosen basis-harmonic cross derivatives vanish. For nonresonant $t$ this is nonzero. A meter $Y$ coupling followed by $X$ readout measures a finite-duration weak response of $F_{j}$. At zero meter strength its first derivative is $(2/\hbar)\int_0^s\langle F_j(u)\rangle\,du$, where $F_j(u)$ is evolved under the actual free rotor after the probe. Continuity gives a nonzero derivative with respect to $\theta_{j}$ for every sufficiently small positive $s$. Analytic dependence on bounded meter strength then supplies finite sufficiently small nonzero $g$ with nonzero sensitivity. Thus the declared finite instrument actually detects the potential-probe response; it is not a claim that an abstract QFI bound is automatically attained. Independent copies/experiments for the 24 nominated harmonics give a locally nondegenerate response record. Exact probabilities are those of the displayed finite propagators and Kraus operators, with no approximation in their definition.

**Full finite experiment and intertwining.**

A finite experiment is a finite list of rational programmed control amplitudes/durations, harmonic labels $j$, finite ancilla gates, and record projections. The unknown signal coefficients $\theta$ range over an open bounded real neighborhood and are physical source parameters, not finite ROM words. Its controls are stored in a finite ROM, or supplied as a finite external program. If a stored program contains $L$ bits, allocate $L$ additional degenerate positive-energy storage bits at $\epsilon_{\mathrm{ROM}}$ each, with their supplied initialized purity charged. Let $E_{\mathrm{aux}}=N(\epsilon_A+\epsilon_R)+L\epsilon_{\mathrm{ROM}}$ plus any nominated clock/reserve storage energy. Every term is retained in the source and in its lifted version.

The same-carrier comparison extends W to W tensor $I_{\mathrm{ancillas}}$ tensor $I_{\mathrm{records}}$ tensor $I_{\mathrm{reference}}$. The interaction $F_{j}$ tensor Y, ancilla gates, storage terms, and every record projector intertwine exactly. Therefore the entire Stinespring process, all histories and their probabilities, postselected unnormalized states, full finite-time responses, and untouched references agree. No record is discarded in the comparison, and no physical deck-sector projection is secretly executed: both sources are defined on the geometric quotient sector from the outset.

**Finite work/time/capacity bounds for each declared finite protocol.**

Let $V(t)$ be the sum of the active bounded potential and meter/control terms, and write $B(t)$ for the sum of their operator-norm bounds. Include actual positive reserve $RI$ with $R\ge\sup_t B(t)$ if a positive total Hamiltonian is required. The actual supplied-control switching work at each change is expectation of $\Delta V$ in the current full state. Its absolute sum is bounded by

$$
\lvert W_{\mathrm{actual}}\rvert\le W_{\mathrm{budget}}:=\sum_{\mathrm{switches}}\lVert\Delta V\rVert,
$$

with a supplied available-work reserve chosen at least $W_{\mathrm{budget}}$; for absolutely continuous switching use $W_{\mathrm{budget}}:=\int\lVert\dot{V}(t)\rVert\,dt$. For a finite list of pulses returning to zero, a conservative bound is twice the sum of individual pulse norms. Thus each specified finite protocol has a finite bounded-expected-work requirement, finite declared elapsed time, finite number of ancillas/records/ROM bits, and a retained positive storage bill. Supplied core ground purity and apparatus blank purity are separate resources. This bound does not claim a deterministic battery shift, autonomous waveform generator, or finite energy variance of an ideal clock.

Because the initial rotor has finite kinetic energy zero and all controls are bounded, this work identity also bounds the final mean rotor energy: full expected energy can grow by at most the available switching work, with all storage/reserve terms included on both sides. At no stage is an unbounded hidden excited-state energy invoked to implement a finite instrument.

The pointer/record/storage core is allowed degenerate positive storage, so the total source including active apparatus is not claimed to have a unique ground. The **vacuum selector is the isolated observation rotor H0**; apparatus prepares/probes/reads it as an explicitly separate supplied service.

**Energy-constrained error estimates.**

For pulse-amplitude or bounded-control implementation error $\delta V(t)$, Duhamel gives operator-norm propagator error at most $\eta=(1/\hbar)\int\lVert\delta V(t)\rVert\,dt$. A finite sequence has error at most the sum of these $\eta$ values, so output trace distance and total-variation distance are bounded by that sum (capped at 1). This applies with arbitrary retained references and survives the deck-cover comparison. Rational approximation of finite amplitudes therefore has an explicit operational tolerance.

Exact durations use the nominated ideal supplied clock. For finite timing errors one must use an energy-constrained estimate, not a uniform norm bound on unbounded H0. If a segment's shifted positive Hamiltonian A has mean energy at most E on the actual entering state, then

$$
\lVert(\exp(-i\delta_tA/\hbar)-I)\psi\rVert\le\sqrt{2\lvert\delta_t\rvert E/\hbar}.
$$

The inequality follows from $|\exp(-ix)-1|^2\le 2|x|$ and spectral integration. Scalar energy offsets affect only global phases and may be removed for this estimate while remaining in physical energy accounting. Purifications give the corresponding trace-distance bound; accumulate these errors over the finite sequence. The source work bounds supply the needed finite $E$. A fully autonomous finite quantum clock with arbitrary-input uniform accuracy is not asserted.

**Complete source class and recomposition.**

The source universe is exactly the ideal compact rotor on the accepted marked quotient, the 24 named bounded scalar-potential channels, finite meter/record/ROM registers, supplied clock/control/reserve services, and finite histories composed from the stated operations. It is **not** all possible devices or every Leech-based theory. The source menu and kinetic action are physical postulates, independent of the subsequently solved ground state; no vacuum-projector control or extra hidden force term belongs to the menu. Constants $I_{\mathrm{rot}},\hbar$ are fixed independently and positive; time is physical clock time; programmed amplitudes/durations have finite rational descriptions. The unknown real signal $\theta$ lies in an open bounded neighborhood $B<\text{gap}/2$. Stored program bits describe the experimental settings, not the continuum-valued unknown signal.

The default prepared rotor is its supplied normalized zero-momentum pure state, whose energy-minimizing property is then proved independently from the full spectrum. Ancillas and records are supplied pure blanks. Each protocol may include an arbitrary untouched reference and already produced meter/record entanglement. For the stronger operator/response comparison, all joint states with rotor mean kinetic energy at most a declared $E_{\max}$ are admitted; the exact isometry and histories work for these states too. Preparation of arbitrary such inputs is not asserted to cost the same as the supplied vacuum. The supplied preparation entry names the input and its purity/energy entitlement and is never counted as free purification.

Complete rotor classification is the exact Fourier basis indexed by **all** $p\in\Lambda^*=\Lambda$. Tensor products with finite apparatus basis vectors classify the complete admitted carrier. This is an infinite exact Hilbert-space classification, not an enumeration cut off at the first shell. All finite histories are compositions of the declared generators and record instruments; there is no unlisted interaction. Mathematical evolution is defined by the closed kinetic quadratic form plus bounded perturbations, so no ultraviolet regulator or renormalization subtraction is introduced. No spectral/numerical truncation is needed for the theorem. If a numerical experiment uses a spectral energy cutoff $E_{\mathrm{cut}}$, the instantaneous tail probability of any admitted state is at most $E_{\max}/E_{\mathrm{cut}}$ (Markov's inequality for the positive kinetic energy); subsequent $E_{\max}$ is bounded by the actual finite work ledger. This does not claim a projected propagator approximation without a separate approximation estimate.

The complete pre-subtraction source ledger has distinct owners:

- kinetic core: $H_0$ and rotor configuration/purity resource;
- unknown physical probe: $\sum\theta_jF_j$, with its own actual energy and signal calibration;
- programmed meter interaction: $g(t)F_jY$, counted once as an interaction, never again as independent rotor or meter energy;
- finite ancilla controls: their actual bounded generators and switching work;
- storage: the exact constant positive bill $E_{\mathrm{aux}}$, including every inactive allocated blank and ROM bit;
- reserve: actual loaded $RI$ and the separate finite available-work entitlement, neither subtracted from physical energy;
- service: supplied physical clock and finite control program, and final standard record instrument. Their ideal-service status is an explicit source premise, not a derived autonomous circuit.

Recomposition is the explicitly displayed sum $H_{\mathrm{total}}=H_0+V_{\mathrm{probe}}+V_{\mathrm{meter}}+V_{\mathrm{ancilla}}+E_{\mathrm{aux}}I+RI$, piecewise in physical time. Each atom has exactly one owner. Expected energy and switching-work observables are linear in these owned atoms, so additivity is exact before any reference choice. Outcome probabilities are computed from the complete common propagator and instrument; they are not falsely decomposed into additive probabilities of the energy atoms. There are no counterterms, removed tails, erased records, or null sources claimed by cancellation. During inactive periods an interaction is exactly the zero operator by its declared waveform, while its allocated storage remains charged.

**Conditional histories and resource bounds.**

The finite work, mean-energy, and timing-error bounds apply to the unconditional retained experiment or coherent history. A rare postselected branch of probability $p>0$ can have conditional mean energy up to the unconditional bound divided by p. No branch-independent hard energy or work ceiling follows. The exact response join holds for every unnormalized branch state, including zero-probability branches, without such a conditioning assumption.

**Fixed-size and response scope.** For $T_L=\mathbb{R}^{24}/(L\Lambda)$, the exact gap becomes $16\pi^2\kappa/L^2$. It is positive at each fixed $L$, but tends to zero as $L$ tends to infinity. The unconfined Laplacian on $\mathbb{R}^{24}$ has no normalizable constant ground and no positive spectral gap. Thus this construction cannot be advertised as a size-uniform thermodynamic vacuum theorem. The constant ground under canonical normalized-Haar identification is unchanged by flat-metric variations, so its metric-variation QFI is zero; the nonzero rank 24 response above belongs to the explicit potential-probe experiment. The unique vacuum is that of the isolated observation rotor; positive degenerate apparatus storage is not claimed to have a unique ground. Supplied ideal clock and waveform services are declared rather than internalized as autonomous finite hardware.

#### R.4.2.1.4 Leech Lattice from PCE Constraints

<a id="theorem-r46-leech-lattice-under-the-24d-admissibility-criteria"></a>**Theorem R.4.6 (Leech Lattice under the 24D admissibility criteria).**

Assume the admissible 24-dimensional vacuum lattice is required to be:

1. positive-definite,
2. even,
3. unimodular, and
4. rootless, i.e. to have no vectors of squared norm $2$,

with rootlessness independently included in the admissibility ledger. QFI isotropy fixes an inner product but does not exclude norm-$2$ vectors. Under these stated criteria, the unique compatible Euclidean-isometry class is the class of the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$.

*Proof.*

**Step 1 (Classification input).** By the Niemeier classification (Niemeier 1973), every positive-definite even unimodular lattice of rank $24$ is isometric to one of exactly $24$ Niemeier lattices.

**Step 2 (Root-system characterization).** Each Niemeier lattice is characterized by its root system, namely the set of vectors of squared norm $2$. Twenty-three of the Niemeier lattices have nonempty root system. Exactly one has empty root system.

**Step 3 (Rootless branch).** The unique Niemeier isometry class with empty root system is represented by the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$ (Leech 1967; Conway 1969a).

**Step 4 (Application of the admissibility criteria).** Under the stated criteria, the admissible lattice must lie in the positive-definite rank-$24$ even unimodular class and must be rootless. By Steps 1--3, there is exactly one such Euclidean-isometry class, represented by $\Lambda_{24}$. A marked embedded representative is additional coordinate data.

Therefore the admissibility criteria select the Leech Euclidean-isometry class uniquely. ∎

---

#### <a id="r4215-compatibility-with-m-24-factorization"></a>R.4.2.1.5 Compatibility with M = 24 Factorization

<a id="proposition-r47-compatibility-of-e_8-triple-structure-with-m-24"></a>**Proposition R.4.7 (Compatibility of $E_8$ Triple Structure with M = 24).**

On a registered gluing datum satisfying Lemma R.4.5, the resulting overlattice $L_H\cong\Lambda_{24}$ contains its declared base lattice
$$
L_0=(\sqrt2E_8)^3
$$
with index $[L_H:L_0]=2^{12}$. Its three rank-$8$ summands are dimensionally compatible with $24=3d_0$ on the $d_0=8$ branch.

*Proof.* Containment is part of the overlattice construction $L_0\subset L_H$, and the registered maximal isotropic subgroup has order $2^{12}$, giving the stated index. Rank additivity gives $24=3\cdot8$. These facts establish compatibility only. They do not show that the three blocks are Golay octads, identify them with generations, or construct the required discriminant-form marking; each stronger interpretation needs its own registered map. ∎

#### R.4.2.1.6 Automorphism Group Structure

<a id="proposition-r48-automorphism-group-and-symmetry-structure"></a>**Proposition R.4.8 (Automorphism Group and Symmetry Structure).**

The automorphism group of the Leech lattice is:

$$
\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_{24}) = \mathrm{Co}_0
$$

where $\mathrm{Co}_0$ is the Conway group with order:

$$
|\mathrm{Co}_0| = 2^{22} \cdot 3^9 \cdot 5^4 \cdot 7^2 \cdot 11 \cdot 13 \cdot 23 = 8{,}315{,}553{,}613{,}086{,}720{,}000
$$

*Proof.*

**Step 1 (Automorphism group).** $\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_{24})$ consists of all orthogonal transformations $O \in O(24)$ such that $O(\Lambda_{24}) = \Lambda_{24}$.

**Step 2 (Conway's theorem).** By Conway (1969b), this group is denoted $\mathrm{Co}_0$ and has the stated order. The center contains $\{-I_{24}\}$.

**Step 3 (Sporadic simple group).** The quotient $\mathrm{Co}_1 = \mathrm{Co}_0/\{\pm I\}$ is a simple group, one of the 26 sporadic simple groups.

**Step 4 (Subgroup structure).** The stabilizer of various sublattice structures yields other sporadic groups:

- Stabilizer of a norm-4 vector: $\mathrm{Co}_2$
- Stabilizer of a norm-6 vector: $\mathrm{Co}_3$
- Stabilizer of a coordinate frame: $2^{12}:M_{24}$

The appearance of $M_{24} = \operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{24})$ reflects the Golay code's role in the construction. ∎

#### R.4.2.1.7 The Conditional Golay Bridge

<a id="theorem-r49-the-conditional-golay-bridge"></a>**Theorem R.4.9 (The Conditional Golay Bridge).**

Three statements are distinct:

1. the extended binary Golay code is, up to permutation equivalence, the unique binary linear $[24,12,8]$ code and attains the maximal distance in the binary linear $[24,12]$ class;
2. the explicit Construction-B coordinates following Lemma R.4.5 turn any marked copy $C=\mathcal G_{24}\subset\mathbb F_2^{24}$ directly into an even unimodular rootless rank-$24$ lattice $\Lambda_C\cong\Lambda_{24}$;
3. the separate $\sqrt2E_8^{\oplus3}$ scaffold yields the same isometry class only after a finite-quadratic-module marking satisfying items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5 and either the complete minimum ledger in item 3 or the base-coset identity, Equation (R.4.2a.1), and $d(\mathcal G_{24})=8$.

Thus the code-to-Leech bridge is closed constructively by the displayed coordinate construction. The abstract $E_8^{\oplus3}$ scaffold remains a distinct realization and still requires its stated marking and norm certificate; the code parameters alone do not determine $\phi$, $q_B$, or its glue representatives.

*Proof.* For a binary linear $[24,12,d]$ code, the Griesmer bound (Griesmer 1960) requires
$$
24\ge\sum_{i=0}^{11}\left\lceil\frac d{2^i}\right\rceil.
$$
If $d\ge9$, the right-hand side is at least
$$
9+5+3+2+\underbrace{1+\cdots+1}_{8\text{ terms}}=27,
$$
a contradiction. Hence $d\le8$. The extended binary Golay code has parameters $[24,12,8]$, so it attains the maximum. The classification theorem for binary linear $[24,12,8]$ codes (Pless 1968; MacWilliams and Sloane 1977) states that every such code is permutation-equivalent to the extended binary Golay code; its hypotheses are exactly binary linearity and the three displayed parameters. This proves statement 1.

Statement 2 is the explicit coordinate calculation following Lemma R.4.5: index and covolume give unimodularity, double evenness gives evenness, and the zero- and odd-coset estimates give minimum squared norm $4$. For statement 3, items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5 and the even-lattice gluing theorem make $L_H$ even and unimodular of rank $24$. Item 3 gives minimum squared norm $4$ directly; alternatively, $d(\mathcal G_{24})=8$ and (R.4.2a.1) control every nonzero glue coset while $m_0^{\times}=4$ controls the zero coset. Either abstract-scaffold route makes $L_H$ rootless, and Theorem R.4.10 gives $L_H\cong\Lambda_{24}$.

Statement 1 supplies the classified code up to coordinate permutation. Statement 2 then closes a concrete coordinate lattice functorially under those permutations. Statement 3 is a different carrier realization whose extra marking data are not supplied by statement 1. ∎

#### R.4.2.1.8 Dual Optimality Summary

The binary code and the twenty-four-dimensional lattice are each optimal in their own setting. Their registered construction supplies the connection between them.

##### Technical comparison ledger

<a id="remark-r42-conditional-code-lattice-cross-ledger"></a>**Remark R.4.2: Conditional Code--Lattice Cross-Ledger.**

| Domain | Closed statement | Additional bridge datum |
|--------|------------------|--------------------------|
| Binary coding | The extended Golay code is the unique $[24,12,8]$ binary linear code up to equivalence | None for the coding theorem |
| Rank-$24$ lattices | The Leech lattice is the unique positive-definite even unimodular rootless rank-$24$ lattice up to isometry | None after positive definiteness, evenness, unimodularity, rank, and rootlessness are assumed |
| Code-to-lattice transfer | The explicit coordinate construction following Lemma R.4.5 sends $\mathcal G_{24}$ to $\Lambda_C\cong\Lambda_{24}$; the separate $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ scaffold reaches the same isometry class only on its abstract gluing branch | No extra bridge datum for the displayed coordinate construction; for the abstract scaffold, items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5 plus either its complete minimum ledger or $m_0^{\times}=4$ together with Equation (R.4.2a.1) |

| Domain | Optimization Problem | Result | Role of $\mathcal{G}_{24}$ | Reference |
|--------|---------------------|--------|---------------------------|-----------|
| Information theory | Maximize $d$ for binary linear $[24,12,d]$ codes | $d_{\max}=8$ | Unique coordinate-permutation equivalence class of attainers; retained selection requires $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{dist}}$ | Theorems R.4.4 and Z.13b |
| Lattice geometry | Find rootless even unimodular $\Lambda \subset \mathbb{R}^{24}$ | $\Lambda_{24}$ | Gluing code for $\sqrt{2}E_8^3 \to \Lambda_{24}$ | Lemma R.4.5 |

**Why the two ledgers can be compared but do not coincide automatically:**

1. **Separate occurrences of $24$:** The code has block length $24$, the lattice has rank $24$, and the interface branch has mode count $M=24$. Equality of these integers does not identify the underlying objects.

2. **Different symmetry statements:** PCE coordinate-label neutrality requires the code selection rule to be equivariant under coordinate relabeling; it does not make the selected code invariant under all of $S_{24}$. The permutation automorphism group of the extended Golay code is $M_{24}$. Rootlessness of a lattice is a metric minimum-norm condition, not the analogous assertion of full permutation invariance.

3. **Different metrics:** Hamming weight and Euclidean squared norm are distinct functions. In the explicit coordinate construction their relation is proved by the displayed parity and minimum calculation; on an arbitrary finite-quadratic-module marking it requires the separate metric-compatibility theorem (R.4.2a.1).

4. **Certified intersection:** The explicit coordinate construction produces an even unimodular rootless lattice directly. On the separate $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ scaffold, items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5 construct the even unimodular overlattice and rootlessness follows from either its complete minimum ledger or the noncircular distance-transfer route of Corollary R.4.2b.

Thus the Golay bridge is the registered construction and its norm certificate, not a consequence of shared dimension, full $S_{24}$ invariance, or two unrelated extremality statements.

#### R.4.2.1.9 Connection to Spacetime Emergence

The integer $M=24$ enters the predictive-recovery, registered-gluing, and mode-channel branches as a shared input; it does not define a causal chain from the Golay code through the Leech lattice to spacetime. The licensed branchwise implications are the following.

The predictive-recovery rate branch plus an accepted $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{dist}}$ selects the Golay equivalence class. The explicit coordinate construction following Lemma R.4.5 then constructs $\Lambda_C\cong\Lambda_{24}$; the abstract $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ scaffold reaches the same class only with its registered marking and norm certificate. Independently, the faithful tangent-shell branch uses $M=24\le K(D)$, the $K(3)=12$ obstruction, the regular $24$-cell, and least-feasible support cost to select $D=4$. Neither lattice construction is a premise of that dimensional proof.

Consequently the licensed diagram is
$$
M=24\longrightarrow
\begin{cases}
\mathcal G_{24},&\text{predictive-recovery code branch},\\
\Lambda_{24},&\text{explicit Golay coordinate construction or certified abstract-gluing branch},\\
D=4,&\text{independent faithful-shell least-feasible branch}.
\end{cases}
$$
On an arbitrary finite-quadratic-module marking, code distance transfers to nonzero glue-coset minima only when Equation (R.4.2a.1) is proved. The explicit coordinate construction instead proves its Euclidean minimum directly. Identifying either mathematical construction with a physical vacuum, or proving its dynamical stability, requires a separate realization certificate.

### R.4.2.3 Code Distance and the Registered Gluing Datum

<a id="proposition-r42a-code-distance-does-not-replace-the-gluing-certificate"></a>**Proposition R.4.2a (Code Distance Does Not Replace the Gluing Certificate).** Binary Hamming distance is not invariant under an arbitrary vector-space identification
$$
A_{L_0}\cong\mathbb F_2^{24}
$$
and therefore does not by itself determine minimum norms of glue cosets. For the marked datum of items 1–2 in Lemma R.4.5, rootlessness has two independent metric obligations:
$$
m_0^{\times}=4,
\qquad
\min_{0\ne h\in\phi^{-1}(C)}m(h)\ge4.
$$
If a particular marked construction additionally proves
$$
\min_{0\ne h\in\phi^{-1}(C)}m(h)\ge4
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
d(C):=\min_{0\ne c\in C}\operatorname{wt}(c)\ge8,
\tag{R.4.2a.1}
$$
then code distance $8$ discharges the nonzero-glue-coset obligation. The independent base-lattice identity $m_0^{\times}=4$, the finite-quadratic-module marking, and maximal isotropy remain required.

*Proof.* The left side of (R.4.2a.1) depends on the discriminant quadratic form, its marking, and Euclidean minima in the nonzero marked cosets; the right side depends on the chosen binary Hamming metric. Thus (R.4.2a.1) is exactly the required metric-compatibility theorem. When it holds and $d(C)\ge8$, every nonzero glue coset has minimum squared norm at least $4$; combining this with $m_0^{\times}=4$ proves that every nonzero vector of the glued lattice has squared norm at least $4$. ∎

<a id="corollary-r42b-conditional-code-to-rootlessness-transfer"></a>**Corollary R.4.2b (Conditional Code-to-Rootlessness Transfer).** Assume items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5, the base-lattice identity $m_0^{\times}=4$, and Equation (R.4.2a.1), but do not assume the nonzero-coset bound in item 3. If $C=\mathcal G_{24}$, then $d(C)=8$ supplies that missing bound, and the resulting overlattice is rootless and hence isometric to the Leech lattice.

*Proof.* Since $d(\mathcal G_{24})=8$, Equation (R.4.2a.1) gives
$$
\min_{0\ne h\in\phi^{-1}(\mathcal G_{24})}m(h)\ge4.
$$
Together with $m_0^{\times}=4$, this excludes norm-$2$ vectors in both the zero and nonzero glue cosets. Items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5 make the overlattice positive-definite, even, unimodular, and rank $24$; Niemeier classification therefore identifies it with $\Lambda_{24}$. A packing-energy statement or physical stability claim requires its own stated theorem or dynamical certificate. ∎

<a id="corollary-r42b1-conditional-universal-optimality-support-for-dimension-specific-lattice-subledgers"></a>**Corollary R.4.2b.1 (Conditional Universal-Optimality Support for Dimension-Specific Lattice Subledgers).** Let a PU lattice subledger have Euclidean dimension $n\in\{8,24\}$ and density $\rho>0$. Let $\Lambda_8=E_8$ and $\Lambda_{24}$ be the Leech lattice, both at covolume $1$. Assume its admissible periodic-configuration class $\mathcal A_n$ consists of configurations of density $\rho$ and contains $\rho^{-1/n}\Lambda_n$. For $X=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}(t_i+L)$ with distinct representatives modulo a period lattice $L$, suppose the descended PCE pair cost is the finite energy per point
$$
V_f(X)=\frac1m\sum_{i=1}^{m}\ \sum_{\substack{x\in X\\x\ne t_i}} f(|t_i-x|^2),
\qquad X\in\mathcal A_n.
\tag{R.4.2b.1}
$$
Assume $f$ is completely monotone on squared distance and the accepted finite-response ledger proves that no non-pairwise, anomaly, quotient, calibration, or capacity term remains outside this cost. Then $\rho^{-1/n}\Lambda_n$ minimizes the subledger cost among $\mathcal A_n$.

This support is fixed-dimension support only. It does not derive $d_0=8$, $M=24$, $D=4$, $K_0=3$, or the three-generation count, and it does not promote the $D_4$/24-cell leg to universal optimality. The $D_4$ and 24-cell statements remain the kissing-number, triality, and design-optimality statements already used elsewhere in the manuscript.

*Proof.* Write $\Lambda_n=E_8$ for $n=8$ and let $\Lambda_n$ be the Leech lattice for $n=24$, each at covolume $1$. For an admissible configuration $X$ of density $\rho$, set $Y=\rho^{1/n}X$, which has density $1$, and define $g(t)=f(\rho^{-2/n}t)$. For every integer $j\ge0$,
$$
(-1)^j g^{(j)}(t)
=\rho^{-2j/n}(-1)^j f^{(j)}(\rho^{-2/n}t)\ge0,
$$
so $g$ is completely monotone. The normalized periodic-cell sums obey $V_g(Y)=V_f(X)$. Applying the universal-optimality theorem at density $1$ gives
$$
V_f(X)=V_g(Y)\ge V_g(\Lambda_n)
=V_f(\rho^{-1/n}\Lambda_n).
$$
The comparison lattice is admissible and its energy is finite by hypothesis. Thus it minimizes the stated pair-cost subledger. This argument assumes the dimension, density and entire accepted cost form; it neither selects those dimensions nor supplies a parent invariant for other source roles. ∎
