<a id="proposition-r42c-branchwise-m-24-compatibility-ledger-for-code-lattice-gauge-and-generation-records"></a>**Proposition R.4.2c (Branchwise $M=24$ Compatibility Ledger for Code, Lattice, Gauge, and Generation Records).** On the conjunction of the separately registered branches:

1. the predictive-recovery rate branch plus an accepted $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{dist}}$ selects the extended binary Golay code $[24,12,8]$ up to coordinate permutation;
2. the retained arithmetic identities give $M/2=ab=k=n_G=\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathrm{Gr}(2,8)=12$ on their cited branches;
3. the explicit coordinate construction following Lemma R.4.5 sends the selected Golay copy to an even unimodular rootless rank-$24$ lattice $\Lambda_C\cong\Lambda_{24}$;
4. independently, the $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ scaffold yields the same isometry class only after items 1–2 of Lemma R.4.5 and either its complete minimum ledger or $m_0^{\times}=4$ together with Equation (R.4.2a.1); and
5. the scaled three-block scaffold is compatible with, but does not derive, the conditional family-count selector.

These statements share branch-selected integers and a compatible lattice isometry class; no one of the gauge, generation, code, or mode-count records is thereby derived from another. The coordinate construction closes the mathematical code-to-Leech map, but neither route establishes a physical vacuum, decoder for physical noise, dynamical stability, or an energy gap. ∎

### R.4.2.4 Niemeier Classification and Conditional Selection

<a id="theorem-r410-unique-rootless-positive-definite-niemeier-lattice-conditional-pce-selection"></a>**Theorem R.4.10 (Unique Rootless Positive-Definite Niemeier Lattice; Conditional PCE Selection).** Among Euclidean-isometry classes of positive-definite even unimodular lattices of rank $24$, exactly one has no norm-$2$ vectors: the class of the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$. Therefore any already-registered PCE branch whose admissibility conditions independently require a positive-definite even unimodular rootless rank-$24$ lattice selects the Leech isometry class uniquely. PCE alone does not supply positive definiteness, rootlessness, a marked embedded representative, or physical-vacuum stability.

*Proof.* Chenevier and Lannes (2015, Theorems II.3.16–II.3.17) classify the positive-definite even unimodular rank-$24$ lattices into exactly $24$ Euclidean-isometry classes: $23$ have nonempty norm-$2$ root systems and one has the empty root system. Their Theorem II.3.16 identifies the rootless class as $\Lambda_{24}$, consistently with Conway (1969a). Restricting an independently certified admissible class to the stated positive-definite, even, unimodular, rank-$24$, rootless conditions therefore leaves exactly the Leech isometry class. No theorem here chooses a marking, establishes the registered gluing datum, or supplies a physical vacuum. ∎

<a id="remark-r42a-nested-uniqueness"></a>**Remark R.4.2a: Nested Uniqueness.** Three finite statements meet at the number $24$: the faithful-shell branch selects the least feasible $D=4$ from $24\le K(D)$, $K(3)=12$, and the regular $24$-cell; Niemeier classification contains exactly $24$ positive-definite even unimodular rank-$24$ isometry classes; and exactly one of them is rootless. Their conjunction identifies one rootless rank-$24$ isometry class together with compatible finite branch data; physical vacuum realization retains its registered certificate.

1. On the registered faithful-shell branch, $M=24\le K(D)$ and least-feasible selection give $D=4$ using $K(3)=12$ and the regular $24$-cell.
2. At the independently fixed positive-definite rank $24$, the Niemeier theorem classifies exactly $24$ even unimodular lattice isometry classes.
3. Within that fixed-rank class, rootlessness selects the unique Leech isometry class.

### R.4.2.5 Automorphism Group and Symmetry Structure

<a id="proposition-r44-automorphism-group"></a>**Proposition R.4.4 (Automorphism Group).** The stabilizer in $\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_{24})$ of a coordinate frame has the form $2^{12}:M_{24}$, where $M_{24}=\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{24})$. This follows from Proposition R.4.8 (Step 4). ∎

<a id="remark-r42b-physical-status-of-sporadic-symmetries"></a>**Remark R.4.2b: Physical Status of Sporadic Symmetries.** Once the marked Golay and Leech structures are selected, their automorphism groups are determined: $\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal G_{24})=M_{24}$ and $\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_{24})=\mathrm{Co}_0=2.\mathrm{Co}_1$, whose central quotient is $\mathrm{Co}_1$. A physical action of those groups is not thereby established. These groups act on the $24$-dimensional mode space, not directly on the four-dimensional Euclidean response carrier or on any promoted spacetime. A physical action on $3+1$ spacetime would require independent promotion and observable-action certificates in addition to the mode-to-carrier map.

The experimental predictions in **Section Z.29–Z.31** provide protocols for testing the $M = 24$ mode structure and Golay code organization. Whether sporadic group structure itself produces observable signatures—such as specific degeneracy patterns in quantum systems engineered to probe the full 24-mode space, or constraints on allowed transitions—remains an open question.

### R.4.2.6 Over-Determination and Structural Consistency

**Independence verification:** 
- Mechanism 1 (Family selection): relies on the $\pi_2(\Sigma_8)$ sector lattice, the marked family-charge map, the SM15 anomaly equations or the SM16 linear-anomaly-plus-charge-norm route, a registered CP-active realization, and the additive-monotone realized-count objective (Sections R.3–R.4.1).
- Mechanism 2 (Triality compatibility): relies on the marked real 8-carrier $\mathfrak{so}(8)$ structure and the $D_4$ diagram automorphism group $S_3$ (Proposition R.4.2).
- Mechanism 3 (Geometric compatibility): relies on sphere packing optimality and $E_8$/Leech lattice constructions (Section R.4.2).
- Mechanism 4 (Mode-count compatibility): relies on the QFI interface count $M=24$ from Theorem Z.5.

These four strands are logically distinct, though they share the minimal-branch backbone constants $d_0 = 8$ (Theorem Z.2) and $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$ (Theorem J.1). Only Mechanism 1 derives the minimal family-count theorem. Mechanism 2 shows that a marked real 8-carrier has a canonical three-element triality orbit; Mechanism 3 shows that the geometric sector carries a compatible three-fold lattice scaffold; Mechanism 4 shows that the interface-mode count is compatible with three 8-dimensional blocks.

**Structural compatibility from QFI mode count:** The interface mode count $M = 24$ (**Appendix Z**, Theorem Z.5) admits the factorization:
$$
M = 24 = 8 \times 3 = d_0 \times N_g.
$$
This factorization shows that the topological count is compatible with organizing the interface modes into three 8-dimensional blocks; the QFI structure is a consistency check.

**Qualitative assessment:** The convergence of one branch-resolved derivation with three compatibility checks supports internal coherence. The proved result is the SM15 anomaly-plus-CP minimum or the SM16 linear-anomaly-plus-primitive-norm-plus-CP minimum, followed by the additive-monotone pre-flavor realization theorem.

**Remark: Methodological Note.** The strength of the consistency argument rests on the agreement between the topological derivation, the $D_4$ triality orbit, the $E_8$/Leech scaffold, and the QFI factorization. The latter three supply supporting structural checks. ∎

The $24$-cell realizes the local kissing configuration on the third branch; network dynamics from it requires a separate realization theorem. The correlation and stability protocols in Sections Z.29--Z.31 test distinct records: a Golay correlation pattern tests the predictive-recovery encoding; a mathematical lattice check can test either the explicit coordinates or the abstract marked-gluing ledger; and a physical lattice or stability claim additionally requires its realization dynamics. Instability or dimensional reduction requires an independent evolution equation and Lyapunov certificate.

### R.4.2.8 Syndrome-Charge Homology

<a id="definition-r428a-predictive-syndrome-complex"></a>**Definition R.4.2.8a (Predictive Syndrome Complex).** Let
$$
C_2\xrightarrow{\partial_2}C_1\xrightarrow{\partial_1}C_0
$$
be a chain complex of finite abelian groups and group homomorphisms, with $\partial_1\partial_2=0$, on a marked Golay--Leech carrier. Let $\nu:C_0\to N$ be a group homomorphism to a finite abelian group satisfying $\nu\partial_1=0$. Set $K=\ker\nu$ and $B=\operatorname{im}\partial_1$, so $B\subset K$. Defect syndrome labels are elements of $K$, and their algebraic combination law is addition. Register a set $U\subset B$ of permitted translation increments such that $U=-U$ and the subgroup generated by $U$ is $B$. A registered neutral move is $s\mapsto s+u$ with $u\in U$. Define local-move equivalence by reachability through a finite sequence of these moves. The syndrome-charge group is
$$
H_{\mathrm{synd}}=K/B
=\ker\nu/\operatorname{im}\partial_1.
\tag{R.30}
$$
These are algebraic syndrome data; their interpretation as physical processes and readouts requires a separate registered realization.

<a id="theorem-r428b-charge-as-stable-error-correction-syndrome"></a>**Theorem R.4.2.8b (Charge as Stable Error-Correction Syndrome).** In a predictive syndrome complex:

1. registered neutral moves preserve $[s]\in H_{\mathrm{synd}}$;
2. algebraic combination of syndrome labels induces addition in $H_{\mathrm{synd}}$;
3. two syndrome labels are local-move equivalent if and only if they represent the same quotient class;
4. on $\mathscr H=\mathbb C[K]$, with orthonormal basis $\{|s\rangle:s\in K\}$, suppose a represented allowed-process algebra $\mathscr A\subset\operatorname{End}(\mathscr H)$ satisfies
$$
\langle t|A|s\rangle=0
\quad\text{whenever }t-s\notin B,
\qquad A\in\mathscr A.
$$
Then each quotient-class subspace is invariant under $\mathscr A$, and its orthogonal projector commutes with every $A\in\mathscr A$.

*Proof.* Since $\nu$ is a homomorphism and $\nu\partial_1=0$, $K$ and $B$ are subgroups with $B\subset K$. Each permitted increment lies in $B$, so a move leaves the quotient class unchanged, proving (1). For $s_1,s_2\in K$, $\nu(s_1+s_2)=0$ and $[s_1+s_2]=[s_1]+[s_2]$, proving (2).

Any finite sequence of permitted moves changes a label by an element of $B$. Conversely, if $t-s\in B$, the generating and reversibility assumptions express $t-s$ as a finite sum of elements of $U$. The corresponding sequence remains in $K$ and carries $s$ to $t$. This proves (3).

For $h\in H_{\mathrm{synd}}$, let $\Pi_h$ project onto the span of basis labels in class $h$. The hypothesis in (4) gives
$$
(\Pi_hA-A\Pi_h)_{ts}
=\bigl(\mathbf1_{[t]=h}-\mathbf1_{[s]=h}\bigr)A_{ts}=0:
$$
the coefficient vanishes for equal classes, and $A_{ts}=0$ for different classes. This proves (4). The conclusion is class preservation for the represented algebra. It does not establish irreducibility, completeness of physical superselection sectors, or indistinguishability under unspecified operational protocols. ∎

<a id="corollary-r428c-golay-distance-protects-nontrivial-syndrome-charge"></a>**Corollary R.4.2.8c (Golay Distance Protects Nontrivial Syndrome Charge).** On a marked Golay-stabilized branch with the syndrome complex and represented label space of Theorem R.4.2.8b, let $\operatorname{supp}(u)\subset\{1,\ldots,24\}$ be the registered coordinate support of a syndrome change $u\in K$. Independently assume the syndrome-distance certificate
$$
d_{\mathrm{synd}}
:=\min_{\substack{u\in K\\ [u]\ne0}}
|\operatorname{supp}(u)|=8.
$$
The binary Golay distance alone does not identify this quotient distance without a registered syndrome map. For every represented operator $A$ supported on a carrier set $S$, assume each nonzero matrix element $\langle t|A|s\rangle$ satisfies $\operatorname{supp}(t-s)\subset S$. Then every such operator with $|S|<8$ preserves each syndrome-charge class subspace.

*Proof.* If $\langle t|A|s\rangle\ne0$, the change $u=t-s$ lies in $K$ and has support size at most $|S|<8$. The distance certificate therefore forces $[u]=0$, so $[t]=[s]$. All matrix elements between different quotient classes vanish; the class projectors commute with $A$ by the calculation in Theorem R.4.2.8b. This proves preservation of the syndrome-charge classes. Decoder success, readout discrimination and protection of arbitrary physical states require their own noise, instrument and realization certificates. ∎
<a id="lemma-r428d1-separate-oscillator-registers-and-a-finite-compiler"></a>**Lemma R.4.2.8d.1 (Separate Oscillator Registers and a Finite Compiler).**

1. **Actual tensor registers and elementary controls.** Register $\hbar,\Omega>0$ and $T=2\pi/\Omega$. A $q$-level factor is a separate set of $q$ bosonic rails, each of frequency $\Omega$, on the invariant one-excitation code
$$
S_q|j\rangle=c_j^\dagger|\mathrm{vac}\rangle,
\qquad H_q^0=\hbar\Omega\sum_{j=0}^{q-1}c_j^\dagger c_j.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.C.1}
$$
Distinct logical factors use distinct physical rail sets. Their tensor product carries all entangled inputs and effects by $\bigotimes_qS_q$; a single global rail encoding is not used to change a memory/output ownership cut. Each ready register has one excitation and stores $\hbar\Omega$.

For a local unitary $e^{ih}$ choose $h=h^*$ with $\|h\|\le\pi$, and use
$$
s(t)=3(t/T)^2-2(t/T)^3,\qquad
H_h(t)=H_q^0-\hbar\dot s(t)\sum_{j,k}h_{jk}c_j^\dagger c_k.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.C.2}
$$
Its exact code endpoint is $e^{ih}$, since the free period is one; its local one-particle coefficient matrix is at least $\hbar\Omega I/4$. For a phase controlled by two selected occupation levels, use the positive cross-Kerr pulse
$$
H_\vartheta(t)=H^0+\vartheta\hbar\dot s(t)n_{A,j}n_{B,k},
\qquad 0\le\vartheta\le2\pi.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.C.3}
$$
It has endpoint $e^{-i\vartheta n_{A,j}n_{B,k}}$. Every required sign of a controlled phase has such a representative modulo $2\pi$. Local gates have code norm action at most $\pi\hbar$ and peak coupling at most $3\hbar\Omega/4$; general (3) has action at most $2\pi\hbar$ and peak code coupling at most $3\hbar\Omega/2$. The latter bound is needed for angles above $\pi$. The full source is nonnegative on every occupation sector: local gates are second quantizations of positive one-particle matrices and the Kerr terms are nonnegative. The controls are serialized, vanish at their endpoints and preserve each register's total occupation exactly.

2. **A finite compiler with its workspace counted.** Any unitary on $r\ge1$ actual registers of total code dimension $D$ is exactly synthesized using at most
$$
B(D,r)=\left[\frac{D(D-1)}2+D\right](2r+1)(84r+4)
\tag{R.4.2.8d.C.4}
$$
elementary full-period slots (2)--(3), with at most $2r-1$ reusable scratch dual-rail registers initially and finally in $0$. Identity padding gives a common deadline when comparing compiled branches. This is a finite upper bound, not an optimal synthesis count.

Here is an explicit construction. Complex Givens elimination uses at most $D(D-1)/2$ two-basis-vector rotations followed by $D$ basis phases. A Gray path changes at most one register coordinate at a time and reduces a two-vector rotation to at most $2r-1$ adjacent-coordinate controlled gates. Equality to a selected local basis value is copied into a scratch bit by Hadamard--$\pi$-Kerr--Hadamard. Conjunctions of these equality bits use Toffoli gates. For three bits,
$$
4abc=a+b+c-(a\mathbin\oplus b)-(a\mathbin\oplus c)
-(b\mathbin\oplus c)+(a\mathbin\oplus b\mathbin\oplus c).
\tag{R.4.2.8d.C.5}
$$
Computing each parity, applying its $\pm\pi/4$ phase and uncomputing gives a controlled-controlled phase with ten CNOTs and seven single-bit phases. Each CNOT uses two Hadamards and a $\pi$-Kerr pulse; two further Hadamards turn this phase into a Toffoli. Thus a Toffoli uses at most $39$ elementary slots.

For $k\ge1$ equality controls, compute their $k$ flags, compute their conjunction with $k-1$ Toffolis, apply the controlled two-level rotation, and undo both computations. Diagonalize that rotation on its two target levels; its eigenbasis change, two controlled level phases and inverse basis change use four slots. The whole gate uses at most $6k+78(k-1)+4\le84r+4$ slots. A phase on one joint basis vector uses at most $6r+78(r-1)+1\le84r+4$. At most $r$ equality flags and $r-1$ conjunction flags are live, proving the scratch bound and (4). An uncontrolled local gate uses (2) directly. Every temporary bit is uncomputed coherently, including its relative phase.

*Proof.* On one excitation, $c_j^\dagger c_k$ restricts to $|j\rangle\langle k|$. Each degenerate register's free propagator over $T$ is the identity, and its local control commutes with its own fixed logarithm. Equation (2) therefore integrates exactly to $e^{ih}$. Since $\max\dot s=3/(2T)$ and $\int\dot s=1$, its peak norm is at most $3\hbar\Omega/4$ and its action at most $\pi\hbar$. Its one-particle free-plus-control matrix is positive. The Kerr occupation product is nonnegative on the full Fock space and has value zero or one on the relevant code factors, proving (3)'s action and peak bounds. Occupation numbers are integers, so adding $2\pi$ to its phase changes no endpoint, including off-code sectors. Serialization preserves the full-source positivity at every time.

For the compiler, complex Givens rotations eliminate successive matrix entries of an arbitrary finite unitary until a diagonal unitary remains. Each such rotation acts on exactly two product-basis vectors. Join those vectors by a path changing each differing coordinate once. Swapping the intermediate path vectors conjugates a rotation on the first adjacent pair to the desired pair, using at most $2r-1$ adjacent controlled operations and leaving all other basis vectors unchanged after undoing the swaps. Equality flags are coherent CNOTs controlled by a chosen register occupation. Conjunction into fresh zero bits uses $k-1$ Toffolis, and reverse application restores those bits and every equality flag to zero. The parity identity (5) holds on every three-bit basis state, so the seven commuting parity phases implement $e^{i\pi abc}$. Computing and undoing the pair and triple parities requires $2[3\cdot1+1\cdot2]=10$ CNOTs. Counting their three elementary pulses, seven phases and two target Hadamards gives $39$. The controlled two-level gate is its local spectral eigenbasis, two controlled eigenphases and the inverse eigenbasis; it preserves the exact phase also when the flag is in superposition. The explicit slot counts in item 2 follow, and the generous factor in (4) bounds both rotations and diagonal phases. The reversible flag computations use at most $r+(r-1)$ scratch bits and return them to their ready state for reuse. $\square$

<a id="theorem-r428d-a-marked-golay-charge-and-its-complete-correctable-instruments"></a>**Theorem R.4.2.8d (A Marked Golay Charge and Its Complete Correctable Instruments).** The following finite instance of Definition R.4.2.8a has a nontrivial physical class observable, an exact coherent decoder and a complete registered correctable-instrument class. Coordinates throughout are the same twenty-four coordinates used in the marked Golay--Leech construction of Section R.4.2; physical coordinate $i$ is a distinct two-state register.

1. **Code, complex and coordinate map.** Work over $\mathbb F_2$, with row messages $m=(m_0,\ldots,m_{11})$. Let $G=[I_{12}\mid A]$, where the rows of $A$, in coordinate order, are
$$
\begin{gathered}
101011100011,\quad111110010010,\quad110100101011,\quad110001110110,\\
110011011001,\quad011001101101,\quad001100110111,\quad101101111000,\\
010110111100,\quad001011011110,\quad101110001101,\quad010111000111.
\end{gathered}
\tag{R.4.2.8d.1}
$$
This is the systematic extended binary Golay marking recorded in the SageMath reference manual. Put $K=\{mG:m\in\mathbb F_2^{12}\}$ and $\mathbf1=(1,\ldots,1)\in K$. Register
$$
C_2=0,\quad C_1=\mathbb F_2,\quad C_0=K,\quad
\partial_1(b)=b\mathbf1,\quad N=0,\quad \nu=0,\quad U=\{\mathbf1\}.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.2}
$$
Then $B=\langle\mathbf1\rangle$, and $H_{\mathrm{synd}}=K/B\simeq\mathbb F_2^{11}$. An explicit quotient and section are
$$
b=m_{11},\qquad h_i=m_i+b\ (0\le i<11),\qquad
c(h,b)=(h_0+b,\ldots,h_{10}+b,b)G.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.3}
$$
There are $2048$ classes, each with two codewords. With the actual coordinate support $\operatorname{supp}(c)=\{i:c_i=1\}$, the quotient distance in Corollary R.4.2.8c is exactly eight. Each marked vector $c/\sqrt2$ belongs to the already constructed $L_B(K)\subset\Lambda_K$: its parity is in $K$ and $\sum_i c_i=0\pmod4$. This supplies the code-to-lattice marking used here. The carrier and dynamics below supply the additional physical charge realization.

2. **Physical carrier and complete decoder domain.** Let $\mathcal H_D=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes24}$, with basis $|w\rangle$, $w\in\mathbb F_2^{24}$, and code projector $P=\sum_{c\in K}|c\rangle\langle c|$. Set
$$
\mathcal E=\{e\in\mathbb F_2^{24}:|e|\le3\},\quad
m_E=|\mathcal E|=2325,\quad z(w)=Gw^T.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.4}
$$
The syndromes $z(e)$ are pairwise distinct. Define the leader $\ell(z)=e$ on these $2325$ syndromes, and retain an invalid-syndrome outcome on the other $1771$. Thus
$$
\begin{split}
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{good}}&=\operatorname{span}\{|c+e\rangle:c\in K,e\in\mathcal E\},\\
D|c+e\rangle&=|c\rangle\otimes|e\rangle,\qquad
\dim\mathcal H_{\mathrm{good}}=9\,523\,200.
\end{split}
\tag{R.4.2.8d.5}
$$
Here $D$ is a unitary identification of this subspace with $\mathbb C[K]\otimes\mathbb C[\mathcal E]$. Proposition R.4.2.8e supplies its finite physical implementation with the input and displaced syndrome registers retained. The physical charge/validity projectors are
$$
P_h=\sum_{b\in\mathbb F_2,\ e\in\mathcal E}
|c(h,b)+e\rangle\langle c(h,b)+e|,
\qquad P_\perp=I-\sum_hP_h.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.6}
$$
Each $P_h$ has rank $4650$; $P_\perp$ has rank $7\,254\,016$. The operator-to-chain identification is the complete matrix-unit map
$$
|c+e\rangle\langle c'+e'|
\longmapsto |c\rangle\langle c'|\otimes|e\rangle\langle e'|,
\qquad \Delta c=c+c'\in K.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.7}
$$
It extends linearly and preserves products, adjoints and all quantum instruments on the good subspace. A matrix unit is neutral precisely when $\Delta c\in B$. The exceptional sector is retained separately; it is not assigned a valid chain label.

For $a\in\mathbb F_2^{11}$, the commuting physical unitaries
$$
Z_a=\sum_h(-1)^{a\cdot h}P_h+P_\perp
\tag{R.4.2.8d.8}
$$
give a faithful representation of $\mathbb F_2^{11}$, with a separate validity measurement. On two valid carriers, $Z_a\otimes Z_a$ measures the character of $h_1+h_2$. A neutral pointer can record this sum. Its classical record is an observation of the total charge and is not an additional charged carrier.

3. **Every instrument in the registered error class.** Write $X_e=\bigotimes_iX_i^{e_i}$ and $V_e=X_eP$. A registered error window begins on $P\mathcal H_D$ and admits every finite instrument whose Kraus operators belong to $\operatorname{span}\{V_e:e\in\mathcal E\}$. Equivalently, it admits every finite family of matrices
$$
C_a\succeq0\quad\hbox{on }\mathbb C[\mathcal E],\qquad
\sum_a\operatorname{Tr}C_a=1,
\tag{R.4.2.8d.9}
$$
with complete leaves
$$
\mathcal N_a(\rho)=\sum_{e,f\in\mathcal E}(C_a)_{ef}V_e\rho V_f^*,
\qquad \mathcal N_\mathrm{ready\,fail}(\rho)=(I-P)\rho(I-P).
\tag{R.4.2.8d.10}
$$
The last leaf completes the instrument on every input to the twenty-four registers. Its probability is zero on the registered code input. The matrices $C_a$, rather than a chosen Kraus decomposition, determine the exported completely positive leaves; an exported refinement of a Kraus/environment label is registered as a corresponding finer outcome. Arbitrary coherent superpositions of the weight-at-most-three bit changes and arbitrary correlations among their retained syndrome labels are included.

For every code state, including entanglement with an arbitrary spectator $R$, the exact identities are
$$
V_e^*V_f=\delta_{ef}P,\qquad
(D\otimes I_R)(\mathcal N_a\otimes\mathrm{id}_R)(\rho_{DR})
(D^*\otimes I_R)=\rho_{KR}\otimes C_a.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.11}
$$
The second equality uses the output tensor order $K,R,\mathcal E$. Thus the physical recovery restores the entire code state and transfers the displaced syndrome state to a retained fresh register. Its unconditional recovered channel is the identity on $P\mathcal H_D$. Reading the syndrome refines the record and leaves the recovered code state unchanged. Reading the charge itself instead gives the complete nondemolition leaves $P_h\rho P_h$ and $P_\perp\rho P_\perp$, with their actual postmeasurement states.

Every Kraus operator in (10), including the ready-failure operator, commutes with all $P_h$ and $P_\perp$ on the full data Hilbert space. The same is true of the all-ones gauge translation $X_{\mathbf1}$ and of the physical recovery extension in Proposition R.4.2.8e. Consequently each complete error/recovery history preserves every initial charge sector and has exactly the stated syndrome and spectator output, including the state changes of any registered projective readout. If a subsequent instrument has charge-dependent outcome probabilities, conditioning can change a mixture's class weights; the unconditional conserved observable and the support of each leaf remain as stated.

4. **The complete neutral algebra and the correction schedule.** For any finite trace-preserving instrument with Kraus operators $K_{a r}$ on the data carrier,
$$
\sum_{a,r}K_{ar}^*P_hK_{ar}=P_h\quad\hbox{for every }h,\perp
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
[K_{ar},P_h]=0\quad\hbox{for every }a,r,h,\perp.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.12}
$$
Thus its neutral operator algebra is exactly
$\bigoplus_h\operatorname{End}(P_h\mathcal H_D)\oplus\operatorname{End}(P_\perp\mathcal H_D)$, and (7) realizes the hypothesis of Theorem R.4.2.8b. Conservation and exact quantum correctability are distinct properties: this algebra also contains operations that destroy coherence within a class. The correctable class in item 3 is specified by its full bit-error span, rather than by every individually reversible ambient channel. A logical translation $X_c$ with $c\in K\setminus B$ is reversible and changes $h$.

The registered repeated protocol completes recovery, checks the ready outcome and isolates the old syndrome/environment before starting the next error window. Every finite adaptive sequence satisfying that rule is covered. Two successive uncorrected windows are not combined into a single weight-three window. Indeed, take an octad $c_*\in K$, split six of its coordinates into disjoint three-element sets $e_1,e_2$, and let $f$ be its remaining two coordinates. Then $e_1+e_2=c_*+f$, so the registered decoder assigns this uncorrected composition to $c_*$ and changes the nonzero quotient charge. Finally, any physical operator supported on fewer than eight coordinate registers, restricted from code to code, has no matrix elements between different codewords and therefore satisfies the support premise of Corollary R.4.2.8c. Arbitrary off-code leakage still has its explicit validity and recovery record.

*Proof.* The identity block proves $\dim K=12$. Direct multiplication of the twelve displayed rows gives $GG^T=0$; hence $K=K^\perp$. For completeness, the finite weight certificate can be reproduced without importing a distance assertion. Start with the list $L_0=(0)$ and, for row $g_j$ of $G$, set $L_{j+1}=L_j\mathbin\Vert(g_j+L_j)$, preserving both halves. For each resulting bit string $w$, increment the coefficient at $z^{|w|}$. The twelve recurrences, involving exactly $2^{12}$ final strings and the explicitly displayed rows, give
$$
\sum_{c\in K}z^{|c|}=1+759z^8+2576z^{12}+759z^{16}+z^{24}.
\tag{R.4.2.8d.13}
$$
The sole weight-twenty-four word is $\mathbf1=(1,\ldots,1)G$. This establishes the minimum weight eight, double evenness, and all counts used here. Equations (2)--(3) now follow by linearity. A nontrivial quotient element is represented by a codeword other than $0,\mathbf1$; (13) gives its minimum coordinate weight eight, achieved by every row of $G$. The stated lattice membership follows from the defining parity and sum conditions for $L_B(K)$ in Section R.4.2.

If $z(e)=z(f)$ for distinct errors of weight at most three, then $e+f\in\ker G=K$ has weight at most six, contradicting (13). Likewise $c+e=c'+f$ forces $c=c'$ and $e=f$. This proves the unique decoder and dimensions in (5)--(6). Every four-coordinate bit error has an invalid syndrome: equality with a radius-three leader would produce a nonzero codeword of weight at most seven. Formula (7) is a basis-unitary conjugation, so it preserves the complete algebra, not just the diagonal labels. Formula (8) obeys $Z_aZ_b=Z_{a+b}$; every $h$ occurs, so its character representation is faithful. Tensoring two such diagonal decompositions gives the addition rule.

For any $c,c'\in K$, a matrix element of $PX_{e+f}P$ requires $c+c'=e+f$. The same distance argument gives $PX_{e+f}P=\delta_{ef}P$. Write each admitted Kraus operator as $K_{ar}=\sum_e\alpha_{ar,e}V_e$ and set $(C_a)_{ef}=\sum_r\alpha_{ar,e}\overline{\alpha_{ar,f}}$. These matrices are positive, and the Kraus normalization is exactly $(\sum_a\operatorname{Tr}C_a)P=P$. Conversely a square-root factorization of each $C_a$ gives precisely these Kraus operators. Their Hilbert--Schmidt independence, with $\operatorname{Tr}V_e^*V_f=4096\delta_{ef}$, proves uniqueness of $C_a$ from the CP leaf's Choi matrix. Adding $I-P$ gives total Kraus square $I$ on all inputs.

On matrix units $|c\rangle\langle c'|$, conjugating the leaf by $D$ gives $|c\rangle\langle c'|\otimes C_a$. Linearity proves (11) for every state and every spectator. This identity proves exact quantum recovery and the full outcome law $\Pr(a)=\operatorname{Tr}C_a$. Each $V_e$ maps a code basis vector to a good basis vector with the same $c$ and annihilates every non-code input. Thus $P_hV_e=V_eP_h$ on the full data space. The diagonal $I-P$ commutes as well. Translation by $\mathbf1$ sends $c+e$ to $(c+\mathbf1)+e$, retaining $h$; since it is a permutation, it preserves the exceptional complement too.

For (12), the backward implication is immediate from trace preservation. For the forward implication take $v\in\ker P_h$. Then
$0=\langle v,\sum_{ar}K_{ar}^*P_hK_{ar}v\rangle=\sum_{ar}\|P_hK_{ar}v\|^2$,
so $P_hK_{ar}(I-P_h)=0$. The same argument for $I-P_h$, whose Heisenberg image is also itself, gives $(I-P_h)K_{ar}P_h=0$. Therefore every Kraus operator is block diagonal. Products, adaptive restrictions and sums over the complete record preserve the same blocks. Exact recovery returns the data to $P$, which supplies the induction premise at each new window. The explicit octad example proves the boundary on uncorrected composition. A tensor-supported operator on a set $S$ has zero basis matrix elements when the two bit strings differ outside $S$; with code inputs and outputs and $|S|<8$, the distance forces equality. This verifies the stated physical support map. ∎

<a id="proposition-r428e-positive-source-dynamics-for-conserved-syndrome-charge"></a>**Proposition R.4.2.8e (Positive Source Dynamics for Conserved Syndrome Charge).** Every finite catalogue of the complete instruments, recoveries, neutral gauge operations and charge readouts in Theorem R.4.2.8d has the following finite register-source realization. The source premises are distinct oscillator registers, a positive assisted-hopping interaction with specified finite register coefficients, externally supplied finite pulse timing, and the complete nondemolition occupation service already used in Lemma R.4.2.8d.1. These premises define the physical model. The result identifies its complete retained instruments and conserved observable, including the controls and intermediate stages.

1. **Actual registers and charge-preserving dilations.** Each of the twenty-four data bits is its own dual-rail oscillator register. A neutral $q$-level environment, pointer or program is a separate $q$-rail register, on the one-excitation code
$$
S_q|k\rangle=b_k^*|\mathrm{vac}\rangle,\qquad
H_q^0=\hbar\Omega\sum_{k=0}^{q-1}b_k^*b_k,
\quad \hbar,\Omega>0,\quad T=\frac{2\pi}{\Omega}.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.1}
$$
Thus physical bit support and the tensor access cut are preserved by $S_D=S_2^{\otimes24}$. Every occupied register stores exactly $\hbar\Omega$ on this code. The source includes all ready environment and pointer factors; previous records remain separate factors.

For a catalogue instrument, choose a square-root Kraus factorization of its matrices $C_a$, with $R_C=\sum_a\operatorname{rank}C_a$. At most $R_C+1\le2325A+1$ neutral environment levels and $A+1$ pointer levels suffice for its $A$ named outcomes and its ready-failure outcome. The complete isometry is
$$
W_{\mathcal N}|\psi\rangle
=\sum_{a,r}K_{ar}|\psi\rangle|a,r\rangle_E|a\rangle_F
 +(I-P)|\psi\rangle|\mathrm{fail}\rangle_E|\mathrm{fail}\rangle_F.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.2}
$$
Here $K_{ar}=\sum_e\alpha_{ar,e}V_e$ has the coefficients in the proof of Theorem R.4.2.8d. Retaining $E$ gives the specified physical dilation; tracing its private factor and reading $F$ gives exactly (R.4.2.8d.10). Its private ready state and chosen Kraus factorization belong to the source record. Enlarging the public access cut to $E$ requires the correspondingly finer record.

The recovery uses a fresh $(2325+1)$-level archive and a two-level validity pointer. Its ready-input action is
$$
\begin{split}
W_{\mathrm{rec}}|c+e\rangle|0\rangle_E|0\rangle_F
 &=|c\rangle|e\rangle_E|0\rangle_F,\\
W_{\mathrm{rec}}|w\rangle|0\rangle_E|0\rangle_F
 &=|w\rangle|\mathrm{fail}\rangle_E|1\rangle_F
 \quad(|w\rangle\notin\mathcal H_{\mathrm{good}}).
\end{split}
\tag{R.4.2.8e.3}
$$
Distinct inputs have orthogonal outputs, and both lines retain the same data charge/validity sector. A charge readout uses a fresh $2049$-level pointer and the isometry
$$
W_{\mathrm{charge}}|\psi\rangle|0\rangle_F
=\sum_hP_h|\psi\rangle|h\rangle_F+P_\perp|\psi\rangle|\perp\rangle_F.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.4}
$$
All these isometries extend, separately within each data charge/validity block, to unitaries $U$ on their full finite register codes satisfying $[U,P_h\otimes I]=0$ for every $h,\perp$. The same construction covers every neutral finite instrument characterized by (R.4.2.8d.12). This is an existence and exact synthesis statement on every finite set of real or complex coefficient matrices. Algebraic coefficient records additionally admit an effective finite exact construction by algebraic linear algebra. The effective finite-description claim has this algebraic-input scope; the all-real statement classifies the source family mathematically.

2. **A dynamical stage register and one fixed conserved operator.** Use the explicit finite register compiler of Lemma R.4.2.8d.1 to produce a word $\mathcal U=U_L\cdots U_1$ with identity padding so $L\ge2$. Its $s$ reusable scratch bits are physical registers. It satisfies $\mathcal U(|\psi\rangle\otimes|0^s\rangle)=U|\psi\rangle\otimes|0^s\rangle$ on every input to the original registers. With $P_{\mathrm{ready}}=|0^s\rangle\langle0^s|$, define $\Pi_h=P_h\otimes I_{\mathrm{neutral}}\otimes P_{\mathrm{ready}}$. Include the distinct data-exceptional projector $\Pi_{\mathrm{data\,fail}}=P_\perp\otimes I_{\mathrm{neutral}}\otimes P_{\mathrm{ready}}$ and scratch-failure projector $\Pi_{\mathrm{scratch\,fail}}=I\otimes(I-P_{\mathrm{ready}})$. These form a complete partition, and $\mathcal U$ commutes with every member: the ready subspace is invariant, its restriction is $U$, and unitarity preserves its orthogonal complement. This explicitly types arbitrary off-ready scratch inputs. Put $V_0=I$ and $V_j=U_j\cdots U_1$.

Add $L$ equal-frequency bosonic stage rails $a_0,\ldots,a_{L-1}$ with exactly one excitation. At edge $j\to j+1$ use $U_{j+1}$, with the last edge $L-1\to0$ carrying $U_L$. On the tensor code of the data and neutral registers, and the one-excitation stage sector, define the time-independent orthogonal projectors
$$
Q_h=\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}|j\rangle\langle j|\otimes V_j\Pi_hV_j^*.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.5}
$$
The physical source interaction for that edge is
$$
\begin{split}
F_j&=a_{j+1}-\widetilde U_{j+1}a_j,\\
H_j(t)&=H^0+\hbar g(t)F_j^*F_j,\qquad
g(t)=\frac{\pi}{2T}\,6\frac tT\left(1-\frac tT\right),
\quad0\le t\le T.
\end{split}
\tag{R.4.2.8e.6}
$$
Indices of stage rails are cyclic. $H^0$ is the sum of the register and stage free Hamiltonians (1). The bounded unitary $\widetilde U_{j+1}$ is the actual number-preserving extension of the compiler gate to its physical oscillator registers: local mode rotations and occupation phases give such extensions directly. It acts on the finite set of registers touched by that gate and is the coefficient of the explicitly admitted assisted hopping in (6). The model grants this coupling, with its displayed strength and duration. Each edge executes the assisted-hopping pulse itself; its fixed coefficient is specified by the compiler gate.

Every $H_j$ is nonnegative on the full oscillator Fock space, preserves each register's total occupation and the stage's total occupation, and has a unitary evolution. On the registered occupation sector it commutes with every $Q_h$ at every physical time. Assign all other occupation sectors to one additional source-failure block; extending $Q_h$ by zero there and extending the validity projector by their complement gives the corresponding full-Fock conserved projectors. The registered data-exceptional block in (R.4.2.8d.6) remains separately identified when occupations are valid.

The exact edge endpoint transfers the stage from $j$ to $j+1$ and applies $U_{j+1}$, including its complex phase. A full cycle returns the stage to $0$ and applies $\mathcal U$, hence $U$ on its ready-scratch domain. At that common ready/release boundary, $Q_h=\Pi_h$, whose restriction to ready scratch is the actual decoded data projector $P_h$. At intermediate times, the conserved physical charge is the fixed joint data--stage observable (5). It includes the control carrier that exchanges charge with the data during a compiled step.

For a finite catalogue, pad all words to a common $L$ and include a retained program register $p$. This register is held fixed while a word runs and is outside that word's workspace; it is included in the total rail, excitation and holding census. The per-word compiler can use the work-register bound, or the larger bound (7) obtained by including the idle program factor in $r,D$. Replace (5) by $\sum_p|p\rangle\langle p|\otimes Q_{p,h}$ and control each edge coefficient by $p$. This gives one fixed source observable and one physical carrier for the entire catalogue. Program selection or finite adaptive feedback takes place at stage $0$, where every $Q_{p,h}=\Pi_h$ after padding to the same ready-scratch carrier. All neutral record/program processing is confined to that stage and has its own finite control slots. Every controller slot leaves the stage-word scratch bits untouched. A compiled controller uses separate neutral workspace, on which the charge acts as the identity; its additional rails, ready excitations, gate time, action and retained records enter the controller ledger. Its workspace is never silently borrowed from the stage-word scratch whose readiness occurs in $\Pi_h$. The physical commutator holds also on coherent program inputs. Their response is defined by the registered joint controlled dilation, including its common environment basis and relative phases; equality of individual catalogue CP leaves alone would not determine those cross-program terms. A finite adaptive protocol selects from the registered catalogue only after each completed return; its full record-conditioned instruments then compose on the same carrier.

3. **Physical readout, source map and preparation.** A complete source-readiness occupation service runs initially and after each returned cycle. Its successful projector $R_0$ requires stage $0$, the declared one-excitation occupation in every register of the active cycle carrier, and zero stage-word scratch. It is diagonal in the source occupations; use the two complete Lüders leaves $R_0\rho R_0$ and $(I-R_0)\rho(I-R_0)$, with both outcomes and their conditional states retained. The service is stage-zero gated, so $R_0$ commutes with every fixed $Q_h$: at stage $0$ it is the ready-scratch/occupation projector, and on other stage positions it vanishes. A failed service aborts the next operation and retains its source-failure label. Only successful readiness permits an unqualified valid data-charge report. Each readiness service uses a fresh dual-rail flag. Let $S_F$ be the Hermitian unitary that swaps its two rails on the full Fock space. The explicitly admitted coarse-predicate control is
$$
H_{\mathrm{ready}}(t)=H^0+\hbar g(t)R_0\otimes(I-S_F),\qquad
U_{\mathrm{ready}}=(I-R_0)\otimes I+R_0\otimes S_F,
\tag{R.4.2.8e.6a}
$$
where $g$ is (6), and $H^0$ now includes that flag's free Hamiltonian. This nonnegative Hamiltonian produces the displayed endpoint in time $T$. Starting the flag in $0$ and applying its complete neutral occupation readout gives exactly the two stated Lüders leaves, with $1$ denoting successful readiness. The coarse-predicate coupling is a source premise; measuring individual data rails and discarding their labels would give a different instrument. Each check adds two flag rails, energy $\hbar\Omega$, one $T$ control slot, action at most $\pi\hbar$, absolute control work at most $3\pi\hbar/T$, and the positive pointer-service duration and apparatus charge. Old readiness flags remain retained neutral records, on which the source charge acts as the identity.

Occupation readout of each neutral pointer is likewise the complete projective service, with a positive registered duration, its finite apparatus charge and every failure outcome retained. Its apparatus acts on neutral pointer/environment factors and leaves the stage at $0$. For Definition 27, the registered arming predicate is completion of this scheduled service after the returned stage, and the perspective kernel is unit mass at one fixed registered label; the complete CP leaves and Born probabilities are those displayed here. Free evolution during this service preserves the code operators because every register has the same fixed one-excitation energy. The exact source-to-response map first uses the tensor isometries $S_q$, includes the actual stage and retained program, applies the displayed Hamiltonian history, and pairs the resulting state with the pointer projections and every declared later intervention. Tracing only permanently private factors gives the specified instrument. Consequently the map identifies the entire postmeasurement state and every finite allowed continuation, not just the outcome probabilities of one readout.

On the running stage, use the unitary $J=\sum_j|j\rangle\langle j|\otimes V_j^*$, then (R.4.2.8d.7) on its valid data factor. In particular a cross-stage matrix unit $|j\rangle\langle k|\otimes X$ maps to $|j\rangle\langle k|\otimes V_j^*XV_k$ before the decoder, so the map retains intermediate stage coherences. It satisfies $JQ_hJ^*=I_{\mathrm{stage}}\otimes\Pi_h$ and is the representation of the single fixed joint observable (5). Every neutral source operation intertwines this representation, and every charge projector commutes with the source dynamics. At the beginning and end of a complete operation it reduces to the same marked physical twenty-four-register decoder.

Preparing a charged initial code state is an explicitly separate input port before the conservation interval. For supplied logical $(h,b)$ inputs, eleven CNOTs form $m_i=h_i+b$ and eighty-four CNOTs write $mA$ into twelve ready zero registers. Thus at most ninety-five CNOTs, or $285$ elementary slots of Lemma R.4.2.8d.1, encode any supplied quantum state of the twelve logical bits. Source loading owns that state, all ready excitations and its preparation work. The conservation theorem begins after this preparation and never obtains an arbitrary charged input from neutral ready registers for free.

4. **A finite resource and perturbation record.** The following intentionally large bound makes every catalogue member finite. Let $r$ be the number of its original tensor registers, $D=\prod_{i=1}^rq_i$, and include all output, archive, pointer, environment and retained program factors among the $q_i$. Define
$$
\begin{split}
s&=2r-1,\qquad
L=\max\{2,B(D,r)\},\\
B(d,k)&=\left[\frac{d(d-1)}2+d\right](2k+1)(84k+4).
\end{split}
\tag{R.4.2.8e.7}
$$
Use the compiler with at most $s$ scratch bits, padding unused scratch by identities, and retain its exact gate list. Its ready-subspace property gives the full partition $\Pi_h,\Pi_{\mathrm{data\,fail}},\Pi_{\mathrm{scratch\,fail}}$ in item 2. With the list padded to length $L$, the stage implementation has
$$
\begin{split}
N_{\mathrm{rails}}&\le\sum_{i=1}^rq_i+2s+L,\qquad
E^0=(r+s+1)\hbar\Omega=3r\hbar\Omega,\\
t_{\mathrm{cycle}}&=LT,\qquad
\int\|H-H^0\|_{\mathrm{code}}dt\le\pi\hbar L,\\
\max_t\|H-H^0\|_{\mathrm{code}}&\le\frac{3\hbar\Omega}{4},\qquad
W_{\mathrm{abs}}\le\frac{3\pi\hbar L}{T}.
\end{split}
\tag{R.4.2.8e.8}
$$
Here $W_{\mathrm{abs}}=\int|\operatorname{Tr}\rho(t)\dot H(t)|dt$ bounds absolute work exchanged with the pulse actuator; the signed endpoint work of each ideal gate cycle is zero on the fixed-occupation branch. Initial excitation energy, pointer service, program preparation, finite classical control and holding earlier archives are additional named entries. For a finite run their energy-time holding is exactly $\hbar\Omega\sum_i t_i$ for the occupied register and stage factors, plus the apparatus's own registered holding charge. A scalar tariff prices this complete nonnegative resource vector, including the external pulse clock and detector. Each corrected interval supplies a fresh archive and retains the displaced state. Catalogue identity padding gives a common output deadline, including pointer service and control latency.

A concrete four-program catalogue uses the two noise leaves $C_0=v_0v_0^*$ and $C_1=v_1v_1^*$, where $v_0=(|0\rangle+i|e_1\rangle)/2$ and $v_1=(|e_2\rangle+|e_3\rangle)/2$ for the first three single-coordinate errors. Each outcome has probability $1/2$, with $R_C=2$. Take its noise, recovery, charge-readout and all-ones-gauge programmes on one carrier. Besides the twenty-four dual-rail data factors, its neutral register dimensions are $3,3,2326,2,2049,4$ for noise environment/outcome, recovery archive/validity, charge readout and program. Hence $r=30$, $D=5\,757\,103\,817\,883\,648$, $s=59$, and (7) fixes $L$ without an unspecified compiler size. The unitary carrier has $4553+L$ rails and energy $90\hbar\Omega$. One noise--recovery--charge trial uses three padded cycles, three program-selection slots, three complete pointer services and four source-readiness services (one initial and one after each cycle). The programme selections are local permutations on the four-level program factor and use no controller scratch. The four readiness flags add eight rails and $4\hbar\Omega$ to the occupied inventory, four $T$ control slots and their four action/work entries. All seven detector services' specified positive durations, apparatus charges and fresh records are added to the release clock and resource vector. With a common detector duration $t_{\mathrm m}>0$, this trial after source loading takes $(3L+7)T+7t_{\mathrm m}$; the preparation encoder adds $285T$ when that loading route is used. The gauge programme realizes the nontrivial element of $B$. This is an exact algebraic witness.

For initial trace distance $\delta_0$, integrated Hamiltonian error
$\epsilon_H=\hbar^{-1}\int\|\Delta H(t)\|_{\mathrm{code}}dt$
for ideal and perturbed drives that preserve the declared occupation sector, and complete-instrument errors $\eta_j$ in half diamond norm on the retained record--target carrier, every finite run obeys
$$
\tfrac12\|\widetilde\rho_{\mathrm{out}}-\rho_{\mathrm{out}}\|_1
\le\delta_0+\epsilon_H+\sum_j\eta_j.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.9}
$$
For adaptive services, $\eta_j$ is uniform over all retained histories at that slot. The same bound controls the complete transcript's total variation and the probability of leaving an initially definite charge sector. A separately measured occupation-leakage event is retained with its own error bound; an unrestricted full-Fock operator norm is not inferred from a code norm. Fixed nominal public clock labels require their own timing certificate when physical delays are perturbed.

*Proof.* The tensor code in (1) preserves the complete matrix algebra of each factor and their tensor product. In particular its forty-eight data rails retain the original twenty-four-bit coordinate support; the separate stage, environment and pointer rails do not change that support count. On the stated occupation sector $H^0$ is a scalar. Local mode rotations and occupation phases preserve each register occupation on the full Fock space, so their bounded unitary endpoints give the extensions required in (6).

For (2), $\sum_{ar}K_{ar}^*K_{ar}=P$ and the failure square is $I-P$, so $W_{\mathcal N}^*W_{\mathcal N}=I$. Orthogonal environment labels also verify the advertised marginal leaves directly. Theorem R.4.2.8d proves $K_{ar}P_h=P_hK_{ar}$, including the exceptional projector, so the isometry preserves every block. In (3), the unique leader decomposition makes the valid output vectors orthonormal; the failure flag separates them from the invalid outputs. The data component stays in its original block. Formula (4) has the same properties by the orthogonal-projector resolution of identity. In each finite block, complete an orthonormal basis of the ready-input image to an orthonormal basis of the full block. Mapping one completed basis to the other gives a unitary extension. Taking the direct sum over blocks proves the claimed commuting extension. Algebraic square roots, nullspaces and orthonormal completions give the algebraic-input procedure; degeneracies are handled by selecting an exact basis for each nullspace.

The compiler supplies at most $s=2r-1$ scratch bits and the count $B(D,r)$, with their exact coherent return on every original input. Its total word $\mathcal U$ preserves the ready-scratch subspace and has restriction $U$ there. Since it is unitary in finite dimension, that subspace reduces it. The complement, the ready data-exceptional block and each ready valid charge block therefore reduce $\mathcal U$. This proves commutation with every $\Pi$ in item 2 without assuming an action on unknown scratch inputs that the compiler did not establish.

Write $\Pi_{j,h}=V_j\Pi_hV_j^*$. For an internal edge, $\Pi_{j+1,h}U_{j+1}=U_{j+1}\Pi_{j,h}$. For the closing edge the same identity is
$$
\Pi_hU_L=U_LV_{L-1}\Pi_hV_{L-1}^*,
\tag{R.4.2.8e.10}
$$
which follows from $[U_LV_{L-1},\Pi_h]=0$. Thus the closure tests the complete endpoint; a charge-changing word cannot use the same closed charge frame. On its two active stage positions, $F_j^*F_j$ is the block operator
$$
A_j=
\begin{pmatrix}I&-U_{j+1}^*\\-U_{j+1}&I\end{pmatrix},
\qquad A_j^*=A_j,\quad A_j^2=2A_j.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.11}
$$
It vanishes on the other one-excitation stage positions. The intertwining identity proves $[A_j,Q_h]=0$, and the free term commutes as well. Its eigenvalues on the registered sector are zero and two. Since $\int_0^Tg(t)dt=\pi/2$ and the full-period free evolution is identity, its exact endpoint is
$$
\exp[-i(\pi/2)A_j]=I-A_j,
\qquad
|j\rangle|\psi\rangle\longmapsto|j+1\rangle U_{j+1}|\psi\rangle.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.12}
$$
The last formula includes its sign and scalar phase. Multiplication around the cycle proves the endpoint and stage return.

For full-Fock existence, decompose into the simultaneous total-occupation eigenspaces of all finitely many register and stage rail sets. Every such eigenspace is finite dimensional. The coefficient unitaries commute with those totals, $F_j^*F_j$ is nonnegative, and $H^0$ is nonnegative and scalar on each block. The direct sum of these positive finite matrices defines the self-adjoint source Hamiltonian on its natural domain. Finite-occupation vectors form an invariant core; the direct sum of their unitary evolutions extends to the full Hilbert space. This proves positivity and dynamics without extending a code norm to unbounded occupations. The registered occupation sector reduces all the Hamiltonians, so extending its good projectors by zero and its failure partition on the complement gives the stated global conservation law.

For a catalogue, a static program register gives orthogonal program blocks. In each block the preceding identities hold, including off-diagonal coherent program density-matrix entries because the same full operator commutator vanishes. At stage $0$, the charge partition is independent of $p$. A program/record gate restricted to that stage therefore commutes with the joint charge, and the stage condition is itself a conserved projector while the gate is applied. Such neutral control gates use the register pulses of Lemma R.4.2.8d.1, multiplied by the stage-zero projector, and act only on the program, neutral records and distinct neutral controller workspace. In particular no elementary controller gate acts on the stage-word scratch. The charge is the identity on the controller workspace, including its off-ready states; its own ready requirement belongs to the controller-response record. Positivity follows from the same positive local one-particle bound and positive occupation-phase bound: the stage-zero and complementary sectors reduce the pulse. Their durations, scratch, action and fresh records are charged as additional controller operations. Arbitrary program changes at running stages are outside this registered interface. Induction over completed cycles proves every finite adaptive history on the common source.

The readiness projector is supported at stage $0$ and agrees there with the full source-occupation/ready-scratch projector. Both it and its complement commute with every $Q_h$, proving the complete success/failure claim, including off-ready scratch and other stage positions. For (6a), $R_0$ is an orthogonal projector and $I-S_F$ is positive with eigenvalues zero and two; they act on distinct factors. Thus the added Hamiltonian is positive on full Fock and commutes with the source charge and free occupations. Its time integral yields the displayed controlled swap, including its phase, so neutral flag readout gives the two complete Lüders leaves. On an exact registered run its successful leaf is the identity. The physical pointer service then acts at stage $0$ and commutes with the data--scratch charge partition. Copying its orthogonal outcome into a fresh neutral archive, if required by the retained service record, also commutes. Its complete instrument gives precisely the CP leaves in (2)--(4), with any source-occupation failure retained separately. Every allowed future operation uses the same source embedding and access cut; composing their maps proves complete finite response equivalence, including conditional quantum states, spectator correlations, release labels and the full private-record ownership. The source's coordinate-class readout is therefore the one in Theorem R.4.2.8d. Equation (5) gives the corresponding physical intermediate-stage charge rather than asserting that the bare data class remains fixed during a control pulse.

The encoder count follows directly from (R.4.2.8d.1): each row of $A$ contains seven ones, giving $84$ target CNOTs, preceded by the eleven gauge additions. Each CNOT uses the three elementary slots stated in Lemma R.4.2.8d.1. The supplied unknown logical state is transported isometrically, and all loading factors are present before the conserved running interval.

For the resource bounds, each original register contributes $q_i$ rails and one excitation, each scratch bit contributes two rails and one excitation, and the stage contributes $L$ rails and one excitation. This proves both the mode count and $E^0$ in (8). The common pulse has
$$
\int_0^Tg=\frac\pi2,\qquad
\max g=\frac{3\pi}{4T},\qquad
\int_0^T|\dot g|=\frac{3\pi}{2T}.
\tag{R.4.2.8e.13}
$$
Together with $\|A_j\|=2$, these give the action, peak and absolute-work bounds in (8). On a single pulse $\langle A_j\rangle$ is constant, because both terms in the Hamiltonian commute with $A_j$; since $g$ vanishes at both endpoints its signed work is zero. At the next pulse the same statement holds for its new edge. Preparation supplies the finite nonzero energy inventory of $H^0$, and old archives keep their holding of their $H^0$ energy until the declared end of the run. Pointer and controller service charges are added by their actual counted calls and durations. Thus zero signed gate work is never used as a zero total resource claim.

Finally, on the invariant finite sector the Duhamel identity gives $\|\widetilde U-U\|\le\hbar^{-1}\int\|\Delta H(t)\|dt$. For any state and spectator, the trace distance between the corresponding unitary outputs is at most this operator norm. Trace-distance contractivity and a telescoping sum over complete instruments prove (9); the uniform retained-history error premise makes the same argument valid for adaptive control. Measurement gives the total-variation bound. The ideal state has support in the starting conserved sector, so testing its complement gives the same bound on the actual leakage probability. Occupation leakage and timing effects outside this norm's declared carrier require their separately retained terms, exactly as stated. ∎

**Metadata.** Exact domain: the explicit marked code (R.4.2.8d.1), the complex (R.4.2.8d.2), every positive coefficient family (R.4.2.8d.9), every finite catalogue and adaptive recovery history in Proposition R.4.2.8e, with its declared source/readout services. Premises: Definitions R.4.2.8a and 27, the registered coordinate access cut, the positive Hamiltonians (R.4.2.8e.6) and (R.4.2.8e.6a), fresh ready registers and complete retained records. Equivalence: equality of the complete finite instruments and their future declared responses under the tensor, decoder and stage embeddings; source gauge includes common private unitary changes of Kraus coordinates with the access cut transformed consistently. For coherent program controls, the joint dilation and its cross-program terms are part of the source record; independently changing one branch's environment gauge is not automatically response equivalence. Budget: all code states, all correctable coefficient matrices, all spectators, every retained outcome and every finite registered history, with the exact mode/time/action/work/holding vector and error bound in (R.4.2.8e.7)--(R.4.2.8e.9). Verifier: the displayed finite code recurrence, unique leader algorithm, matrix-unit recovery identity, blockwise dilation, cyclic intertwining and positive-square Hamiltonian. Falsifier: a nonzero cross-charge matrix element of an admitted Kraus operator, a failure of an edge commutator or complete CP leaf, or a missing declared record/resource. Nonvacuity: each of the $2048$ valid charges, the nontrivial all-ones gauge, all $2325$ one-window bit errors, and their arbitrary positive coherent coefficient families occur on the same marked carrier. The verified systematic code marking is imported explicitly, and its required weight/distance facts are independently derived by (R.4.2.8d.13). The physical charge supplied is this registered encoded Abelian charge; any identification with a separately specified particle charge or physical Leech vacuum requires its own source identification.

### R.4.2.8f Faithful Sporadic Actions on the Existing Coherent Error Factor

<a id="theorem-r428f-faithful-same-source-sporadic-observable-action"></a>**Theorem R.4.2.8f (Faithful Same-Source Sporadic Observable Action).** Retain exactly the marked code, twenty-four data registers, complete weight-at-most-three coherent error class, charge/validity projectors, decoder, and positive source of Theorem R.4.2.8d and Proposition R.4.2.8e. Let \(G=\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_K)=\mathrm{Co}_0\) in the original marked real coordinates. Identify the existing error basis \(\mathbb C[\mathcal E]\) with \(\bigoplus_{k=0}^3\Lambda^k\mathbb C^{24}\) by sending an increasing error support to its increasing wedge basis vector. Define

\[
R(g)=\bigoplus_{k=0}^3\Lambda^k g,\qquad
R(g)_{F,E}=\begin{cases}\det g_{F,E},&|F|=|E|,\\0,&|F|\ne|E|,\end{cases}
\qquad
U(g)=D^*(I_K\otimes R(g))D+P_\perp.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.1}
\]

The empty minor is one. This is an exact unitary representation on the same full physical data space. It fixes every encoded logical state pointwise, commutes with every original \(P_h,P_\perp\) and the all-ones gauge translation, and satisfies

\[
U(g)|c+e_i\rangle=\sum_jg_{ji}|c+e_j\rangle.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.2}
\]

Thus its degree-one restriction is the original marked 24-dimensional mode action on actual single-coordinate errors. It is faithful as an observable conjugation action. For every complete original coefficient family \(C_a\),

\[
\operatorname{Ad}_{U(g)}\circ\mathcal N_{C,a}
=\mathcal N_{R(g)C_aR(g)^*,a}\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{U(g)},
\tag{R.4.2.8f.3}
\]

including the ready-failure leaf and all spectator extensions. The original recovery isometry intertwines it with the retained archive action \(R(g)\oplus1_{\rm fail}\), leaving the recovered logical carrier unchanged. This is covariance of the complete admitted noise family; it does not assert invariance of each individual anisotropic instrument, or treat a general coherent syndrome projector as a relabeling of individual error outcomes.

Every new group action, matrix/lattice probe, and projective readout in this construction is neutral for the original charge partition and has algebraic coefficients. The original complete noise class still allows arbitrary real or complex coefficient records: its all-real finite-source existence statement is unchanged, while effective finite exact synthesis retains the original algebraic-input scope. Proposition R.4.2.8e therefore supplies the all-neutral finite-instrument realization, positive assisted-hopping source, source map, scratch return, stage/closing-edge identities, complete readiness records, and resource bounds for each finite registered catalogue at those same coefficient scopes. No different data carrier, smaller noise class, or uncharged auxiliary reference is substituted.

*Proof.* Cauchy–Binet proves the group law and orthogonality of each exterior power. The decoder is the original unitary identification of the good subspace, so its pullback and the identity on the exceptional complement give the representation on the full data space. Its degree-zero block is one, and it leaves the codeword factor fixed, proving all charge and gauge claims. The degree-one block gives (R.4.2.8f.2). The identities
\(U(g)V_e=\sum_fR(g)_{fe}V_f\) and \(V_eU(g)=V_e\) give (R.4.2.8f.3), including normalization and positivity of all transformed coefficients. Commutation with \(P\) supplies the failure-leaf identity. Applying the original recovery formula to each good basis vector transfers exactly \(R(g)\) to its archive; both sides are the identity on the invalid input and fail label. These are complete isometry identities, so they retain postmeasurement states, spectators, and coherent archive records. If \(U(g)\) is scalar, its identity action on the code forces the scalar to be one and (R.4.2.8f.2) forces \(g=I\). The neutral physical probes below establish faithfulness through admitted observations as well. Effective algebraic exact synthesis for the new endpoints, and the original all-real existence assertion for arbitrary admitted coefficient catalogues, follow from the original compiler at its respective stated scopes. ∎

**Representation boundary.** The marked coordinate permutation subgroup \(M_{24}\subset G\) has the signed restriction

\[
U(P_\pi)|c+e_E\rangle=s_\pi(E)|c+e_{\pi E}\rangle,
\]

where \(s_\pi(E)\) is the parity of reordering the permuted error support. This is not the natural unsigned qubit-coordinate action, which sends \(|c+e_E\rangle\) to \(|\pi c+e_{\pi E}\rangle\). The former fixes every codeword; the latter generally permutes them. The exact source proof and finite witness below supply a marked Golay automorphism fixing an error pair with exterior sign −1, and a codeword that the natural coordinate action moves. Thus no identification of the two actions, or extension of the natural whole-code action to Co0, is asserted. The action here meets the existential faithful-action requirement by its declared syndrome representation and its exact marked single-error mode intertwiner.

<a id="theorem-r428g-complete-physical-matrix-and-orbit-signatures"></a>**Theorem R.4.2.8g (Complete Physical Matrix and Orbit Signatures).** On the same source, prepare the complete original error instrument

\[
K_i=(P+iX_iP)/\sqrt2,\quad K_{\rm fail}=I-P,
\]

and measure the complete three-outcome spectral instrument of
\(Y_j=i(|e_j\rangle\langle0|-|0\rangle\langle e_j|)\), pulled back through the decoder and set to zero on the exceptional complement. After \(U(g)\) its probabilities are

\[
p_\pm=\frac{(1\pm g_{ji})^2}{4},\qquad
p_0=\frac{1-g_{ji}^2}{2},\qquad
\mathbb E Y_j=g_{ji}.
\tag{R.4.2.8g.1}
\]

The 576 means are therefore an injective signature of the complete marked group action. In particular the diagonal probe has deterministic opposite outcomes for \(I\) and \(-I\). The required reference is the original no-error syndrome, coherently prepared by the displayed complete instrument; all its physical costs are charged.

For any rational \(R\ge0\), let \(\Omega_R=\{x\in\Lambda_K:\|x\|^2\le R\}\), exhaustively generated from the original coordinate-lattice congruences. Its exact orbit signature

\[
\kappa_G(x)=\min_{g\in G}^{\rm lex}gx
\tag{R.4.2.8g.2}
\]

is invariant and separates the group orbits. The same construction for the marked permutation subgroup separates its M24 orbits without identifying them with Co0 orbits. The finite exact group census is obtained by enumerating lattice images of an integer lattice basis with its same Gram matrix and testing integral unimodular basis transport; it is an algorithmic construction, not a claim that the entire census was practically executed.

Prepare the complete original rank-one error instrument with syndrome vector

\[
v(x)=\frac{|0\rangle+i\sum_jx_j|e_j\rangle}{\sqrt{1+\|x\|^2}},
\]

and its original ready-failure leaf. Measure the syndrome-vacuum projector and the 24 complete \(Y_j\) instruments. Their means are

\[
f_0(x)=\frac1{1+\|x\|^2},\qquad
f_j(x)=\frac{2x_j}{1+\|x\|^2},\qquad x_j=\frac{f_j}{2f_0}.
\tag{R.4.2.8g.3}
\]

This physical response is injective, including magnitude and sign. Distinct candidates in the window have mean-vector separation at least \(8/(8+\lfloor8R\rfloor)^2\). The complete ternary probabilities, exact covariance map under independent fresh trials, calibrated source-error transport, source/resource ownership, and exhaustive nonvacuous norm-four witness are given in the complete proof record below. They use the unchanged encoder and complete original noise/recovery services on the same source.

*Proof.* The accepted identity \(V_e^*V_f=\delta_{ef}P\) proves normalization of each prepared probe, including its failure complement. The two eigenvectors \((|0\rangle\pm i|e_j\rangle)/\sqrt2\) give (R.4.2.8g.1) and (R.4.2.8g.3). The means expose the center because the no-error and single-error components transform with opposite central signs, while retaining equal physical oscillator occupation. Finite group orbits have equal lexicographic minima precisely when they intersect. Write a lattice vector as \(x=n/\sqrt8\). Different squared integer norms separate the vacuum means by at least \(8/(8+\lfloor8R\rfloor)^2\); equal norms and different integer coordinates separate a Y mean by at least \(4\sqrt2/(8+\lfloor8R\rfloor)\). This proves the stated conservative gap. All readout projectors are neutral, so their complete instruments have the same physical synthesis and retained-record guarantees as the action. ∎

<a id="definition-r428h-total-calibrated-orbit-extractor"></a>**Definition R.4.2.8h (Total Calibrated Orbit Extractor).** Before inspecting outcome data, register the exact source hashes, group and norm window, candidate/prediction table, covariance and complete-instrument calibration bounds, fixed independent fresh-trial schedule, confidence level \(\alpha\), and extraction rule. Each failed trial aborts its remaining source operations and has predefined statistic zero; it is never discarded or replaced. All raw flags and outcomes are retained. The final output is `SOURCE_FAILURE` if any required failure/missing-record flag occurs.

For the unconditional 25-setting raw sample means with \(n\) trials per setting, uniform unconditional mean-bias bound \(b\), and

\[
r_n=\sqrt{2\log(50/\alpha)/n},\quad
\mathcal C=\{x\in\Omega_R:\|\widehat f-f(x)\|_\infty\le r_n+b\},
\]

return, on failure-free complete records, `FALSIFIED_AT_REGISTERED_CONFIDENCE` if \(\mathcal C\) is empty, the unique compatible orbit signature if all candidates share it, and `INCONCLUSIVE` otherwise. Hoeffding and the union bound apply to unconditional complete trial records, not to a postselected successful sample. The probability of wrong certification or false rejection is at most \(\alpha\). If \(r_n+b\) is smaller than half the candidate separation, correct singleton delivery has probability at least \(1-\alpha-\beta_{\rm fail}\), where \(\beta_{\rm fail}\) is a separate bound on any required failure event. The exact ideal source has \(\beta_{\rm fail}=0\). A bound on mean bias alone gives no high delivery probability. The analogous matrix-action test uses 576 settings and its exact rational matrix separation. This is a finite-sample confidence extractor, not perfect single-shot tomography.

#### Complete existing source, representation and uncertainty proof record

The following plain equation numbers 1–32 and theorem numbers 1–3 are local to this proof record; their native tags carry the prefix R.4.2.8f.PR. All original code, noise, charge, compiler, record and resource premises remain those of R.4.2.8d–e.

##### 1. Sources, immutable inputs, and preserved contract

The canonical inputs are the marked Golay and coordinate Leech constructions in Appendix R, the source/instrument and physical rail realization of Theorem R.4.2.8d and Proposition R.4.2.8e, and the action, complete observable signatures and extractor of Theorems R.4.2.8f–R.4.2.8g and Definition R.4.2.8h. The source group identification \(\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_K)=\mathrm{Co}_0\) and \(\operatorname{Aut}(K)=M_{24}\) is consumed at its accepted scope. No new experimental evidence, claim about spacetime, physical Leech vacuum, optimal resource use, arbitrary quantum noise, or unsigned representation extension is imported.

Keep the original data space \(\mathcal H_D=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes24}\), the exact systematic Golay matrix of R.4.2.8d.1, code \(K\), code projector \(P\), error set \(\mathcal E=\{e:|e|\le3\}\), operators \(V_e=X_eP\), good-space projector \(Q=\sum_hP_h\), exceptional projector \(P_\perp=I-Q\), and decoder

\[
D|c+e\rangle=|c\rangle|e\rangle.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.1}
\]

The syndrome \(e\) is the unique radius-three leader, not a newly chosen label. The physical embedding remains \(S_2^{\otimes24}\) into the same forty-eight data rails. No tensor factor is substituted for any coordinate. Original neutral archives, controls, detector services, readiness checks, and failure leaves remain owned by the source.

The full original instrument class remains

\[
\mathcal N_{C,a}(\rho)=\sum_{e,f}(C_a)_{ef}V_e\rho V_f^*,\qquad
C_a\succeq0,\quad\sum_a\operatorname{Tr}C_a=1,
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.2}
\]

with the separate leaf \((I-P)\rho(I-P)\). Arbitrary spectators, retained outcomes, coherent syndrome correlations, and the recover-before-next-error-window schedule remain in force.

##### 2. The marked finite group and an effective exact census

Let \(\Lambda_K\) be the *same* source lattice, with

\[
\sqrt8\Lambda_K=N=2K_{\mathbb Z}\;\cup\;(v+2K_{\mathbb Z}),\quad
K_{\mathbb Z}=\{x\in\mathbb Z^{24}:x\bmod2\in K,\ \sum_i x_i\equiv0\pmod4\},
\quad v=(-3,1,\ldots,1).
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.3}
\]

The notation \(2K_{\mathbb Z}\) denotes a lattice, not the binary code. A finite integer generating list for \(N\) is

\[
2g_0,\ldots,2g_{11},\quad
4(e_i-e_{23})\ (0\le i<23),\quad8e_{23},\quad v,
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.4}
\]

where each binary row \(g_j\) is now its integer 0/1 representative. To verify generation, subtract binary generator rows successively from any \(x\in K_{\mathbb Z}\). The residual is \(2z\), with even coordinate sum for \(z\), because each row has weight eight. The displayed difference vectors and \(2e_{23}\) generate that even-sum integer lattice. Multiplying by two and adjoining \(v\) gives (4).

A Hermite-normal-form basis \(B\in\mathbb Z^{24\times24}\) of (4) is an effective exact finite object. The accepted covolume-one property of \(\Lambda_K\) gives

\[
|\det B|=(\sqrt8)^{24}=2^{36}=:d.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.5}
\]

Here and below all group matrices act on column vectors in the original marked coordinate order. Define

\[
G=\{g\in O(24):gN=N\}=\mathrm{Co}_0.
\]

This is not an oracle-defined input: a finite exhaustive procedure is as follows. For each basis column \(b_j\), enumerate integer vectors \(n_j\in N\) of norm \(\|b_j\|\), using the bounded integer coordinate box and the explicit membership test (3). Enumerate tuples \((n_1,\ldots,n_{24})\) having Gram matrix \(B^TB\). For each tuple put \(g=[n_1\cdots n_{24}]B^{-1}\), and test that \(B^{-1}[n_1\cdots n_{24}]\) has integer entries. Gram equality gives determinant \(\pm1\); hence an accepted tuple maps the lattice onto itself. Conversely every automorphism occurs in this enumeration. Distinct tuples give distinct matrices. This proves a finite exact group census, including a computable multiplication table if requested, without claiming a practical enumeration of all \(8{,}315{,}553{,}613{,}086{,}720{,}000\) elements was run.

Every matrix entry lies in \(d^{-1}\mathbb Z\). Thus distinct group matrices satisfy

\[
\max_{i,j}|g_{ij}-g'_{ij}|\ge d^{-1}=2^{-36}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.6}
\]

The bound is intentionally loose. It follows directly from the original marked lattice rather than an unverified small-denominator assertion.

For \(\pi\in\operatorname{Aut}(K)\), let \(P_\pi e_i=e_{\pi(i)}\). It preserves \(K_{\mathbb Z}\). Also \(P_\pi v-v=4(e_0-e_{\pi(0)})\in2K_{\mathbb Z}\), so it preserves the same glue coset. Hence these *exact marked* permutation matrices embed \(M_{24}\) in \(G\).

##### 3. Same-source action, full faithfulness, and noise/recovery identities

###### Theorem R.4.2.8f.1. Coherent error-factor action

Identify the error basis, using increasing coordinate order, with the exterior basis

\[
T|e_{\{i_1<\cdots<i_k\}}\rangle=e_{i_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{i_k},\quad
0\le k\le3.
\]

Define

\[
R(g)=T^{-1}\left(\bigoplus_{k=0}^3\Lambda^k g\right)T,
\qquad
R(g)_{F,E}=\begin{cases}\det(g_{F,E}),&|F|=|E|\le3,\\0,&|F|\ne|E|.\end{cases}
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.7}
\]

The empty minor is one. Define on the *full original data space*

\[
U(g)=D^*(I_{\mathbb C[K]}\otimes R(g))D+P_\perp.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.8}
\]

Then:

1. \(U(g)\) is an exact unitary representation. It fixes \(P\mathcal H_D\) pointwise and commutes with every \(P_h,P_\perp\), and with the all-ones gauge translation.
2. For each actual codeword \(c\), the single-error coordinate map
   \(J_c e_i=|c+e_i\rangle\) satisfies
   \[
   U(g)J_c=J_cg.
   \tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.9}
   \]
   Thus the original marked 24-dimensional real action is response-faithfully realized on actual single-coordinate error amplitudes of every code state.
3. The induced action on observables by conjugation is faithful, including the central element.
4. For every complete original instrument (2), with
   \(C'_a=R(g)C_aR(g)^*\),
   \[
   \operatorname{Ad}_{U(g)}\circ\mathcal N_{C,a}
   =\mathcal N_{C',a}\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{U(g)}
   \tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.10}
   \]
   on every input, including arbitrary spectators. The corresponding equality holds for the complete ready-failure leaf.
5. The original recovery isometry obeys, on *all ready inputs*,
   \[
   W_{\rm rec}U(g)=(I_D\otimes\overline R(g)_E\otimes I_F)W_{\rm rec},
   \quad \overline R(g)=R(g)\oplus1_{\rm fail}.
   \tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.11}
   \]
   All syndrome and invalid records are retained. Tracing the archive restores the same logical channel; leaving it accessible retains the actual transformed quantum syndrome state.

*Proof.* Cauchy–Binet proves \(R(gh)=R(g)R(h)\), and orthogonality of \(g\) proves unitarity of each exterior power. Direct sum with the identity on the orthogonal exceptional sector proves the first representation assertion. The degree-zero error state is fixed. The action does not change \(c\), and hence it preserves \(h=[c]\), the all-ones gauge relation, and the invalid sector. Equation (9) is the degree-one block of (7).

If \(\operatorname{Ad}_{U(g)}\) is the identity on the full data algebra, \(U(g)\) is scalar. Its restriction to the nonzero code space is the identity, so the scalar is one. Equation (9) then implies \(g=I\). More operationally, Section 5 supplies charge-neutral preparation and readout tests distinguishing every nonidentity element; this prevents the argument from relying on inaccessible observables.

For each \(e\),

\[
U(g)V_e=\sum_fR(g)_{fe}V_f,\qquad V_eU(g)=V_e.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.12}
\]

These equalities prove (10). Positivity and trace normalization of every \(C'_a\) follow by unitary conjugation. Since \([U(g),P]=0\), the ready-failure leaf is covariant as well. This is covariance of the entire original instrument family; it is not a claim that every individual anisotropic \(C_a\) is invariant. An individual noise instrument is invariant precisely when its complete family of \(C_a\) satisfies the relevant conjugation identities, with any authorized outcome relabeling stated separately.

On a good basis vector, the two sides of (11) equal
\(\sum_fR(g)_{fe}|c\rangle|f\rangle|0\rangle\). On an exceptional basis vector both sides retain that vector and the same fail archive/validity label. Linearity, including tensoring a spectator, proves the complete isometry identity. ∎

**What is and is not covariant.** Reading a particular original error label after the transformation measures \(R(g)^*|e\rangle\langle e|R(g)\) in the original syndrome coordinates. For a general Conway element it is a coherent projector, not a permutation of old labels. Equation (11) retains this full instrument and its postmeasurement state. Neither discarding the archive nor matching only the recovered logical state is used to claim equality of full retained responses. A selected Kraus factorization is transported using the same environment basis and relative phases; arbitrary independent changes of Kraus gauge in coherent program branches are not introduced.

The encoder intertwiner is \(U(g)\operatorname{Enc}=\operatorname{Enc}\), with the identity representation on supplied logical/gauge inputs. The action therefore coexists with the exact accepted encoder without modifying its definition or pretending it has the natural coordinate-permutation representation on logical messages.

##### 4. The M24 restriction and its exact compatibility boundary

For a marked coordinate permutation,

\[
R(P_\pi)|E\rangle=s_\pi(E)|\pi E\rangle,
\quad
s_\pi(E)=(-1)^{\#\{i<j\text{ in }E:\pi(i)>\pi(j)\}}.
