\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.13}
\]

Hence the action in Theorem R.4.2.8f.1 is

\[
U(P_\pi)|c+e_E\rangle=s_\pi(E)|c+e_{\pi E}\rangle.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.14}
\]

The natural unsigned qubit-coordinate action \(A_\pi|w\rangle=|\pi w\rangle\) instead satisfies

\[
D A_\pi D^*=L_\pi\otimes T_\pi,
\quad L_\pi|c\rangle=|\pi c\rangle,
\quad T_\pi|E\rangle=|\pi E\rangle.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.15}
\]

Both are faithful M24 actions on the actual carrier, but they are different actions, with different noise coefficient transports. Equation (15) preserves the same ECC family and permutes charge labels; (14) preserves each charge label. Only (14) is the restriction of (8).

This is a substantive, exactly verified difference. In zero-based coordinates the verifier supplies

\[
\pi=(0\ 3)(1\ 2)(4\ 7)(5\ 14)(6\ 9)(8\ 15)
(10\ 12)(11\ 22)(13\ 18)(16\ 19)(17\ 23)(20\ 21).
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.16}
\]

It checks \(\pi K=K\) on all 4096 codewords. The error support \(E=\{0,3\}\) is fixed setwise; (14) gives phase \(-1\), while (15) gives phase \(+1\) when \(c=0\). Moreover the codeword with support

\[
\{1,2,3,4,5,8,11,13\}
\]

is sent by (15) to the different support

\[
\{0,1,2,7,14,15,18,22\},
\]

while (14) fixes it. Thus no diagonal rephasing of the error-support basis removes the sign discrepancy: a rephasing cannot change the eigenvalue on the fixed support \(\{0,3\}\). This rules out the tempting claim that the two actions differ merely by harmless labels or diagonal phases. No claim about all possible non-diagonal intertwiners or all possible Co0 extensions follows.

The original mode interpretation is nevertheless explicit: on every \(J_c\mathbb C^{24}\) the subgroup acts by the actual marked permutation of *single-error coordinates*, exactly as (9) requires, with no sign there. The higher-degree representation choice is now a declared physical control, not a covert change to the code, its encoder, or its noise class.

##### 5. Complete physical probes and observable faithfulness

###### Theorem R.4.2.8f.2. Exact complete matrix-response signature

For input coordinate \(i\), use the original admitted rank-one coefficient record

\[
v_i=(|\varnothing\rangle+i|\{i\}\rangle)/\sqrt2,\qquad C_i=v_iv_i^*,
\quad K_i=(P+iX_iP)/\sqrt2.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.17}
\]

Include the original ready-failure Kraus operator \(I-P\). Since \(PX_iP=0\),

\[
K_i^*K_i=P,\qquad K_i^*K_i+(I-P)=I.
\]

This is a complete legitimate instrument. The unprojected expression \((I+X_i)/\sqrt2\) is not used. For an arbitrary code input, including entanglement with a spectator, the decoded output of its success leaf is exactly the original code/spectator state tensored with \(|v_i\rangle\langle v_i|\). Its code-input success probability is one. Off-code failure remains a separate retained outcome.

On the syndrome factor set

\[
Y_j=i(|\{j\}\rangle\langle\varnothing|-|\varnothing\rangle\langle\{j\}|).
\]

Pull this observable back through \(D\), acting as zero on the exceptional sector. Its complete spectral measurement has outcomes \(+1,-1,0\) with projectors

\[
E_{j,\pm}=D^*\left(I_K\otimes\frac{P_0+P_j\pm Y_j}{2}\right)D,
\quad E_{j,0}=I-E_{j,+}-E_{j,-}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.18}
\]

All three are orthogonal projectors, sum to identity on the full data space, and commute with every existing charge/validity projector. The zero outcome includes all other error degrees and the exceptional sector; source and data-validity failures remain separately flagged by their already retained services. The actual instrument is the complete Lüders family \(E_{j,a}\rho E_{j,a}\), not merely a list of outcome probabilities.

After (17) and \(U(g)\),

\[
\Pr(\pm1\mid i,j,g)=\frac{(1\pm g_{ji})^2}{4},\quad
\Pr(0\mid i,j,g)=\frac{1-g_{ji}^2}{2},
\quad \mathbb E[Y_j]=g_{ji}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.19}
\]

Consequently the 576-entry vector of expectations is precisely the marked matrix \(g\). This is a complete response signature of the group action. It distinguishes every two elements, not only conjugacy classes.

For \(g=I\) the \((i,i)\) probe yields \(+1\) with certainty; for \(g=-I\) it yields \(-1\) with certainty. The no-error component has eigenvalue +1 and the single-error component eigenvalue −1 under the central element. All probes remain in the same original charge block. No superselection rule between these degrees is added: they are logical error weights in the original dual-rail code, whose physical total oscillator occupation is the same. This is not a physical fermion-parity assertion.

For independent fresh trials at each setting, the ideal sample-mean covariance is diagonal across settings and

\[
\operatorname{Var}(\widehat g_{ji})=\frac{1-g_{ji}^2}{2n}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.20}
\]

Independence is a registered source/trial premise; it is not inferred from one-shot channel normalization.

##### 6. Actual Leech-orbit signatures and a total uncertainty-aware extractor

###### Theorem R.4.2.8f.3. Exact orbit invariant and physical response map

Before inspecting outcome data, choose a rational squared-norm cutoff \(R\ge0\) and the exact finite source-lattice window

\[
\Omega_R=\{x\in\Lambda_K:\|x\|^2\le R\}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.21}
\]

This is generated by enumerating integer numerators in (3) with \(\sum n_i^2\le8R\), so the candidate window is exhaustive. It is invariant under \(G\). Define

\[
\kappa_G(x)=\min_{g\in G}^{\rm lex}gx.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.22}
\]

Exact algebraic comparisons make this a finite algorithm. It is invariant and complete:

\[
\kappa_G(x)=\kappa_G(y)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad y\in Gx.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.23}
\]

The same construction with the marked permutation subgroup gives \(\kappa_{M_{24}}\); the two orbit equivalences are not conflated.

For any \(x\in\Omega_R\), prepare with the *same original error instrument class*

\[
v(x)=\frac{|\varnothing\rangle+i\sum_jx_j|\{j\}\rangle}{\sqrt{1+\|x\|^2}},
\quad C(x)=|v(x)\rangle\langle v(x)|,
\quad K(x)=\frac{P+i\sum_jx_jX_jP}{\sqrt{1+\|x\|^2}}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.24}
\]

The accepted matrix-unit identity gives \(K(x)^*K(x)=P\), so adjoining \(I-P\) completes it exactly. This does not assume that the full-carrier polynomial without its code projector is unitary. After the action the prepared factor is \(v(gx)\).

In addition to (18), measure the complete binary syndrome-vacuum projector \(P_0\), again pulled back and with its full complement retained. The 25 exact means are

\[
f_0(x)=\frac1{1+\|x\|^2},\qquad
f_j(x)=\frac{2x_j}{1+\|x\|^2},\quad1\le j\le24.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.25}
\]

This response map is injective on all of \(\mathbb R^{24}\): \(x_j=f_j/(2f_0)\), and \(f_0>0\). The physical reference therefore retains both direction and magnitude, including the sign of \(x\). The nonlinear denominator does not silently quotient the lattice by arbitrary scaling.

For the ternary \(Y_j\) measurement,

\[
p_\pm(x)=\frac{(1\pm x_j)^2}{2(1+\|x\|^2)},\qquad
p_0(x)=\frac{\|x\|^2-x_j^2}{1+\|x\|^2},
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.26}
\]

and these are nonnegative and sum to one. Its single-trial variance is

\[
\frac{1+x_j^2}{1+\|x\|^2}-f_j(x)^2.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.27}
\]

The vacuum Bernoulli variance is \(f_0(1-f_0)\). With independent blocks of \(n\) trials per setting these variances divided by \(n\), and zero interblock covariances, give the calibrated covariance matrix. If trials have registered correlations, their actual covariance bounds must replace this independence formula; the extractor cannot silently use (27) as an iid certificate.

Let \(L=\lfloor8R\rfloor\). For distinct \(x=n/\sqrt8\), \(x'=n'/\sqrt8\) in the window, their squared integer norms either differ or agree. In the first case their vacuum means differ by at least \(8/(8+L)^2\). In the second case one integer coordinate differs, so the corresponding Y mean differs by at least \(4\sqrt2/(8+L)\). Hence the uniformly explicit separation

\[
\|f(x)-f(x')\|_\infty\ge\delta_R:=\frac8{(8+L)^2}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.28}
\]

If the window is a singleton, uniqueness is automatic and (28) is simply an unused safe threshold. Equation (23) follows because intersecting finite group orbits are equal. All claimed statements hold for every finite rational cutoff; choosing a cutoff is part of a finite experiment, not a claim that the entire unbounded lattice has been physically enumerated. ∎

###### Definition R.4.2.8f.4. Pre-registered total extraction and falsification rule

The empirical-input schema contains:

1. immutable source/marking hashes and the chosen group (Co0 or marked M24);
2. \(R\), the generated list (21), the prediction table (25)–(27), and the exact orbit map (22);
3. confidence level \(0<\alpha<1\), repetitions \(n\), complete service/calibration error bounds, and declared independent trial preparation;
4. every raw outcome, zero outcome, readiness/invalid flag, setting, public clock label, and retained record identifier;
5. the deterministic rule below, fixed before reading outcomes.

Use a pre-fixed schedule of fresh independent trials, including each trial’s complete success/failure record. A failed trial aborts its remaining source operations under the original rule, records the failure, and is assigned statistic zero for that setting. Later scheduled trials use independently loaded source copies; no failed run is replaced, discarded, or resampled. Pad public release times to the pre-registered deadlines where the source contract requires this. Missing or malformed records cause the final result **SOURCE_FAILURE**, never a certificate. For probabilistic claims the registered complete-record model must cover these cases, with a preassigned zero statistic as well.

Thus a bounded raw statistic is defined for every one of the pre-fixed trials, even when a service failed. Let \(\widehat f\) be the 25 means of these **unconditional** raw statistics. The independence premise applies to complete trial records, and the systematic bound \(|\mathbb E\widehat f_s-f_s(x)|\le b\) also concerns these unconditional means. No Hoeffding statement is conditioned on all trials succeeding. With outcomes in \([-1,1]\), Hoeffding and a union bound give

\[
r_n=\sqrt{\frac2n\log\frac{50}{\alpha}},\qquad
\Pr\{\|\widehat f-f(x)\|_\infty>r_n+b\}\le\alpha.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.29}
\]

Define the finite compatible set

\[
\mathcal C=\{x\in\Omega_R:\|\widehat f-f(x)\|_\infty\le r_n+b\}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.30}
\]

The output is total:

- **SOURCE_FAILURE** if any required readiness/validity service failed or a required record is missing/malformed. Return all available records. The following three outputs apply only when no such flag is present; their confidence is established from the unconditional raw-statistic event above.
- **FALSIFIED_AT_REGISTERED_CONFIDENCE** if \(\mathcal C=\varnothing\). This rejects the declared finite model/window plus its stated calibration and sampling assumptions; it does not prove which assumption failed.
- **ORBIT_CERTIFIED_AT_REGISTERED_CONFIDENCE** if \(\{\kappa_G(x):x\in\mathcal C\}\) is a singleton. Return that signature, the full compatible set, \(\alpha,b,r_n\), and every retained flag.
- **INCONCLUSIVE** otherwise, with the full set of compatible orbit signatures and the same uncertainty record.

On the registered model the true vector belongs to the mathematically defined compatible set with probability at least \(1-\alpha\), whether or not that set is exported. Therefore the **unconditional probability of an incorrect orbit certificate or false model rejection is at most \(\alpha\)**. This is not a claim that a certificate is delivered with probability \(1-\alpha\), and not a conditional coverage claim among the successful datasets. If \(r_n+b<\delta_R/2\), at most one vector is compatible. Writing \(\beta_{\rm fail}\) for a separately certified upper bound on the probability that any source/record failure flag occurs, correct singleton certification then has probability at least \(1-\alpha-\beta_{\rm fail}\). The exact ideal registered source has \(\beta_{\rm fail}=0\). If trial \(t\) has complete output TV error at most \(\epsilon_t\) relative to the ideal failure-free trial, the union bound gives \(\beta_{\rm fail}\le\min(1,\sum_t\epsilon_t)\), with any separately specified record-loss events added. A mean-bias budget alone does not promise a small abort probability. A finite sufficient repetition count follows from (29), for any declared \(b<\delta_R/2\); high delivery probability additionally requires its own failure budget.

The analogous 576-setting group-action extractor substitutes the exact matrix catalogue of Section 2, \(50\) by \(1152\) in (29), and separation \(2^{-36}\) from (6). It returns the compatible element(s), or their declared conjugacy/orbit signatures if that is the chosen quotient. It does not read a program label as a substitute for measuring the action.

**Calibration transport.** The source's original trace/half-diamond bound
\(\epsilon=\delta_0+\epsilon_H+\sum_j\eta_j\), plus separately recorded leakage and timing terms, bounds total variation of the complete trial output. A statistic in \([-1,1]\) then has mean bias at most \(2\epsilon\); for the vacuum 0/1 statistic the sharper bound is \(\epsilon\). Thus \(b=2\epsilon\) is a valid common bound when no further calibration errors are present. Registered detector gain/offset errors add their explicit uniform bounds. The raw complete transcript remains the measured object, and the predefined zero statistic for failed trials is part of this comparison; failure events are never removed to justify a smaller error. The output-supply probability uses the separate \(\beta_{\rm fail}\) budget above, not just this per-setting mean-bias bound. For nonidentical independent trials the same Hoeffding estimate applies with their uniform bias bounds.

This is **not** zero-error exact state or group identification from a finite number of arbitrary nonorthogonal probes. The exact signature is the theoretical response functional; its physical finite-sample realization is the stated confidence certificate with failure and inconclusive outputs. Nothing in S8 licenses replacing the statistical extractor by perfect tomography. Conversely a demand for perfect single-shot orbit labels on an unrestricted continuous state family would be a different assertion, not proved here.

##### 7. Same positive source, retained controls, and complete resources

Every new action \(U(g)\), every projector in (18)/(25), and every probe instrument (17)/(24) is neutral for the *existing* decoded charge partition. They therefore fall under the explicit all-neutral finite-instrument synthesis clause in R.4.2.8e, rather than requiring an unregistered source interaction. All coefficients are algebraic: group matrices are rational by Section 2, exterior entries are minors, and lattice-probe coefficients involve only rational arithmetic and algebraic square roots. The exact finite register compiler of Lemma R.4.2.8d.1 applies.

For a finite registered protocol form one common catalogue containing its action elements, its probes, recovery, readouts, and the original required services. Use the exact source isometries of Proposition R.4.2.8e, algebraic blockwise unitary completion, and padded positive assisted-hopping cycle. For each catalogue word retain its full gate list and its scratch-return property. Its endpoint commutes with every existing charge projector, so the closing-edge intertwiner and the single fixed programme-conditioned conserved projector \(Q_h\) of R.4.2.8e apply unchanged. This is essential: it would fail for a charge-changing endpoint merely inserted into the existing closed charge frame.

At stage zero the charge/validity partition is still the original one. Programme changes and feedback use separate neutral controller workspace. Coherent programme inputs use one registered joint dilation, including shared environment bases and relative phases. All outputs, including zero outcomes, displaced syndrome, Kraus/environment ownership, old records, and programme controls remain in the source census. The action does not borrow an uncharged reference: its no-error/single-error coherence was prepared by (17)/(24) and its actual preparation noise service, clock, and excitation inventory are charged.

For any resulting finite register list \((q_1,\ldots,q_r)\), including all declared fresh/retained records, controller and programme factors, set

\[
D_{\rm reg}=\prod_iq_i,\quad s=2r-1,\quad
L_{\rm cyc}=\max\!\left\{2,
\left[\frac{D_{\rm reg}(D_{\rm reg}-1)}2+D_{\rm reg}\right](2r+1)(84r+4)\right\}.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.31}
\]

The accepted common padded carrier and pulse use

\[
N_{\rm rails}\le\sum_iq_i+2s+L_{\rm cyc},\quad
E^0=3r\hbar\Omega,\quad T=2\pi/\Omega,
\]
\[
t_{\rm cyc}=L_{\rm cyc}T,\quad
\int\|H-H^0\|dt\le\pi\hbar L_{\rm cyc},\quad
W_{\rm abs}\le3\pi\hbar L_{\rm cyc}/T.
\tag{R.4.2.8f.PR.32}
\]

Each readiness check adds its own two rails, one \(\hbar\Omega\) excitation, one \(T\) pulse, action/work terms, and a complete detector service. Every detector has its positive registered duration and apparatus charge. Programme selection, separate controller workspace, source loading, and classical enumeration/extraction are separately finite charged operations, not hidden inside \(L_{\rm cyc}\). Old occupied factors add exactly their registered energy-time holding. A fresh supplied code state uses the accepted encoder with at most 285 elementary slots; it is not prepared from a free charged state inside the conservation interval.

For illustration, a single trial needs a probe, the selected action, a complete readout, and optionally a recovery that restores the same code input. The catalogue can use a two-level probe environment and pointer, a 2326-level recovery archive and two-level validity flag, and a three-level measurement pointer (padding the binary vacuum measurement). The measurement pointer is copied to a fresh retained archive or kept; it is never reused by overwriting an unowned outcome. Registering a finite run simply unrolls all its fresh record factors before applying (31). This unrolling is the explicit algorithm generating the finite register-list input to the accepted compiler, rather than a separate unresolved existence premise: for each scheduled operation append its specified fresh pointer/environment/archive factors, retain all earlier factors, append the finite programme and separate controller workspace, and then compute the displayed products and bounds. A physical execution must of course instantiate that generated inventory, just as it must instantiate its chosen finite gate list. Repeated experiments may use fresh copies of this exact same source model; their independence and additional loading costs are explicit statistical/resource premises.

The finite lattice and group enumerations are exact bounded integer computations, though enormous. Their finite classical memory, gate steps, input precision, and retention duration are determined by the declared exhaustive algorithms and are controller costs. No feasible-runtime, small-resource, optimality, or free classical postprocessing claim is made. This is a formal source construction, as is the compiler of Lemma R.4.2.8d.1 that it consumes.

The physical source-to-response map is the original tensor rail embedding, programme/stage evolution, complete pointer projection, and trace only over permanently private factors. Pulling (7)–(30) through that same map identifies their complete instruments and future responses. Consequently the representation of Theorem R.4.2.8f and the orbit protocol sit on the same source as Proposition R.4.2.8e, not a separate Hilbert space with a reused name.

###### A frozen finite nonvacuous protocol instance

The finite protocol instance below fixes, before any outcome data, the complete window \(R=4\), 25 settings, \(n=20000\) fresh trials per setting, \(\alpha=1/100\), and unconditional mean-bias budget \(b=1/64\). This window has the zero vector and exactly 196560 norm-four vectors, by the following source-internal exhaustive census of numerator shapes:

- two entries of absolute value four: \(4\binom{24}{2}=1104\);
- eight entries of absolute value two supported on a Golay octad, with an even number of minus signs: \(759\cdot128=97152\);
- one entry of absolute value three and 23 of absolute value one: \(24\cdot4096=98304\).

For the odd case write \(v_j\) for the vector with \(-3\) at coordinate \(j\) and ones elsewhere. These are precisely \(D_c v_j\), \(c\in K\), with \(D_c\) the codeword sign change. Indeed the parity condition on \((D_cv_j-v)/2\) is exactly \(c\in K\), and its sum is a multiple of four by double evenness. For even numerators the bound on squared norm and minimum codeword weight force the first two shapes, with the indicated sum congruence. Odd numerators of smaller squared norm would have all entries of absolute value one, giving squared lattice norm three, excluded by evenness. The verifier checks all odd-case members against the original congruences; the two even counts follow directly from their displayed supports and signs. The three families are disjoint and exhaustive.

For two different norm-four vectors, (25) gives a Y-mean separation of at least \(1/(5\sqrt2)>1/8\). Between zero and a norm-four vector the vacuum means differ by \(4/5\). Thus the protocol uses the improved safe \(\delta=1/8\). Since \(\log5000<9\), its sampling radius is less than \(1/32\), so \(r_n+b<3/64<\delta/2\). It separately requires \(\beta_{\rm fail}\le1/100\) if a correct-certificate delivery probability of at least 98% is claimed. Without that additional failure certificate, only the wrong-certification bound remains. This is a frozen mathematical candidate protocol, not an assertion that 500000 physical trials or their calibration have occurred, or that any campaign owner has accepted them. General windows and the action-matrix test retain the full theorems above.

### R.4.2.9 Golay Matroid Locality

<a id="definition-r429a-golay-dependency-matroid"></a>**Definition R.4.2.9a (Golay Dependency Matroid).** Let $\mathcal G_{24}\subset\mathbb F_2^{24}$ be the marked extended binary Golay code, and let $G$ be a $12\times24$ generator matrix. The Golay dependency matroid $\mathcal M_G$ is the binary matroid represented by the columns of $G$. A circuit is a minimal linearly dependent set of columns. A cocircuit is a circuit of the dual matroid $\mathcal M_G^*$.

<a id="theorem-r429b-self-dual-golay-matroid-cuts-and-dependencies"></a>**Theorem R.4.2.9b (Self-Dual Golay Matroid Cuts and Dependencies).** Since
$$
\mathcal G_{24}=\mathcal G_{24}^{\perp},
$$
the matroid $\mathcal M_G$ is self-dual. Its circuits are exactly the minimal supports of nonzero Golay codewords, and its cocircuits are exactly the minimal supports of nonzero dual codewords. Therefore the circuit girth and cocircuit girth are both
$$
8.
\tag{R.31}
$$

*Proof.* For a binary matrix $G$, a set of columns $S$ is dependent exactly when there is a nonzero vector $c\in\mathbb F_2^{24}$ supported on $S$ such that
$$
Gc^T=0.
$$
Thus circuits are the minimal supports of nonzero vectors in the nullspace of $G$. Since $G$ generates $\mathcal G_{24}$ and the code is self-dual,
$$
\ker G=\mathcal G_{24}^{\perp}=\mathcal G_{24}.
$$
Therefore circuits are minimal supports of nonzero Golay codewords. The dual matroid is represented by a parity-check matrix for $\mathcal G_{24}$, but for a self-dual code a parity-check matrix may be chosen as a generator matrix. Hence cocircuits are minimal supports of nonzero codewords in $\mathcal G_{24}^{\perp}=\mathcal G_{24}$. The extended binary Golay code has minimum Hamming weight $8$, so both the smallest circuit and the smallest cocircuit have size $8$. ∎

<a id="corollary-r429c-dependency-cut-equivalence-at-the-golay-carrier"></a>**Corollary R.4.2.9c (Dependency-Cut Equivalence at the Golay Carrier).** On the marked 24-mode carrier, minimal predictive dependencies and minimal predictive cuts are dual presentations of the same Golay matroid structure. No nontrivial dependency or cut syndrome exists below weight $8$.

*Proof.* Theorem R.4.2.9b identifies circuits with minimal dependencies and cocircuits with minimal cuts. Self-duality identifies the two classes, and the common girth is $8$. ∎

### R.4.2.9d A Physical Marked-Cut Net with Full Continuum Duality

<a id="theorem-r429d-marked-golay-recovery-physical-net-comparison-and-all-diamond-haag-duality"></a>**Theorem R.4.2.9d (Marked Golay Recovery, Physical Net Comparison and All-Diamond Haag Duality).** On the separately supplied regular flat3+1 Lorentzian branch, retain the inherited admitted primitive MPU source with its explicitly owned finite auxiliaries, the full marked24-qubit Golay input algebra, both parity responses and every original syndrome/sextet instrument. The adapted-Whitney ordinary-tensor construction below supplies one physical local-insertion net and an exact same-clock, completely isometric comparison system. It carries all marked cuts, full input/reference-stable local recovery, every retained cover/time slice/geometric map, exact full finite complements and a vanishing correctly typed boundary ledger. Its actual vacuum/source-isometry representation is nonatomic symmetric Fock space. For every tilted or untilted diamond, every full represented commutant operator has uniformly bounded strong approximants from actual compatible exterior families, and the represented net satisfies Haag duality.

The field auxiliary is a declared physical retained algebra with complete norm isometry and autonomous observation dynamics inside the owned core/auxiliary hardware. No whole-hardware commutant is silently replaced by a field commutant. Persistent MPU carriers follow the constant-topology worldline branch of Lemma E.9.5.2; distinct observations of one carrier are not independent copied Hilbert factors. The local-insertion source below directly satisfies the physical F.10 net/recovery/complement predicates. It is not identified with a separately tensorized raw point-event construction of Definitions F.1–F.2. The admitted primitive premise and supplied geometry are retained, rather than inferred from a bareC8 dimension or from field-only duality.

**Existing marked finite source and physical cut response.**

**Finite-source component 1.**

Let $\Omega$ be the 24 marked coordinates and $C\subset\mathbb{F}_2^\Omega$ the extended binary self-dual $[24,12,8]$ Golay code, with a fixed rank-12 generator $G$. The physical finite carrier is
\[
 \mathcal H=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes24},\quad
 \mathcal B=B(\mathcal H),\quad
 V=\mathbb F_2^{24}\oplus\mathbb F_2^{24}.
\]
The marked constructor fixes $\Omega=\mathbb{F}_{23}\cup\{\infty\}$, with $\infty$ last, and uses the exact original-source span in the proof of U.71a, item 4: the all-ones word and the 23 words $(N_{23}+s)\cup\{\infty\}$, where $N_{23}=\{5,7,10,11,14,15,17,19,20,21,22\}$. Its fixed generator masks are those printed in the marked-register construction below. This is a narrow generator prerequisite, not work on U.71a's separate owner. The rank 12 self-dual Golay data and native complete weight spectrum are retained as the original marked input.

For $v=(x,z)$, take $W(v)=X(x)Z(z)$; harmless phase conventions do not change its symplectic commutator. For a coordinate subset $R\subset\Omega$ let $V_{R}$ consist of both Pauli coordinates supported inside $R$. The finite region algebra is $A(R)=\operatorname{span}\{W(v):v\in V_R\}$, concretely $B((\mathbb{C}^2)^{\otimes R})\otimes I_{R^c}$.

The matroid cuts are its cocircuit supports. Their map is the actual support map $D\mapsto R_D=D$, followed by the displayed algebra. It is fixed by the marked coordinate identification. For the finite spatial record, fix an independently calibrated length $\ell_{0}>0$ and label $i=x+4y+12z$ for $x\in\{0,1,2,3\}$, $y\in\{0,1,2\}$, $z\in\{0,1\}$. Put the marked qubit at
\[
 \ell_0((2x+1)/8,(2y+1)/6,(2z+1)/4)
\]
and give it its disjoint open cube of side $\ell_0/24$. $R_D$ is the union of the corresponding cubes. The operational complement is relative to this finite carrier's union of cubes, not automatically the whole spacetime causal complement. Intersections, unions, disjointness and supports are preserved exactly. Scaling this block scales every site and interaction support together; it does not rescale the clock without a new record.

**Finite-source component 2.**

**Coordinate-local completion.** Work in the ambient full algebra $B(H)$ and $V$ above. For a region $R$, let $S_R\subset V_R$ and $S_{R^c}\subset V_{R^c}$ be arbitrary linear subspaces. Then
\[
 S_{R^c}=S_R^{\perp_\omega}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 S_R=V_R,\ S_{R^c}=V_{R^c}.
\tag{R.4.2.9d.1}
\]
Thus there is a unique coordinate-local subspace assignment satisfying exact complement duality for all regions.

*Proof.* $V_R^{\perp}=V_{R^c}$ by the nondegenerate coordinate symplectic pairing. The inclusion $S_R\subset V_R$ implies $V_{R^c}\subset S_R^{\perp}$. If $S_R^{\perp}=S_{R^c}\subset V_{R^c}$, equality holds throughout. Taking symplectic complements gives $S_R=V_R$. Conversely the full coordinate spaces are exact complements. Lemma F.10.5b translates this into $A(R)'=A(R^c)$. ∎

This theorem uses the full 24-qubit ambient commutant. A code-projected algebra, gauge quotient or logical representation has a different commutant and is not covered.

Write $C_R=\{c\in C:\operatorname{supp}c\subset R\}$, $r_R=\dim C_R$. The literal shortened CSS space $T_R=C_R\oplus C_R$ is isotropic because $C=C^{\perp}$. Moreover $r_R+r_{R^c}\le 12$, since the two disjointly supported subcodes embed directly in $C$. If $T_{R^c}=T_R^{\perp}$, dimension would require
\[
 2r_{R^c}=48-2r_R,
\]
or $r_R+r_{R^c}=24$, impossible. Thus the shortened stabilizers alone fail the ambient complement gate for every coordinate region, including empty and full ones. Completing them to the full local Pauli algebras is necessary, not a consequence of the Golay code's self-duality.

**Finite-source component 3.**

Prepare
\[
 |C\rangle=2^{-6}\sum_{c\in C}|c\rangle.
\tag{R.4.2.9d.2}
\]
Choose the source generator in reduced row-echelon form, with pivot positions $p_1,\dots,p_{12}$. Start all 24 qubits in $|0\rangle$. Apply $H$ to each pivot qubit. For every nonpivot entry $G_{ij}=1$, apply CNOT from pivot $p_{i}$ to target $j$. No pivot is targeted. For each input $u\in\mathbb{F}_2^{12}$, this maps the pivot word $u$ with zero nonpivots to $uG$. Since the rows are independent, all 4096 outputs are distinct; the resulting state is exactly (3.1). There is no postselection, hidden random seed or unowned codeword lookup table. $G$ and the generated gate list are the finite source data.

For this fixed generator the list contains 12 Hadamard gates and 84 CNOT gates, hence $N_{g}=96$. The action on every pivot wordu is exactlyuG by the displayed gate construction, including its coherent linear extension.

Both $H$ and CNOT are Hermitian involutions. A gate $U$ can be implemented for a declared duration $\tau_{g}>0$ by the bounded positive Hamiltonian
\[
 H_U=\frac{\pi\hbar}{2\tau_g}(I-U),\qquad
 e^{-iH_U\tau_g/\hbar}=U.
\tag{R.4.2.9d.3}
\]
The gate eigenvalues are 0 and $\pi\hbar/\tau_g$. Run the finite gate list serially on one physical time coordinate. The complete driven preparation ledger retains the initial pure qubits, the finite classical program, each switched Hamiltonian, total duration $N_g\tau_g$ and the switching controller. During each constant pulse its mean energy is conserved; switching on injects its actual mean and switching off recovers that same mean in the ideal reversible pulse convention. The total possible positive switched energy is bounded by $N_g\pi\hbar/\tau_g$. No vanishing controller cost, thermal reset floor or scalable PU-complexity theorem is inferred from this bound. Initial preparation and any physical readout/reset must be charged separately if their tasks are claimed.

Every two-site interaction lies inside this finite block. The graph of permitted pairs has bounded degree at most 23 and finite graph diameter; each serial pulse has bounded norm fixed by the declared $\tau_{g}$. This is a finite source realization. Its refinement does not establish an attained continuum propagation speed, generator limit or Lorentz promotion.

The state is stabilized by $X(c)$ and $Z(c)$ for every $c\in C$. These 24 independent commuting Pauli constraints select one normalized vector because $C$ is self-dual. More generally,
\[
 \langle C|X(x)Z(z)|C\rangle=
 \begin{cases}1,&x,z\in C,\\0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
\tag{R.4.2.9d.4}
\]
Indeed $X(x)$ preserves the codeword superposition precisely for $x\in C$, and the character sum of $(-1)^{z\cdot c}$ is nonzero precisely for $z\in C^{\perp}=C$. Thus the marked cut/dependency data change actual retained Pauli response, rather than merely label an unrelated net.

**3.1 Owned one-shot response pointer.**

For a Hermitian Pauli involution $Q$, append one fresh pointer qubit in $|0\rangle$. Apply $H$ to the pointer, controlled-$Q$ to the carrier, then $H$ to the pointer. Controlled-$Q$ is itself a Hermitian involution, so each of these three pulses has the positive implementation (R.4.2.9d.3). The total pointer/source isometry is

  $$
  \begin{gathered}
  |\phi\rangle|0\rangle\mapsto P_+|\phi\rangle|0\rangle+P_-|\phi\rangle|1\rangle, \\
  P_\pm=(I\pm Q)/2.
  \end{gathered}
  $$

The two orthogonal pointer records have probabilities $(1\pm\langle Q\rangle)/2$. This is the actual response coordinate used in (R.4.2.9d.4). For a general Pauli index $(x,z)$, take $Q=i^{x\cdot z\bmod 2}X(x)Z(z)$, which is Hermitian and squares to $I$. For the code-response probes in (R.4.2.9d.4), $x\cdot z=0$ in $\mathbb{F}_2$, so $Q$ is exactly the displayed $X(x)Z(z)$.

This one-shot protocol owns 24 freshly prepared source qubits, one fresh pointer, 99 serial pulses, duration $99\tau_g$, and positive switched-energy bound $99\pi\hbar/\tau_g$. The controlled pulse has finite support contained in one block plus its pointer. The independent length $\ell_{0}$ and physical pulse time $\tau_{g}$ are retained parameters; no speed is inferred from their ratio. Initialization of the declared pure input registers is a retained resource, not a derived zero-work process.

Repeated probes use fresh source/pointer registers and retain their records; no uncharged erasure is used. A finite collection of k one-shot probes therefore has exactly k times these register and pulse budgets. The analytic probabilities and entropies are exact source responses. An empirical estimate from finitely many random records would have sampling error; no exact finite-shot entropy reconstruction is claimed.

**Finite-source component 4.**

**Cut spectrum and matroid connectivity.** For every $R\subset\Omega$, the reduced state $\rho_R=\operatorname{tr}_{R^c}|C\rangle\langle C|$ is uniform on a subspace of rank $2^{|R|-2r_R}$. Consequently
\[
 S(\rho_R)=(|R|-2r_R)\ln2
 =\lambda_{\mathcal M_G}(R)\ln2,
\tag{R.4.2.9d.5}
\]
where
\[
 \lambda_{\mathcal M_G}(R)
 =\operatorname{rank}G_R+\operatorname{rank}G_{R^c}-12.
\]
Its Schmidt coefficients across $R|R^c$ are all equal, and the Schmidt rank is exactly $2^\lambda$.

*Proof.* The full density matrix is the uniform sum of its $2^{24}$ stabilizers, scaled by $2^{-24}$. Partial trace deletes all terms with nontrivial complementary support, leaving
\[
 \rho_R=2^{-|R|}\sum_{c,d\in C_R}X_R(c)Z_R(d).
\tag{R.4.2.9d.6}
\]
The $2r_R$ generators are independent and commute. Their group average is the orthogonal projector $\Pi_{R}$ onto their joint +1 eigenspace, of rank $2^{|R|-2r_R}$. Thus $\rho_R=2^{2r_R-|R|}\Pi_R$, proving its spectrum and entropy.

The nullspace of the restricted column matrix $G_{R}$ consists exactly of vectors supported in $R$ belonging to $\ker G=C$. Therefore $\operatorname{rank}G_R=|R|-r_R$. Restricting codewords to $R$ is a linear map $C\to\mathbb{F}_2^R$ with kernel $C_{R^c}$, so its rank is $12-r_{R^c}$. These ranks agree, giving $r_R-r_{R^c}=|R|-12$. Hence
\[
 \lambda(R)=12-r_R-r_{R^c}=|R|-2r_R.
\]
A pure bipartite state's nonzero reduced eigenvalues are the squares of its Schmidt coefficients; (4.2) proves the final statement. ∎

For any nonempty $R$ of size below eight, $r_{R}=0$ and $S(R)=|R|\ln 2$. For an octad $D$, the only nonzero codeword supported within it is its indicator, so $r_{D}=1$ and $S(D)=6\ln 2$; its complement has $r_{D^c}=5$ and the same entropy. Thus the original minimal cut carries an actual six-ebit response on the completed local net. Full coordinate algebras have not erased the matroid data: their prepared-state spectra measure its connectivity function for every cut and every Boolean combination of cuts.

The minimal code supports, hence circuits and cocircuits, are the 759 octads and 2576 dodecads. A dodecad cannot contain an octad because their difference would be a codeword of weight four. A weight-16 word has an octad complement; the shortening identity makes its shortened code five-dimensional, so it contains nonzero proper codewords. The all-ones word also contains octads. This exhausts the standard Golay weight spectrum. The exact original marked generator and complete weight spectrum are fixed source data.

**One fixed24-atom territorial source.**

**4. A fixed connected spatial partition for all 24 marked cuts.**

This section supplies a **new spatial cut contract**; it is not an equivalence to the old disconnected union of seed balls. Label one coordinate, 1, as the background label. Construct an embedded complete graph on the other 23 chambers, with narrow disjoint tubes joining each pair; all tubes avoid $Q_{1}$ and all nonincident chambers. Split every tube transversely at its midpoint, assigning its two halves to its endpoints. Each half includes a collar to that full cross-section, so two selected endpoint pieces glue along a whole disk, not at a point or edge. Let $C_i$, $i=2,\dots,24$, be the resulting closed chamber-plus-half-tube territories. Their interiors are disjoint. Their only pairwise contacts are the designated disks. Put

$$
\begin{aligned}
U_R&=\operatorname{int}(\bigcup_{i \in R} C_i),\quad \text{if } 1 \notin R,\ R \text{ nonempty}, \\
U_\emptyset&=\emptyset, \\
U_R&=\mathbb{R}^3 \setminus \operatorname{closure}(U_{R^c}),\quad \text{if } 1 \in R.
\end{aligned}
\tag{4.1}
$$

In the first line, the union is a union of the **closed** territories: its interior includes a gluing disk when both endpoints are selected. $U_{\mathrm{all}}=\mathbb{R}^3$. All original seed supports lie strictly in their corresponding $U_{i}$. Every complement is literal regular-open complement: $U_{R^c}=\operatorname{int}(\mathbb{R}^3\setminus U_R)$.

**Effective geometric construction.** Rational polygonal paths can be chosen one at a time between distinct chamber face ports in the complement of the other chambers and old paths. The complement of finitely many disjoint balls/chambers is path connected; removal of finitely many arcs in three dimensions leaves a route after a small transverse polygonal perturbation. A finite rational search checks segment intersections and positive distances to nonincident compact pieces; a sufficiently close rational perturbation of a disjoint route passes those strict tests. After all paths are fixed, the minimum nonincident clearance is positive. Approximate them within a smaller fraction of that clearance by fine dyadic axis paths; erase self loops and separate turns. Use a further sufficiently fine dyadic grid, finite cubical vertex neighborhoods, and constant-width rectangular tubes with separate face ports. At turns use cubical ball neighborhoods. This is a regular neighborhood of the graph: vertex pieces are 3-balls, tubes are 1-handles attached along disjoint disks. Every operation and its strict-clearance test is finite and effective. Choose its final coarse dyadic spacing h once, before regulator refinement, and retain the whole geometry as source data. For a determinate choice, enumerate finite dyadic vertex-ball/rectangular-tube certificates by description length and lexical order, checking disjoint interiors, full-disk attachments, containment of the fixed chambers and avoidance of $Q_{1}$, and take the first certificate. These are finite rational incidence/clearance tests. The construction just proved shows that this search halts. Its selected h, positive clearance, finite bounding box and F are thus fixed effective source data, not additional existence assumptions. Its size and boundary count are not n-dependent freebies.

**Topology proof for every subset.** A nonempty subset of vertices of a complete graph has a connected induced graph. Its regular neighborhood, with the selected half-tubes to unselected vertices as capped pendant handles, is a connected handlebody. Begin with one selected vertex ball and add a spanning tree of selected edges: attaching another ball by one handle leaves a ball. Every additional selected edge attaches a 1-handle to a connected handlebody. Pendant half-tubes merely extend a boundary disk and introduce no new component. Its boundary is connected and has an outward collar. Its exterior is path connected: a generic polygonal path between two exterior points has finitely many transverse boundary crossings; replace each segment inside the handlebody by a path in the connected boundary, pushed outward along that collar. The replacements join in the exterior. Thus every $U_{R}$ with $1\notin R$, $R$ nonempty, and its exterior are connected. Formula (4.1) handles all $R$ containing 1. Empty/full give the explicit empty/$\mathbb{R}^3$ exceptions. In particular all $2^{24}$ cuts, including their complements, are covered by one fixed geometry.

Let $F$ be the number of $h$-square faces in the finite territorial boundary complex. Every cut boundary is a subset of these faces. For $0<r\le h$ its $r$-neighborhood obeys the uniform estimate

 $$
 |\{x:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial U_R)<r\}|\le 2F\,r(h+2r)^2\le 18F\,h^2r.
 \tag{4.2}
 $$

For one square, its Euclidean r-neighborhood lies in a box of normal thickness 2r and tangential side h+2r; sum over all faces. Intersections only improve the bound. Empty/full have zero left side. The minimum seed-to-boundary and nonincident-handle clearances are fixed positive numbers. Dyadic refinement respects all territory faces exactly once $\delta_{n}$ divides h. Exterior-box truncation contributes only the separately receding outer box; every fixed compact support eventually avoids it.

For bounded $f$ and strip deletion $f_r=\mathbf{1}_{\operatorname{dist}(\partial U_R)>r}f$,

 $$
 \lVert f-f_r\rVert_2\le\lVert f\rVert_\infty\sqrt{18Fh^2r}.
 \tag{4.3}
 $$

This gives an explicit CAR norm bound, uniformly over these cuts, by CAR isometry and polynomial telescoping. For the marked seeds it is exactly zero below their fixed clearance. It is not a norm bound on the unit ball of all regional CAR observables.

**Concrete inherited core capability and stock.**

This is the concrete carrier record for P.13.1, P.13.2/Z.2 and P.13.4/Z.1. It does not assert that the entire apparatus is eight-dimensional, that its capacity equals $C_{P}$, or that Definition 13's full hierarchy has been proved. The distinguished core is $E_8=(\mathbb{C}^2)^{\otimes 3}$ with the actual roles ($B$ current/model state, $F$ stored forecast, $c$ commit/update phase). Every source, program, clock, orientation, stock, exported output and diagnostic environment remains a separate owned factor.

**1. Literal Theorem 15 capability on that core.**

Include a separately selected, finite-description SPAP diagnostic protocol with the exact complete permutation

$$
\begin{aligned}
T(B,F,0) &= (B,F,1), \\
T(B,F,1) &= (1-F,B,0).
\end{aligned}
$$

Its orbit is 000,001,100,101,110,111,010,011,000. Thus T is a unitary permutation of all eight basis states and has order eight. At the $c=0$ to $c=1$ commit step both current state and stored forecast are unchanged and simultaneously readable. At the update step the new current state is NOT the committed forecast, while the old current state is retained as the new forecast rather than erased. The phase is an actual register. This is precisely the (O1)–(O3), (FC) witness of Theorem 15, transported by the identity identification of its three roles, not inferred from an unrelated eight-state count. The three independently selectable role flips are also complete physical Pauli-X gates and give the operational context cube.

The mode selector, its immutable instruction library, controls, readout environment and any diagnostic inputs are owned finite stock. A bounded positive finite-order clock implements this protocol by the same Fourier construction as the finite-clock construction below. The mode is available on the same source but is not silently executed during each prediction trial. Its stock is common to all lattice configurations. Its execution contract and output slots are separate from the N-trial prediction service. No successful prediction on the reflex task is claimed.

In the predictive protocol, order the gates as follows: write F; flip c to commit before any change of B; charge the committed workload; produce the source-controlled future $B_{\mathrm{new}}$; verify; clear F reversibly; return c to zero. Flipping c and updating B commute, so this correct commit order does not change any endpoint or risk calculation. It does matter to the literal snapshot interpretation.

**2. Actual sharp binary verification, not a fair-bit coincidence.**

In a prediction trial, immediately after $B$ has become $Y$, the actual mismatch bit is $E=B\oplus F$. Apply the complete CNOT from $F$ to $B$. The physical $B$ readout is now exactly $E$. For $0<p_i<1/4$ both verification alternatives occur, with probabilities $p_i/2$ and $1-p_i/2$ under the optimal policy. They are orthogonal sharp physical alternatives, not two labels imposed on one ray.

To frame this record in the fixed active plane $A_0=\operatorname{span}\{|000\rangle,|100\rangle\}$ without erasure, add an owned two-bit register $R$ initially 00 and SWAP $(F,c)$ into $R$. The core now has $E$ in $B$ and zeros in $F,c$. The two sharp verification rays are therefore precisely those of $A_0$. The old forecast and phase remain in $R$. Inverting the SWAP and CNOT restores the original state; no entropy or information has disappeared. Readout uses a complete instrument and its output record is retained or explicitly included in a separate return protocol.

The active-ray count is $a=2$ because of this actual verification span. It does NOT require a fair error law. The QFI base $\rho_0=(|000\rangle\langle000|+|100\rangle\langle100|)/2$ is a separately supplied, exactly specified fair preparation on the same physical active plane. There is no assertion that the realized error probabilities equal 1/2.

For the canonical Z.2.5h diagnostic channel one may additionally implement

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Phi_{\mathrm{ver}}(\rho) &= \sum_s\langle s|\rho|s\rangle\,|m(s),0,0\rangle\langle m(s),0,0|, \\
m(s) &= 1\oplus B\oplus F.
\end{aligned}
$$

The eight Kraus operators $K_s=|m(s),0,0\rangle\langle s|$ satisfy $\sum K_s^*K_s=I_8$. A full Stinespring maps $|s\rangle|0\rangle_R$ to $|m(s),0,0\rangle|s\rangle_R$; it is an isometry on the input subspace and extends to a finite unitary on the entire hardware. Retain the eight-valued $R$ record. Uniform input contexts yield exactly $\rho_0$. This is the canonical repetition-register channel followed by the fixed unitary $\mathrm{CNOT}(B\to F)\,\mathrm{CNOT}(B\to c)$, which sends its 000/111 output rays to 000/100. It is a response-preserving active-plane overlap, with the environment explicitly owned. It need not overwrite the adaptive state on every trial.

**Finite positive-clock realization for the core service.**

Include every resource, output, comparison, orientation and routing register in the full stage permutation $W$. All charge transfers are resonant and every finite register gate is complete. For any source basis input, only the finitely many charge labels initially present in the $N$ cells and, possibly, the chiral charge state can be moved among slots or negated in the orbit/orientation convention. No gate manufactures a new unbounded label. A crude uniform finite orbit bound therefore exists, independent of the charge magnitudes. For example a source-label assignment bound $(2N+3)^{N+1}$, multiplied by all finite register dimensions and the stage-clock size, bounds every orbit. A chiral basis vector in the orthogonal complement stays in its singleton source sector. This is a full-carrier statement, not only a vacuum-input calculation.

Choose a common integer $L$ divisible by all possible cycle lengths, for example the least common multiple of $1,\dots,D$ for that bound $D$. Then $W^L=I$. Append an actual degenerate $L$-state counter $Q$, initially $|0\rangle$, and put

$$
\tilde{W}=W\otimes\mathrm{Shift}_L.
$$

Every orbit now has exactly $L$ elements: it cannot close before the counter does, and $W^L=I$. The added counter is large but finite and is explicitly charged as stock. It is not called a three-bit MPU or hidden inside $\mathbb{C}^8$.

Let $z_r=\exp(-2\pi ir/L)$ and

$$
\begin{aligned}
P_r &= (1/L)\sum_{k=0}^{L-1}z_r^{-k}\tilde{W}^{k}, \\
H_{\mathrm{clk}} &= (\hbar/\delta)\sum_r(2\pi r/L)P_r.
\end{aligned}
$$

Then $H_{\mathrm{clk}}$ is positive, bounded, a finite polynomial in the complete service operations, and $\exp(-i\delta H_{\mathrm{clk}}/\hbar)=\tilde{W}$. It commutes with the complete free Hamiltonian $H_{\mathrm{free}}$, including all battery/output gaps, because each gate conserves that energy. The native chiral clock is kept in $H_{\mathrm{free}}$.

For any data vector $\psi$ and initial counter $|0\rangle$,

$$
\langle\psi,0|P_r|\psi,0\rangle=1/L.
$$

Indeed all $k=1,\dots,L-1$ terms vanish by the counter overlap, independently of $\psi$. For an initialized basis vector of free energy $E$, the joint spectral distribution of $(H_{\mathrm{free}},H_{\mathrm{clk}})$ is therefore

$$
\delta_E\otimes\operatorname{Uniform}\{2\pi\hbar r/(L\delta)\}.
$$

A diagonal mixture of source, context, core and stock basis states has the common source energy law times this same uniform law. Thus **every integrable function $f(H_{\mathrm{free}},H_{\mathrm{clk}})$** has a candidate-independent expectation, including nonlinear $f(H_{\mathrm{free}}+H_{\mathrm{clk}})$. It is conserved under the autonomous dynamics. This is stronger than assuming separate additive energy prices. It also repairs the mistake of treating $H_{\mathrm{clk}}$ as a fixed core-only operator.

For finite core/local actuator charges, use the actual member/nonmember block intertwiners or the explicit classical-at-tick distributions. Their expectation is affine in p. For N trials the sum of local registered transfers remains affine by linearity even if the complete final output history is correlated. An arbitrary nonlinear price on the entire N-trial error history need not be affine and is not silently covered by the local sum.

Exact operation/readout times are the specified clock ticks. Additional time-resolution machinery, if required by a source beyond this direct Hamiltonian protocol, must be an owned source component. A mathematical periodic Hamiltonian alone is not a measurement-timing admission.

The field construction uses the complete finite order-eight core diagnostic and its owned stock at this role only. No chiral/VOA or lattice-selection source claim is transferred by that clock construction.

**Tensor-source proof 2.**

Here is one fixed splitter, including its action off the prepared subspace. On $b=8$ child qubits of one species use the occupation basis. In its one-excitation sector let $e_{0}$ be the first child and $w=b^{-1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{b-1}e_j$. Put
