### R.4.3 Synthesis: Why Three is Necessary

Three families arise from the anomaly and mixing requirements only on the registered selection branch. Triality and lattice geometry supply independent compatibility checks for that result.

#### Technical synthesis ledger

The family-count and flavor layers have distinct premise packages:

**Minimal admissible count:** On SM15, the linear and cubic anomaly equations plus a registered CP-active realization make $N=3$ the smallest nontrivial admissible count and select $(1,-1,0)$ up to sign and permutation. On SM16, the linear anomaly equation alone does not select that orbit; primitive normalization and the strict charge-norm objective select it at the smallest CP-capable count. Proposition R.3.5.1a then selects the realized count on its additional additive-monotone objective branch.

**Triality compatibility:** A marked real 8-carrier with $\mathfrak{so}(8)$ structure has the canonical $D_4$ triality orbit $\{V,S^+,S^-\}$. Imposing both SM15 equations on charges assigned to that orbit reproduces $\{a,-a,0\}$. On SM16 the cubic equation is absent, so triality is only a three-slot compatibility record and does not replace the primitive charge-norm selection.

**Geometric compatibility:** The registered Leech/$E_8$ construction and $M=24=8\times3$ carry a compatible three-fold scaffold. They do not independently select the family count.

**Phenomenological comparison:** The observed Standard Model count agrees with the value selected on the branch-resolved SM15 or SM16 premise package together with a registered CP-active realization and the additive-monotone family-count objective. Triality and lattice records test internal compatibility only.

## R.5 Conditional Mass-Hierarchy Model from $E_8$ Geometry

The following flavor model localizes wavepackets at selected $E_8$-related vacua and assigns Yukawa responses through overlap kernels. Hierarchical masses follow only after the localization, label, kernel, normalization, scale, and remainder certificates stated here and in Appendix T are imposed.

### R.5.1 Kähler Geometry and PCE-Compatible Potential

The complete flag manifold $\Sigma_8$ is a coadjoint orbit of $U(8)$, but its invariant Kähler metric is not unique: its invariant Kähler cone has one positive parameter for each simple root. Register the equal-simple-root branch
$$
\delta_i:=\lambda_i-\lambda_{i+1}=1,
\qquad i=1,\ldots,7.
$$
On the common graph chart, let $Z_k$ be the $k\times(8-k)$ holomorphic graph coordinate of the flag's $k$-plane, represented by the row space of $(I_k\ Z_k)$. The seven graph coordinates satisfy the flag-incidence relations. A local Kähler potential is
$$
K_\delta=\sum_{k=1}^7\delta_k\log\det(I_k+Z_kZ_k^\dagger).
$$
The associated Kähler two-form and real Riemannian metric are
$$
\omega_\delta=i\partial\bar\partial K_\delta,
\qquad
g_\delta(u,v)=\omega_\delta(u,Jv),
$$
where $J$ is the complex structure. Each summand is the pullback of the Grassmannian Kähler potential: the squared norm of the Plücker coordinate is $\det(I_k+Z_kZ_k^\dagger)$ by the Gram determinant identity. The positive weighted sum gives the declared flag metric. Finite geodesic distances below use $g_\delta$; the two-form $\omega_\delta$ is not itself a symmetric metric. The equal coefficients specify a metric branch, rather than a canonical choice on the full flag.

Let
$$
\mu_{U(8)}(x)=U(x)\Lambda U(x)^\dagger
$$
be the moment map on that branch, and let $\operatorname{pr}_{\mathrm{ss}}$ denote orthogonal projection to the embedded $\mathfrak{su}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)$ algebra. A topological charge $q\in\pi_2(\Sigma_8)$ labels a connected component $\mathscr F_q$ of a map or field-configuration space; it does not label a subset of points of $\Sigma_8$. For each retained $q$, register:

1. a compact finite-dimensional smooth collective-coordinate reduction $\mathcal M_q$ of $\mathscr F_q$;
2. a smooth localization-center map $\ell_q:\mathcal M_q\to\Sigma_8$;
3. a trace-zero diagonal Hermitian pinning matrix $B_q$, fixed before minimizing the potential; and
4. a smooth common pinning-independent term $W:\Sigma_8\to\mathbb R$.

With $C$ from Definition R.3.2, define on $\mathcal M_q$
$$
V_{0,q}(m)
=
\frac{\kappa_\mu}{2}
\left\|\operatorname{pr}_{\mathrm{ss}}\mu_{U(8)}(\ell_q(m))\right\|^2
+
\Lambda_I W(\ell_q(m))
+
\frac{\kappa_C}{2}\|Cq\|^2,
\tag{R.12}
$$
and
$$
h_q(m):=-\left\langle B_q,\mu_{U(8)}(\ell_q(m))\right\rangle,
\qquad
V_q:=V_{0,q}+\eta h_q.
$$
The moment-map term is pointwise and controls the localization center. The sector term $\|Cq\|^2$ is constant on $\mathcal M_q$ and vanishes exactly on the Cartan-neutral candidate lattice. Neither term proves full nonabelian singletness; retained labels must separately pass Definition R.3.2's full-label-singlet certificate.

### R.5.2 Generation Vacua and $E_8$ Root Distances

Choose distinct sector labels $q_+,q_-,q_0\in L_{\rm light}$ with
$$
\ell_F(q_+)=a,
\qquad
\ell_F(q_-)=-a,
\qquad
\ell_F(q_0)=0.
$$
The subscript $0$ denotes zero family charge and need not denote the zero element of $\pi_2(\Sigma_8)$.

<a id="theorem-r51-conditional-three-sector-vacuum-persistence"></a>**Theorem R.5.1 (Conditional Three-Sector Vacuum Persistence).** For each $q\in\{q_+,q_-,q_0\}$, fix a smooth Riemannian metric on the registered compact finite-dimensional reduction $\mathcal M_q$ and a compact window $K_q$ contained in its smooth interior. A local minimum in the window means an ambient local minimum on $\mathcal M_q$ whose location belongs to $K_q$. Assume:

1. $V_{0,q}$ and $h_q$ are smooth on a neighborhood of $K_q$;
2. $\mathscr C_q=\operatorname{argmin}_{K_q}V_{0,q}$ is a nonempty compact embedded critical submanifold without boundary, contained in $\operatorname{int}K_q$, and is Morse–Bott with positive-definite normal Hessian;
3. $h_q|_{\mathscr C_q}$ is Morse and has exactly one local minimum $m_q$; and
4. $\operatorname{Crit}(V_{0,q})\cap K_q=\mathscr C_q$.

Then, for all sufficiently small $\eta>0$, $V_q=V_{0,q}+\eta h_q$ has exactly one nondegenerate local minimum $m_q(\eta)$ in $K_q$, and $m_q(\eta)\to m_q$ as $\eta\downarrow0$. Define its localization center by
$$
p_q:=\ell_q(m_q(\eta))\in\Sigma_8.
$$
The three minima are distinct as elements of the disjoint union $\bigsqcup_q\mathcal M_q$. Their centers $p_q$ are pairwise distinct precisely when the registered localization maps satisfy that separation condition.

*Proof.* Fix one sector and omit its subscript. Write $v_*=\min_KV_0$ and $h_0=h|_{\mathscr C}$. Choose tubular coordinates $(x,z)$ about $\mathscr C$, where $x\in\mathscr C$ and $z$ belongs to its normal fiber, with the closure of the tube contained in $\operatorname{int}K$. Let
$$
F_\eta(x,z)=V_0(x,z)+\eta h(x,z),
\qquad H_x=D_z^2V_0(x,0).
$$
Compactness and positivity give a constant $\lambda>0$ with $H_x\ge\lambda I$ on every nonzero normal fiber. Smoothness and compactness permit a uniform fiber radius $\rho>0$ and a parameter bound such that
$$
\left\|I-H_x^{-1}D_z^2F_\eta(x,z)\right\|\le\tfrac12
\qquad (\|z\|\le\rho).
$$
Here vertical derivatives use the specified normal-fiber metric. Shrink the same bounds so that $D_z^2F_\eta\ge(\lambda/2)I$. Since $D_zV_0(x,0)=0$, the maps
$$
T_{x,\eta}(z)=z-H_x^{-1}D_zF_\eta(x,z)
$$
are contractions with constant at most $1/2$, and $\|T_{x,\eta}(0)\|\le A|\eta|$ for a uniform $A$. Choose $|\eta|$ small enough that $A|\eta|<\rho/2$. Each $T_{x,\eta}$ maps the closed fiber ball into itself. Its iterates satisfy
$$
\|z_{n+1}-z_n\|\le2^{-n}\|z_1-z_0\|,
$$
so their Cauchy limit $s_\eta(x)$ satisfies $T_{x,\eta}(s_\eta(x))=s_\eta(x)$; contraction also proves uniqueness in the fiber ball. In particular,
$$
D_zF_\eta(x,s_\eta(x))=0,\qquad
\|s_\eta(x)\|\le2A|\eta|.
$$
The sections $s_\eta$ depend smoothly on $(x,\eta)$. Indeed, difference quotients of the defining equation have the invertible coefficient $I-D_zT_{x,\eta}$, whose inverse is the convergent Neumann series $\sum_{j\ge0}(D_zT_{x,\eta})^j$ with norm at most $2$. Passing to the difference-quotient limit gives first derivatives, and repeated differentiation gives all higher derivatives. A finite atlas and the uniform contraction bounds make these conclusions uniform on $\mathscr C$. Uniqueness makes the local sections agree on chart overlaps.

Reduce the potential to this normal-critical section:
$$
f_\eta(x):=F_\eta(x,s_\eta(x)).
$$
Since $s_0=0$, $V_0|_{\mathscr C}=v_*$ and $D_zV_0|_{\mathscr C}=0$,
$$
f_0=v_*,
\qquad
\left.\partial_\eta f_\eta\right|_{\eta=0}=h_0.
$$
Taylor's formula with integral remainder therefore gives
$$
f_\eta=v_*+\eta h_0+\eta^2r_\eta,\qquad
r_\eta(x)=\int_0^1(1-t)\,
\left.\partial_\xi^2 f_\xi(x)\right|_{\xi=t\eta}\,dt.
$$
Smoothness on the compact parameterized base bounds $r_\eta$ in $C^2$ uniformly for small $\eta$. Thus
$$
g_\eta:=\frac{f_\eta-v_*}{\eta}
=h_0+\eta r_\eta
\longrightarrow h_0
\quad\text{in }C^2(\mathscr C)
$$
for $\eta\ne0$, with the smooth extension $g_0=h_0$.

Every critical point of $F_\eta$ in the tube must lie on the section, because its vertical derivative vanishes. On that section,
$$
df_\eta=D_xF_\eta+
D_zF_\eta\,ds_\eta=D_xF_\eta.
$$
Consequently, for $\eta\ne0$, the critical points in the tube correspond exactly to critical points of $g_\eta$.

A Morse function on a compact manifold has finitely many critical points: each is isolated by its invertible Hessian, and an infinite sequence would have a critical accumulation point. Write these points as $x_1,\ldots,x_N$ and choose disjoint coordinate balls centered at them. In the $i$th chart set $J_i=D^2h_0(x_i)$, which is invertible. Shrink the ball so that
$$
\left\|I-J_i^{-1}D^2h_0(y)\right\|<\tfrac14.
$$
The $C^2$ convergence then gives
$$
\left\|I-J_i^{-1}D^2g_\eta(y)\right\|<\tfrac12
$$
on that ball for small $|\eta|$. The map
$$
y\longmapsto y-J_i^{-1}Dg_\eta(y)
$$
is a contraction. Its displacement at the center tends to zero, so it maps the closed ball into itself for sufficiently small $|\eta|$. The same geometric-series iteration gives a unique critical point $x_i(\eta)$ there, tending to $x_i$. On the compact complement of the open balls, $dh_0$ has a positive lower bound whenever that complement is nonempty. Uniform $C^1$ convergence excludes further critical points there. The Hessians $D^2g_\eta(x_i(\eta))$ converge to $J_i$, so they remain invertible and have the same numbers of positive and negative eigenvalues.

At a critical point $(x_i(\eta),s_\eta(x_i(\eta)))$, write the full Hessian in tangential and normal coordinates as
$$
\begin{pmatrix} B&C\\ C^T&D\end{pmatrix},
\qquad D=D_z^2F_\eta>0.
$$
Differentiating the normal-critical equation gives $ds_\eta=-D^{-1}C^T$. Therefore
$$
D^2f_\eta=B-CD^{-1}C^T
=\eta D^2g_\eta.
$$
Completing the square in the Hessian quadratic form gives
$$
u^TBu+2u^TCv+v^TDv
=
u^T(\eta D^2g_\eta)u+
(v+D^{-1}C^Tu)^TD(v+D^{-1}C^Tu).
$$
For $\eta>0$ the full Hessian is nondegenerate and its negative index equals that of $h_0$ at $x_i$. Exactly one critical point in the tube is a local minimum, because $h_0$ has exactly one local minimum. The corresponding normal and tangential coordinates converge to $(m,0)$. Zero-dimensional normal fibers or tangential charts contribute only their unique point and an empty Hessian of index zero; the corresponding contraction step is unnecessary.

It remains to exclude critical points elsewhere in the window. Fix the open tube just used. Its complement in $K$ is compact and, by assumption4, contains no critical point of $V_0$. If this complement is nonempty, there is $\gamma>0$ such that $\|dV_0\|\ge\gamma$ there. Put $L=\sup_K\|dh\|<\infty$. For $\eta L<\gamma$,
$$
\|dV_\eta\|\ge\|dV_0\|-\eta\|dh\|
\ge\gamma-\eta L>0
$$
on the complement. If the complement is empty no exclusion is needed. Every ambient local minimum located in $K$ is a critical point, including any location on the cutoff boundary, because $K$ lies in the smooth interior of $\mathcal M$. Hence the unique minimum already found in the tube is the only one in $K$.

Apply this argument to the three sectors and take the minimum of their positive parameter bounds. Disjoint sector components distinguish the configurations; separation of their images under $\ell_q$ is precisely the additional condition stated for the centers. ∎

The three vacua are:
- $p_1$: vacuum for generation 1 (family charge $+a$)
- $p_2$: vacuum for generation 2 (family charge $-a$)
- $p_3$: vacuum for generation 3 (family charge $0$)

**Registered $E_8$ Triad Certificate:**

An $8$-dimensional coordinate count does not canonically embed the selected flag-manifold centers into the $E_8$ lattice, and a local tangent-metric identity does not fix finite geodesic distances. For the three-center flavor branch, register an ordered certificate
$$
\mathfrak C_{E_8}
=
(p_1,p_2,p_3;r_1,r_2,r_3;c_\Sigma),
$$
where $r_1,r_2,r_3$ are distinct $E_8$ roots, $c_\Sigma>0$, and all three pairwise distances satisfy
$$
d_\Sigma^2(p_g,p_{g'})
=
c_\Sigma\|r_g-r_{g'}\|^2.
$$
Define the normalized selected distances
$$
D_{gg'}:=c_\Sigma^{-1}d_\Sigma^2(p_g,p_{g'}).
$$
The common scale then cancels from every distance ratio. A nontrivial hierarchy requires the separately selected inequality
$$
D_{31}\ne D_{32}.
\tag{R.13}
$$
The family-charge values $\{a,-a,0\}$ do not by themselves select the ordered root labels or imply this inequality.

For distinct $E_8$ roots, Section R.2.2 gives
$$
D_{gg'}=\|r_g-r_{g'}\|^2\in\{2,4,6,8\}.
\tag{R.14}
$$
Thus discreteness is a theorem after $\mathfrak C_{E_8}$ is fixed; the physical triad, its ordering, and the common metric scale are certificate data fixed before phenomenological comparison.

<a id="proposition-r51a-weyl-classification-of-ordered-e_8-root-triads"></a>**Proposition R.5.1a (Weyl Classification of Ordered $E_8$ Root Triads).** For an ordered triple $(r_1,r_2,r_3)$ of distinct roots of $E_8$ put $D_{gg'}=\|r_g-r_{g'}\|^2$, so that $(D_{21},D_{31},D_{32})\in\{2,4,6,8\}^3$ by (R.7).

1. A triple $(D_{21},D_{31},D_{32})$ is realized by distinct roots exactly when either all three entries lie in $\{2,4,6\}$, or exactly one entry equals $8$ and the other two sum to $8$. There are $36$ realizable ordered triples.
2. Two ordered triples of distinct roots with the same $(D_{21},D_{31},D_{32})$ are conjugate under the Weyl group $W(E_8)$. Hence $W(E_8)$ has exactly $36$ orbits on ordered triads of distinct roots and exactly $12$ orbits on unordered triads, labeled by the realizable multisets $\{D_{21},D_{31},D_{32}\}$.
3. With $r_1=e_1+e_2$ and with $r_2=e_1+e_3$, $e_3+e_4$, $-e_1+e_3$ or $-e_1-e_2$ for $D_{21}=2,4,6,8$ respectively, the following Type I roots $r_3$ realize every triple of item 1:
$$
\begin{array}{c|l}
D_{21}&(D_{31},D_{32}):\ r_3\\\hline
2&(2,2){:}\,e_2+e_3;\ (2,4){:}\,e_1-e_3;\ (2,6){:}\,e_2-e_3;\ (4,2){:}\,e_1-e_2;\ (4,4){:}\,e_4+e_5;\ (4,6){:}\,-e_1+e_2;\\
&(6,2){:}\,-e_2+e_3;\ (6,4){:}\,-e_1+e_3;\ (6,6){:}\,-e_2-e_3;\ (6,8){:}\,-e_1-e_3;\ (8,6){:}\,-e_1-e_2\\
4&(2,2){:}\,e_1+e_3;\ (2,4){:}\,e_1+e_5;\ (2,6){:}\,e_1-e_3;\ (4,2){:}\,e_3+e_5;\ (4,4){:}\,e_1-e_2;\ (4,6){:}\,-e_3+e_5;\\
&(4,8){:}\,-e_3-e_4;\ (6,2){:}\,-e_1+e_3;\ (6,4){:}\,-e_1+e_5;\ (6,6){:}\,-e_1-e_3;\ (8,4){:}\,-e_1-e_2\\
6&(2,2){:}\,e_2+e_3;\ (2,4){:}\,e_1+e_3;\ (2,6){:}\,e_2-e_3;\ (2,8){:}\,e_1-e_3;\ (4,2){:}\,-e_1+e_2;\ (4,4){:}\,e_4+e_5;\\
&(4,6){:}\,e_1-e_2;\ (6,2){:}\,-e_2+e_3;\ (6,4){:}\,-e_1-e_3;\ (6,6){:}\,-e_2-e_3;\ (8,2){:}\,-e_1-e_2\\
8&(2,6){:}\,e_1+e_3;\ (4,4){:}\,e_1-e_2;\ (6,2){:}\,-e_1+e_3
\end{array}
\tag{R.14a}
$$
4. For a certificate $\mathfrak C_{E_8}$ with $D_{31}>D_{32}$, all six pairs $(D_{31},D_{32})$ with $D_{31}>D_{32}$ in $\{2,4,6,8\}$ are realizable. The remaining distance $D_{21}$ ranges over $\{2,4,6\}$ for $(4,2)$ and $(6,4)$, over $\{2,4,6,8\}$ for $(6,2)$, and equals $8-D_{32}$ when $D_{31}=8$. This class therefore consists of $13$ distance triples, each a single $W(E_8)$-orbit; Theorem T.42.1b lists the same $13$ orbits for the class of Definition T.42, with their stabilizers and orbit sizes.

*Proof.* (1) If $D_{21}=8$, then $r_2=-r_1$, so $D_{32}=\|r_3+r_1\|^2=4+2r_1\cdot r_3=8-D_{31}$, and $D_{31},D_{32}\ne8$ because $r_3\notin\{r_1,r_2\}$; the other positions of the entry $8$ are symmetric. Two entries equal to $8$ would make two of the roots coincide. The table (R.14a) realizes the $27$ triples in $\{2,4,6\}^3$ and the $9$ triples with one entry $8$; every listed vector is a Type I root, and each entry follows from $r_g\cdot r_{g'}=2-D_{gg'}/2$.

(2) The Weyl group acts transitively on the roots (Section R.2.2). It therefore suffices to show the following for a set $F$ of fixed roots, first $F=\{r_1\}$ and then $F=\{r_1,r_2\}$: if two roots $s,s'\notin F$ satisfy $s\cdot r=s'\cdot r$ for every $r\in F$, then some product of reflections in roots orthogonal to $F$ fixes $F$ pointwise and carries $s$ to $s'$. Let $U$ be the span of $F$ and put $L=E_8\cap U$, $M=E_8\cap U^\perp$, and $\Phi_U$ for the set of roots orthogonal to $U$. The lattice $L$ equals $\mathbb ZF$, of type $A_1$, $A_1^2$ or $A_2$: it is even, contains $\mathbb ZF$ and lies in $(\mathbb ZF)^*$, and the nonzero classes of $(\mathbb ZF)^*/\mathbb ZF$ have norms $\tfrac12$ for $A_1$, $\tfrac12,\tfrac12,1$ for $A_1^2$, and $\tfrac23,\tfrac23$ for $A_2$ modulo $2$, none of them even. Since $E_8$ is unimodular and $L$ is primitive, $\det M=\det L$, which is $2$, $4$ or $3$. The corresponding sets $\Phi_U$ are the standard orthogonal subsystems $E_7$, $D_6$ and $E_6$, whose root lattices have the same determinants and lie in $M$; hence $M=\mathbb Z\Phi_U$.

Write $s=p_s+m_s$ with $p_s\in U$ and $m_s\in U^\perp$. The inner products $s\cdot r$, $r\in F$, determine $p_s$, and $m_s\in M^*$ because $s\cdot x=m_s\cdot x\in\mathbb Z$ for $x\in M$. If $s$ and $s'$ have the same inner products with $F$, then $m_s-m_{s'}=s-s'\in M$, so $m_s$ and $m_{s'}$ lie in one coset of $M$ in $M^*$ and have the same norm $2-|p_s|^2$. For $F=\{r_1\}$ and for $r_2=-r_1$, $|p_s|^2=y^2/2$ with $y=s\cdot r_1$; for $r_1\cdot r_2=0$, $|p_s|^2=(y^2+z^2)/2$; and for $r_1\cdot r_2=\pm1$, $|p_s|^2=\tfrac23(y^2\mp yz+z^2)$, with $z=s\cdot r_2$. Hence $|m_s|^2\in\{0,\tfrac32,2\}$ for $E_7$, $\{0,1,\tfrac32,2\}$ for $D_6$ and $\{0,\tfrac43,2\}$ for $E_6$. The norms in the cosets of $M^*/M$ are congruent modulo $2$ to $0,\tfrac32$ for $E_7$, to $0,1,\tfrac32,\tfrac32$ for $D_6$, and to $0,\tfrac43,\tfrac43$ for $E_6$. Therefore either $m_s=0$, so that $s=s'$ is the unique root with the given inner products, or $m_s$ and $m_{s'}$ are minimal vectors of the same coset: roots of $\Phi_U$ in the trivial coset, and weights of the minuscule representations $\mathbf{56}$ of $E_7$, the vector and half-spin representations of $D_6$, or $\mathbf{27}$ and $\overline{\mathbf{27}}$ of $E_6$ in the others. The Weyl group $W(\Phi_U)$ acts transitively on the roots and on the weights of each minuscule representation, and it fixes $U$ pointwise, so some $w\in W(\Phi_U)$ maps $m_s$ to $m_{s'}$ and hence $s$ to $s'$. The count of unordered orbits is the number of realizable multisets: $10$ multisets from $\{2,4,6\}$, together with $\{8,2,6\}$ and $\{8,4,4\}$.

(3) and (4) are read off from items 1 and 2 and the table. ∎

<a id="r52a-per-triad-overlap"></a>

### R.5.2a Per-Triad Overlap Realization and Alignment Competitors

<a id="theorem-r51b"></a>

<a id="theorem-r51b-every-root-triad-has-a-single-block-bures-and-nonsingular-three-profile-realization"></a>**Theorem R.5.1b (Every Root Triad Has a Single-Block Bures and Nonsingular Three-Profile Realization).** Let $(r_1,r_2,r_3)$ be any ordered triple of distinct roots of the $E_8$ root system, with squared root norm 2 and $q_{ij}=|r_i-r_j|^{2}$; the root Gram matrix may be singular. The following statements hold.

1. There are rank-two projectors $P_i$ on $\mathbb{C}^8$ such that $\rho_i=P_i/2$ and every pair satisfies precisely the single active–inactive generator conditions of Lemma T.41.4, with $u_{ij}=\sqrt{q_{ij}/2}$. In particular their intrinsic Bures distances satisfy $d_B(\rho_i,\rho_j)^{2}=q_{ij}/8$.
2. Put $\xi_i=(r_i/(2\sqrt{2}),0_{16})$ in $\mathbb{R}^{24}$. For any $\sigma^{2}>0$ and $\rho>0$, the real two-copy Gaussian profiles below have strictly positive definite Gram matrix

   $$
   G_{ij}=\exp[-\rho q_{ij}/(16 \sigma^2)].
   $$

   Consequently they span exactly three dimensions even when the three root vectors are linearly dependent. On the two stiffness programs of Theorem Y.11.2g, $\sigma^{2}=1/24$ and $\rho\in\{1,1/\sqrt{3}\}$, every such Gram matrix obeys the uniform bound

$$
G\ge mI_3,\quad m=1-2\exp(-\sqrt{3})>0.
$$

3. Exactly three orthonormal real form factors suffice to factor this overlap as $G=BB^{T}$. With the explicitly installed canonical three-sterile action of the stated form-factor grammar, the zero-external-momentum Schur coefficient is $-y^{2}G/M$ for a common nonzero sterile mass $M$. This item is a response-realization statement: the physical controls, preparation and comparison costs of the installed action belong to its source grammar.

The theorem covers all 36 ordered distance classes of Proposition R.5.1a, including its 13 classes with singular root Gram. It constructs a separate embedding for each triad.

*Proof.* Let

$$
\begin{aligned}
&a=\sqrt{q_{12}}/2,\quad b=\sqrt{q_{13}}/2,\quad c=\sqrt{q_{23}}/2, \\
&s=(a+b+c)/2, \\
&C_{ii}=1,\quad C_{ij}=\cos(\sqrt{q_{ij}}/2).
\end{aligned}
$$

Three distinct equal-norm Euclidean vectors cannot be collinear: a line intersects their sphere in at most two points. Their Euclidean side lengths therefore satisfy strict triangle inequalities. Multiplication by 1/2 preserves these inequalities. Since each q is in `{2,4,6,8}`, every side is at most sqrt2, and

$$
\begin{aligned}
&0<s\le 3\sqrt{2}/2<\pi, \\
&0<s-a,\,s-b,\,s-c<\sqrt{2}<\pi.
\end{aligned}
$$

Direct expansion and factorization give

$$
\begin{aligned}
\det C \\
&= 1+2\cos(a)\cos(b)\cos(c)-\cos^{2}(a)-\cos^{2}(b)-\cos^{2}(c) \\
&= (\sin(b)\sin(c))^{2}-(\cos(a)-\cos(b)\cos(c))^{2} \\
&= [\cos(b-c)-\cos(a)][\cos(a)-\cos(b+c)] \\
&= 4\sin(s)\sin(s-a)\sin(s-b)\sin(s-c)>0.
\end{aligned}
$$

The two-by-two principal minors are $\sin^{2}(a)$, $\sin^{2}(b)$ and $\sin^{2}(c)$, all positive. Thus $C$ is positive definite. Choose real unit vectors $v_{i}$ with Gram $C$ in $\operatorname{span}(e_1,e_2,e_3)\subset\mathbb{C}^8$ and put

$$
P_i=|e_0\rangle\langle e_0|+|v_i\rangle\langle v_i|.
$$

For a fixed pair let $\theta=\sqrt{q_{ij}}/2$ and

$$
w_{ij}=(v_j-\cos(\theta)v_i)/\sin(\theta).
$$

Then $w_{ij}$ is unit and orthogonal to both $v_{i}$ and e0. Define on C8 the Hermitian generator

$$
A_{ij}=i(|w_{ij}\rangle\langle v_i|-|v_i\rangle\langle w_{ij}|)/\sqrt{2},
$$

and let it vanish on the complementary six-dimensional space. It has Hilbert–Schmidt norm 1, connects one vector in $\operatorname{Ran}(P_i)$ to one in its orthogonal complement, and has zero diagonal blocks relative to $P_{i}$. Since $u_{ij}=\sqrt{2}\,\theta$, direct exponentiation gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
\exp(-iu_{ij}A_{ij})v_i &= \cos(\theta)v_i+\sin(\theta)w_{ij}=v_j, \\
\exp(-iu_{ij}A_{ij})e_0 &= e_0.
\end{aligned}
$$

Thus it transports $P_{i}$ to $P_{j}$. Because $\theta\le\sqrt{2}<\pi/2$, Lemma T.41.4 applies without crossing its cut locus, giving the claimed intrinsic distance.

For the second assertion define normalized real packets on R24 by

$$
\begin{aligned}
\psi_i(y) &= (\rho/(\pi\sigma^{2}))^{6} \\
&\qquad \exp[-\rho|y-\xi_i|^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})], \\
v_i^{\mathrm{osc}}(y,z) &= \psi_i(y)\psi_i(z),
\end{aligned}
$$

on two disjoint oscillator factors. Completing the square in the Gaussian integral gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
\langle\psi_i,\psi_j\rangle &= \exp[-\rho|\xi_i-\xi_j|^{2}/(4\sigma^{2})], \\
\langle v_i^{\mathrm{osc}},v_j^{\mathrm{osc}}\rangle &= \exp[-\rho|\xi_i-\xi_j|^{2}/(2\sigma^{2})] \\
&= \exp[-\rho q_{ij}/(16\sigma^{2})].
\end{aligned}
$$

This is a two-copy amplitude. The Born covariance of one packet is $\sigma^{2}/(2\rho)$ times the identity; for $\rho=1$ it is half the statistical calibration covariance $\sigma^{2}I_{24}$ of Theorem T.41.1 through Theorem T.41.5.

For completeness, a Gaussian kernel on any finite set of distinct Euclidean centers is strictly positive definite. With $t=\rho/(2\sigma^{2})>0$, its quadratic form has the Fourier representation

$$
\begin{aligned}
z^{*}Gz &= (4\pi t)^{-12}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{24}} \\
&\qquad \exp[-|k|^{2}/(4t)]\left|\sum_iz_i\exp(ik\cdot\xi_i)\right|^{2}dk.
\end{aligned}
$$

If this quantity vanishes, the continuous nonnegative integrand vanishes everywhere. Choose a vector $v$ for which the three numbers $v\cdot\xi_i$ are distinct; the excluded $v$ lie in finitely many proper hyperplanes. Restrict the exponential sum to $k=sv$ and differentiate at $s=0$ through orders 0, 1, 2. The resulting Vandermonde system is invertible, forcing all $z_{i}=0$. The root vectors themselves need not have rank 3.

On the two stiffness programs, every off-diagonal entry is at most $\exp(-\sqrt{3})$: indeed $q_{ij}\ge 2$, $\sigma^{2}=1/24$ and $\rho\ge 1/\sqrt{3}$. For any complex $z$, $2|z_i||z_j|\le|z_i|^{2}+|z_j|^{2}$ gives

$$
z^{*}Gz\ge(1-2\exp(-\sqrt{3}))\sum_i|z_i|^{2}.
$$

Positivity of the displayed constant follows already from $\exp(\sqrt{3})>1+\sqrt{3}>2$. This also bounds each Gram–Schmidt squared residual from below by $m$: such a residual is a Schur-complement variational minimum of the quadratic form with one coefficient fixed to 1.

Apply real Gram–Schmidt to the three profiles to obtain orthonormal $w_{a}$, and set $B_{ia}=\langle v_i^{\mathrm{osc}},w_a\rangle$. Each profile lies in their span, so $BB^{T}=G$. To specify the source-response meaning, install exactly three canonical sterile Weyl fields $N_{a}$ with a common nonzero mass $M$ and internal form factor $N(y,z)=\sum_a w_a(y,z)N_a$. Its internal anticommutator is the rank-three span projector, not a full $\delta$ function or an infinite degenerate sterile tower. The overlap coupling is

$$
\begin{aligned}
-y\sum_i\int dy\,dz\,v_i^{\mathrm{osc}}(y,z)(L_i\cdot H)N(y,z)+\mathrm{h.c.} \\
&= -y\sum_{ia}B_{ia}(L_i\cdot H)N_a+\mathrm{h.c.},
\end{aligned}
$$

together with the canonical kinetic action and $-(M/2)\sum_a N_aN_a+\text{h.c.}$. In the convention of Y.11.2g, exact Gaussian elimination at zero external momentum gives

$$
C_{ij}=-(y^{2}/M)(BB^{\mathsf T})_{ij}=-(y^{2}/M)G_{ij}.
$$

The full determinant/Pfaffian and finite-momentum inverse remain part of that action. The form-factor installation, complementary modes, physical coefficient/current instrument, source gains and finite implementation errors must be owned exactly as in the source grammar. On the source of Theorem Y.11.2g its rotor character fixes $\rho$ and M as stated there. The present result extends the geometric and overlap-factorization interface of that source; its certified response and invoice keep their own comparison universe. ∎

<a id="proposition-r51c"></a>

<a id="proposition-r51c-exact-obstructions-to-extending-the-nine-row-alignment-winner"></a>**Proposition R.5.1c (Exact Obstructions to Extending the Nine-Row Alignment Winner).** Retain the alignment angle map and positive stiffnesses of Definition T.24.9a, with charged reference $(q_{32},q_{31},q_{21})=(2,6,4)$. The following statements leave the nine-row Proposition T.24.9 and its physical realization in Theorem T.79.5g unchanged.

1. The excluded tuple $(2,6,2)$ has the same alignment score as the nine-row winner $(2,6,6)$, but has nonsingular Bures and Gaussian-profile realizations under Theorem R.5.1b despite its singular root Gram.
2. On all 36 ordered root-distance classes, the alignment functional itself has the unique minimizing distance tuple $(2,6,4)$, with score 0. It strictly beats $(2,6,6)$, whose score is $s\kappa_{12}>0$, where $s=(2-\sqrt{3})/4$. All root representatives within the minimizing Weyl orbit remain tied.

These statements concern the displayed alignment functional on the 36-class domain.

*Proof.* For the first tuple use the actual roots

$$
r_3=e_1+e_2,\quad r_2=e_1+e_3,\quad r_1=e_3-e_2.
$$

They satisfy $r_1+r_3=r_2$, so the root Gram has rank 2, while direct distances give $(q_{32},q_{31},q_{21})=(2,6,2)$. The angle map is $\theta(2)=\pi/3$, $\theta(4)=\pi/2$, $\theta(6)=2\pi/3$. Both $(2,6,2)$ and $(2,6,6)$ agree with the charged reference on their first two edges and differ from its last angle by $\pi/6$ in magnitude. Each therefore has score $s\kappa_{12}$.

For an explicit profile-rank check put $x=\exp[-\rho/(8\sigma^{2})]\in(0,1)$. The rival Gaussian Gram has two edges $x$ and one edge $x^{3}$. Its determinant factors as

$$
\begin{aligned}
1-2x^{2}-x^{6}+2x^{5} \\
&= (1-x^{3})(1+x^{3}-2x^{2}) \\
&= (1-x^{3})(1-x)(1+x-x^{2})>0.
\end{aligned}
$$

Theorem R.5.1b independently supplies its single-block Bures embedding. Root rank, profile rank and the number of physical sterile fields are therefore distinct admission conditions.

For the strictly better tuple one may use

$$
r_3=e_1-e_2,\quad r_2=e_1-e_3,\quad r_1=e_2+e_4.
$$

These roots give `(2,6,4)` and root Gram determinant 4. Its alignment score against itself is 0. Every score term is nonnegative. Since all stiffnesses are strictly positive and the angle map is injective on `{2,4,6,8}`, score 0 forces all three labeled distances to equal the reference distances. This proves uniqueness of its tuple on the complete 36-class domain, with the full Weyl orbit retained as the tie set. Its Bures and Gaussian realizations follow from Theorem R.5.1b.

Proposition T.24.9 and Theorem T.79.5g are consistent with these statements. The comparison class of Proposition T.24.9 carries the marked-A2 last-entry restriction, which excludes the zero-score tuple `(2,6,4)`, and the positive-root-Gram requirement, which excludes the tied tuple `(2,6,2)`. Theorem T.79.5g supplies a complete common-batch nine-row field, control and readout source with candidate-independent remainder R0 and the charged reference `(2,6,4)`; its unique winner and gap stand as proved there. Enlarging the admitted class changes the premise of that theorem, and neither the algebraic rivals nor their overlap realizations show that an enlarged physical batch has an unchanged total objective. ∎

<a id="lemma-r51d"></a>

<a id="lemma-r51d-center-independence-of-the-supplied-oscillator-localizers"></a>**Lemma R.5.1d (Center Independence of the Supplied Oscillator Localizers).** For fixed positive $\rho$, $\sigma^2$ and $E_{\mathrm{aux}}$, let

$$
h_\xi=E_{\mathrm{aux}}/2\,[-\sigma^{2}\Delta+\rho^{2}|y-\xi|^{2}/\sigma^{2}]
$$

on $L^2(\mathbb{R}^{24})$. Its spectrum, partition function and ground-state energy are independent of $\xi$; its local potential Hessian is also independent of $\xi$.

*Proof.* The translation $(T_\xi f)(y)=f(y-\xi)$ is unitary and $h_\xi=T_\xi h_0T_\xi^*$. The ground energy is $12E_{\mathrm{aux}}\rho$, gap $E_{\mathrm{aux}}\rho$, and for $\beta>0$

$$
\operatorname{Tr}\exp(-\beta h_\xi)=[2\sinh(\beta E_{\mathrm{aux}}\rho/2)]^{-24}.
$$

The potential Hessian is $E_{\mathrm{aux}}\rho^{2}/\sigma^{2}$ times the identity. Thus any center derivative of these oscillator eigenenergies or free energies vanishes. Two disjoint copies preserve the conclusion with their additive multiplicity. This lemma is restricted to the oscillator contribution: the full coupled field/form-factor apparatus and its preparation or controller can have center-dependent costs or energies. ∎

**Momentum-shell example.** A positive-energy auxiliary model shows which exclusion carries a lattice-shell selection. For three $E_8$-torus rotors with momenta $p_i\in E_8$, energy $c\sum_i|p_i|^{2}$, $c>0$, and total momentum 0, the unrestricted minimum is 0 at $(0,0,0)$. If only this triple is excluded, the minimum is $4c$, attained by permutations of $(r,-r,0)$ with $r$ a root. If each individual momentum is independently required nonzero, the minimum is $6c$, attained exactly by root triples with sum 0. The last triples have pairwise inner product $-1$, hence distance tuple $(6,6,6)$, and form 13440 ordered representatives: there are 240 choices for the first root and 56 roots with inner product $-1$ for the second, while their negative sum fixes the third. These statements follow from the $E_8$ minimum nonzero squared norm 2; no triad-specific energy tariff is used.

The three minima show that the exclusion of zero in every role carries the selection. An exact momentum eigenstate is spatially uniform on its torus. A separately installed momentum-controlled oscillator $|y-gp|^{2}$ realizes auxiliary centers proportional to momentum, without the compact field reduction of Section R.5.1 or a map into $\Sigma_8$. The field-sector lattice is $\mathbb{Z}^7$, while the $E_8$ momentum lattice has rank 8. Moreover $(6,6,6)$ has equal center distances and fails the unequal-distance condition (R.13).

<a id="r52b-controlled-full-flag-centers"></a>

### R.5.2b Controlled Full-Flag Flavor Centers on the Selected Source

<a id="theorem-r51e"></a>

<a id="theorem-r51e-controlled-full-flag-center-realization-on-the-selected-seed"></a>**Theorem R.5.1e (Controlled Full-Flag Center Realization on the Selected Seed).**

**Source scope.** The source is an externally controlled, known-frame, normal finite-source branch. It retains the registered sector triple of Section R.5.2 with its full-singlet and family-charge conditions. It uses the selector and physical label record of Theorem T.42.1c, the topology and current provider of Theorem R.3.3b, a calibrated supplied-ready, full-support preparation, control and holding service, and the Gibbs-swap meter of Proposition 20c. All added controls, fields and records are separately paid.

#### R.5.2b Section 1. Statement and exact source scope

Fix the three distinct registered $q_{g}$ in $L_{\mathrm{light}}$ with family charges $a,-a,0$ and their full-singlet certificates. Let $\Sigma_8=U(8)/T_8$ carry the equal-simple-root Kähler metric $g$ of Section R.5.1, with orbit eigenvalues $\Lambda=\operatorname{diag}(7/2,5/2,\dots,-7/2)$. A $q$ labels a component of smooth maps $S^2\to\Sigma_8$. The construction below provides:

1. the flavor seed: the triad selected by the all-thirteen antisymmetry service of Theorem T.42.1c, with its selector, tie rule and physical labels;
2. for every one of its banks and each $q_{g}$, an embedded compact smooth collective-coordinate family $M_q=S^{56}$ inside $F_{q}$, a smooth physical center map $\ell_q$, a predeclared compact window in its smooth interior and an actual full-flag CAR readout;
3. the moment-map plus common $W$ potential (R.12), as the complete center-dependent part of a predictive, verification and update experiment under the declared service tariff, with a physically retained constant remainder;
4. one strict nondegenerate ambient center minimum in each registered window, and exact ideal full-flag distances $d_g^2=c_\Sigma q_{ij}$ with $c_{\Sigma}=1/100$;
5. finite fully paid selecting controllers whose output fields converge to these centers, including finite operational Hessian estimates with explicit error bounds.

As in Theorem R.5.1, the conclusion is one minimum per tagged window. The reference flags $p_{i}$ are placed by the declared constructor of R.5.2b Section 3, and the common term W of R.5.2b Section 6 is built from return tests to them, so the minimum of each window is its reference flag. Every bank of Theorem T.42.1c, every field factory and every outcome remains physical. The exact ideal centers and Hessians refer to the normal limiting response; finite lookups carry the operational Hessian estimates of R.5.2b Section 8.3. The heat, Higgs and current responses of Theorems T.79.5c–T.79.5g are unchanged.

*Proof.* Sections 2–10 below construct the source and prove items 1–5.

#### R.5.2b Section 2. The selection contract of Theorem T.42.1c

Use the complete admitted ordered-root and marking universe of Definition T.42 and Theorem T.42.1c. Its antisymmetric-outcome utility, all-root and all-thirteen batch, equivalence, lexicographic tie rule and physical label binding remain unchanged. In census order $(q_{32},q_{31},q_{21})$ its two maximizing orbits are (2,4,4) and (4,6,4); its tie rule selects (2,4,4), whose input to the Gram constructor of Theorem T.42.1c is $(q_{21},q_{31},q_{32})=(4,4,2)$. The selected tuple is distinct from the charged tuple (2,6,4) and from the supplied tuple (2,4,2).

The antisymmetric outcome of Theorem T.42.1c remains the scored outcome: no field outcome replaces it, no candidate-specific measurement is optimized, and no added bit or adaptive conditioning enters its utility. All field factories and center computations for all thirteen banks are executed and retained before nomination. Routing, field creation, actuators and exports do not depend on the nomination, which changes only the permitted output record of Theorem T.42.1c. Thus for each complete raw history the added physical source cost is the same for every nomination, even when different banks have different internal field outcomes, and the expected score and tie rule of Theorem T.42.1c are unchanged.

The center comparison inside a bank is a separate physical comparison, proved below; the cost equality across bank nominations does not enter it.

#### R.5.2b Section 3. Exact full-flag metric constructor, with all contexts retained

Write $\pi_{k}$ for the k-plane projection from the full flag. The equal-simple-root metric is

$$
g=\sum_{k=1}^{7}\pi_k^{*}g_{\mathrm{FS},k},
$$

where the Fubini–Study metric is in the real convention of Section R.5.1, $\omega=i\,\partial\bar\partial K$ and $g(u,v)=\omega(u,Jv)$. Consider $C=\mathbb{CP}^1$ obtained by mixing the first two coordinate lines, fixing their span and every higher plane. All $k\ge 2$ projections are constant on $C$. Every flag path has length at least the length of its first-line projection. Conversely a shortest $\mathbb{CP}^7$ geodesic between two lines in this fixed $\mathbb{C}^2$ stays in the $\mathbb{C}^2$ and lifts to $C$. Therefore

$$
d_{\mathrm{flag}}(p,p')=d_{\mathbb{CP}^{1}}(p,p')\quad\text{for every } p,p'\in C.
$$

The sphere radius is $R=1/\sqrt{2}$, and the equality holds for finite distances. In this convention $g$ is twice the usual pure-state Fubini–Study metric $\mathrm{QFI}/4$.

For every admitted root tuple $T=(q_{32},q_{31},q_{21})$, set $\epsilon=1/10$ and $a=\epsilon\sqrt{q_{32}}$, $b=\epsilon\sqrt{q_{31}}$, $c=\epsilon\sqrt{q_{21}}$. Distinct equal-norm roots give strict triangle inequalities. Each side is at most $\sqrt{8}/10$ and the perimeter is below $9/10<2\pi R$. Hence the strict spherical triangle exists. Put $p_3$ at the fixed north pole, $p_2$ at distance $a$ on the fixed meridian, and $p_1$ at distance $b$ with positive azimuth $\phi$ given by

$$
\cos\phi=[\cos(c/R)-\cos(a/R)\cos(b/R)]/[\sin(a/R)\sin(b/R)].
$$

The right side lies strictly between −1 and 1. These computable source functions give

$$
d_{\mathrm{flag}}(p_i,p_j)^{2}=\epsilon^{2}q_{ij}.
$$

The source retains its unit, frame, meridian and positive azimuth. The constructor is applied to one tuple at a time. On the selected tuple $(q_{32},q_{31},q_{21})=(2,4,4)$, $D_{31}\ne D_{32}$ and all three centers are distinct.

The marked representation of Theorem R.3.3b is $\operatorname{diag}(I_2,z^2A,z^{-3}B,1)$. Thus this $\mathbb{CP}^1$ rotates exactly the adjacent semisimple-singlet coordinates $e_1,e_2$ of that marked embedding. Explicit $U(2)$ matrices taking the north-pole flag $p_0$ to each $p_{i}$ are owned control records.

#### R.5.2b Section 4. Same-q compact field reduction

For each fixed $q$, take the based sphere representative $f_{q}^{0}$ of Theorem R.3.3b with Chern degrees $(k_1,\dots,k_8)=(q_1,\dots,q_7,-\sum q_i)$, made from the oriented $(e_i,e_8)$-plane $\mathbb{CP}^1$ spheres and constant necks. Reserve two disjoint disks on which it is exactly $p_0$. This reservation is obtained by shrinking all finitely many nonconstant constituent disks into the complementary belt before smoothing. The finite $q$ list, oriented disks, smoothing profiles and spatial coordinates are owned source descriptors.

A fully effective smooth choice uses the standard bump $b(t)=\exp(-1/t)$ for $t>0$ and 0 otherwise, with $\beta(t)=b(t)/(b(t)+b(1-t))$. On each constituent disk, a radial polar angle made from $\beta$ is constant near its center and boundary; its azimuth is the prescribed degree sign times the disk angle. The constant-ray portions remove the apparent polar singularity. A finite index-ordered list of rational disjoint disks produces every finite $q$. All derivative bounds are computable: derivatives of $b$ are finite sums of powers $t^{-m}\exp(-1/t)$, bounded by the elementary maxima of $u^m\exp(-u)$, with finite rescaling factors.

Let $A(v)$ be the off-diagonal anti-Hermitian matrix with real coordinates $v$ in $\mathbb{R}^{56}$ in the basis

$$
\begin{aligned}
A_{ij}^{R} &= (E_{ij}-E_{ji})/\sqrt{2(j-i)}, \\
A_{ij}^{I} &= i(E_{ij}+E_{ji})/\sqrt{2(j-i)},\quad i<j.
\end{aligned}
$$

At the identity flag this basis is $g$-orthonormal, and $\lVert A(v)\rVert\le\lVert A(v)\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\le|v|$. The local flag chart is $p(v)=\exp(A(v))p_0$.

The seven-projector map $P(v)=(P_1(v),\dots,P_7(v))$ has $|DP(0)u|_{\mathrm{HS}}=|u|$ and $\lVert D^2P\rVert\le 4\sqrt{28}<22$ on its domain, by the commutator bounds for unitary conjugation. Thus on $|v|\le 1/100$,

$$
|P(v)-P(w)|\ge(1-22/100)|v-w|>|v-w|/2.
$$

It is injective there, and its derivative is injective. This explicit bound also supplies a local readout inverse estimate.

For $X=(x_1,\dots,x_{57})$ in the unit $S^{56}$, set $v=10^{-3}(x_1,\dots,x_{56})$ and $w=10^{-3}(1-x_{57})$. On the first reserved disk use the actual flag

$$
\exp[\chi(s)A(v)]\,p_0,
$$

where $\chi$ is a fixed smooth cutoff equal to 1 near the disk center and 0 near its boundary. On the second disk use $\exp[\chi(s)\,w\,A_{12}^R]p_0$. Leave the $q$ scaffold unchanged elsewhere. Finally apply the bank/role $U(2)$ matrix $U_{i}$ to the whole field. Denote the resulting smooth actual map by $f_{q,i,X}$.

These are group-exponential radial controls; no gradient-energy formula is used for them. Their smooth homotopy obtained by scaling $v,w$ to 0 leaves $q$ unchanged. Their two center evaluations recover $(v,w)$ by the injective chart; this recovers $X$. Their derivative recovers every tangent of $S^{56}$. Hence $X\mapsto f_{q,i,X}$ is an injective immersion and, by compactness, an embedded compact smooth reduction $M_{q}$ of the actual $F_{q}$. This is the explicitly declared externally controlled preparation family.

Define $\ell_{q,i}(X)$ as the first disk's physical flag value $U_i\exp(A(v))p_0$. At the north pole $X_0=(0,\dots,0,1)$, $\ell=p_i$ and the map has rank 56. The second disk is a retained physical field coordinate; it distinguishes the south pole and other configurations sharing the first center. The source metric on $M_{q}$ can be the pullback of the two seven-projector embeddings, a positive smooth metric; at $X_0$ its first-disk part has the stated coordinate normalization.

The preparation-frame setting of Theorem R.3.3b is the branch $r=C_0$, a supplied known producer input, and its map context is $f=f_{q,i,X}$ itself. The charged oscillator of Theorem R.3.3b acts with its $q$, $C_0$ and all its instruments. The geometry controls read only this supplied frame.

#### R.5.2b Section 5. Full physical flag carriers and separating readout

At every spatial sample site, retain seven complete eight-mode CAR layers. Prepare k occupied orbitals of the flag's k-plane in layer k and retain all other Fock sectors. The product state is

$$
\Psi(p)=\bigotimes_{k=1}^{7}\wedge^{k}Q_k(p),
$$

with total occupation 28 and even parity. Arbitrary chosen basis phases disappear from its ray. The complete ray stabilizer is T8, so every one of the 56 flag directions is visible. The full carrier is the 56-mode Fock space.

The auxiliary geometric CAR registers are neutral under the physical gauge action of Theorem R.3.3b. The matrices of the marked semisimple algebra below are geometric-coordinate tests on those auxiliary registers; the gauge action, the Noether currents and the current of the charged oscillator stay on its own factor. This separation avoids unpriced charge-changing preparation. Every added gate preserves CAR parity. A source variant imposing additional charge conservation on these auxiliaries must add its reference and reservoir.

At a finite site a full even preparation unitary maps its ready occupation reference to the required product Slater state, with arbitrary completed odd-sector action. The sitewise reference frame and every added control word are owned; no global unitary lift of a nontrivial sphere map is used. On changed local patches the polar map $D=\sum_lP_l(v)P_l(0)$, $U=D(D^*D)^{-1/2}$ supplies a smooth local orbital preparation; on the fixed scaffold all sitewise frames and any chart transition records are supplied and paid.

The actual one-body CAR correlations satisfy

$$
\langle a_{k,j}^{*}a_{k,i}\rangle=(P_k(p))_{i,j}.
$$

The seven ordered projectors determine the full flag. Use the 64 Hilbert–Schmidt-orthonormal Hermitian coordinates per layer: diagonal occupations and off-diagonal combinations normalized by 1/sqrt2. Their CAR observables have norm at most 1. Measuring 448 such real coordinates per site on independent prepared repetitions gives

$$
\Pr(\text{any entry error}>\eta)\le 2\cdot 448\cdot N_{\mathrm{sites}}\exp(-n\eta^{2}/2).
$$

Every repetition, pointer, full outcome, failure and archive is paid. Coordinate error at most $\eta$ gives each layer Frobenius error at most $8\eta$. An explicit flag recovery avoids an unspecified tubular radius. Let $B(p)=\sum_{k=1}^7P_k(p)$; its eigenvalues are $7,6,\dots,0$ with gaps 1. The sum of seven estimated layer matrices has operator error at most $56\eta$, in addition to the separately bounded preparation bias. A Hermitian interpolant $\hat{B}(s)$ on the physical mesh has error

$$
e\le 56\eta+e_{\mathrm{prep}}+L_Bh_{\mathrm{mesh}},
$$

where $L_{B}$ is the effective uniform spatial derivative bound of the printed smooth scaffold/control family. Choose $e\le 1/10000$. Weyl's elementary bound then gives simple ordered eigenvalues with gaps at least 1−2e. Their ordered rank-one spectral projectors, accumulated into k-planes, define a genuine full flag.

For completeness, a radius 1/3 resolvent contour about each integer eigenvalue gives individual spectral-projector operator error at most $5e$ when $e\le 1/8$. With $D=\sum_i\hat{\Pi}_i\Pi_i$, $\lVert D-I\rVert\le 40e$. Its polar unitary transports the true ordered eigenlines to the estimated ones and obeys $\lVert U-I\rVert\le 80e$; its principal logarithm has norm at most $160e$. The invariant flag metric then bounds the flag distance by $160\sqrt{56}\,e<1280e$. These conservative bounds give an explicit physical readout modulus.

For topology, the straight Hermitian path $B_t=(1-t)B+t\hat{B}$ retains all eigenvalue gaps whenever $e<1/4$. Its ordered spectral flags give an explicit homotopy from the true $f$ to the reconstructed field. Use computable smooth interpolation weights, or smooth a piecewise interpolation inside the same strict error margin. Thus the good-event reconstructed field has the same $q$. The exact $q$ context remains the supplied charge-control input.

#### R.5.2b Section 6. The potential (R.12) as a finite experiment

Let $\mu(p)=U\Lambda U^\dagger$ and $F(p)=(\kappa_\mu/2)\lVert\mathrm{pr}_{\mathrm{ss}}\,\mu(p)\rVert^2$, with one independent positive objective unit $\kappa_{\mu}$. On the $\mathbb{CP}^1$ of R.5.2b Section 3,

$$
\mathrm{pr}_{\mathrm{ss}}\,\mu=M=\operatorname{diag}(0,0,1,0,-1,1/2,-1/2,0),
$$

so $F=5\kappa_\mu/4$ and $[\mu,M]=0$. Thus the full 56-dimensional differential of $F$ vanishes at every reference flag $p_{i}$. In the root-plane basis its full Hessian satisfies

$$
\operatorname{Hess}F\ge -2\kappa_\mu g.
$$

Indeed within color/weak planes $F$ is invariant; on a cross-block plane its quadratic coefficient is $2\kappa_\mu(m_i-m_j)(\lambda_j-\lambda_i)|A_{ij}|^2$, while $g$ gives $2(\lambda_i-\lambda_j)|A_{ij}|^2$. The range of the $m_{i}$ is 2. Spectator $U(2)$ transport preserves this calculation.

For the complete three-reference bank define

$$
\begin{aligned}
L_i(p) &= 1-|\langle\Psi(p_i),\Psi(p)\rangle|^{2}, \\
W(p) &= L_1(p)L_2(p)L_3(p).
\end{aligned}
$$

A physical return test has probability $\prod_k|\det(Q_{i,k}^*Q_{p,k})|^2$. On the common flag graph chart it equals $\exp(-K_{\mathrm{equal}})$. Consequently $\operatorname{Hess}L_i$ at $p_{i}$ equals $g$ in the real convention of Section R.5.1. All independent copies and both outcomes remain. $W$ is the probability of all three failures on three independently prepared full flag fields. At $p_{i}$,

$$
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Hess}W &= c_ig, \\
c_i &= \prod_{j\ne i}L_j(p_i)\ge\sin^{4}(1/10).
\end{aligned}
$$

On the $\mathbb{CP}^1$, $L_j(p_i)=\sin^2(d_{\mathrm{flag}}(p_i,p_j)/\sqrt{2})$. Use the rational coefficient $\Lambda_I=40000\kappa_\mu$, fixed in advance. Since $\sin(1/10)>99/1000$,

$$
\operatorname{Hess}(F+\Lambda_IW)|_{p_i}\ge\kappa_\mu g.
$$

The pinning matrix is $B_{q}=0$, a trace-zero diagonal matrix, hence $h_{q}=0$. The sector term $(\kappa_C/2)\lVert Cq\rVert^2$ is a retained constant. A singleton minimum manifold is a zero-dimensional Morse–Bott manifold, and its zero-dimensional pinning restriction has exactly one local minimum. Thus this is a branch of the formulation (R.12) and Theorem R.5.1.

Here are literal physical effects producing these terms. For a trace-orthonormal Hermitian basis $t_{\alpha}$ of the embedded $\mathfrak{su}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)$, put $T_\alpha=\sum_{k=1}^7d\Gamma_k(t_\alpha)$ on the full flag carrier. On its prepared state,

$$
\langle T_\alpha\rangle=\operatorname{Tr}[t_\alpha\sum_kP_k]=\operatorname{Tr}[t_\alpha\mu].
$$

Scalar shifts vanish because $t_{\alpha}$ is traceless. Each $\lVert T_\alpha\rVert\le 56$. On two independent full carriers let $D=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{11}T_\alpha\otimes T_\alpha$. With $b=11\cdot 56^2=34496$, the actual operator $A_\mu=D+bI$ obeys $0\le A_\mu\le 2bI$ and

$$
\langle A_\mu\rangle=\lVert\mathrm{pr}_{\mathrm{ss}}\,\mu\rVert^{2}+b.
$$

This is the two-copy expectation. On three independent carriers, $A_W=\bigotimes_i(I-\Pi_{p_i})$ obeys $0\le A_W\le I$ and $\langle A_W\rangle=W$. These complete operators retain their full Fock complements. Their added constant is physically paid and never erased from the invoice.

#### R.5.2b Section 7. Complete source invoice, causal task and exact tariff

Use a calibrated finite supplied-ready, full-support preparation, control and holding service, whose declared prices depend on supports, slot durations, waveforms, control norms and retention intervals, together with the Gibbs-swap meter of Proposition 20c in the mismatch form (29e). In the added computational, field-preparation and comparison windows the bare Hamiltonian is explicitly zero; positive control pulses, meter Hamiltonians and all work, heat and energy histories remain. A native-Hamiltonian observation window would carry its own invoice and is not part of this branch.

For each effect $0\le A\le BI$, its pointer unitary is

$$
U_A=\begin{bmatrix} \sqrt{I-A/(4B)} & -\sqrt{A/(4B)} \\ \sqrt{A/(4B)} & \sqrt{I-A/(4B)} \end{bmatrix}.
$$

It gives the complete outcome $Z$ with $p=\langle A\rangle/(4B)$. This fixed thinning convention retains all outcomes and guarantees predictive success at least 3/4. Every preparation, effect and reversible record operation is implemented on its full support by a positive tagged pulse. For a unitary $U$, $W_U=U\otimes|1\rangle\langle 0|+U^*\otimes|0\rangle\langle 1|$ satisfies $W_U=W_U^*$ and $W_U^2=I$; $H=\hbar\gamma(t)(I-W_U)$ is positive, and with $\int\gamma=\pi/2$ it implements $\exp[-i(\pi/2)(I-W_U)]=W_U$, which applies $U$ and flips the tag. This is the drift-tracked pulse of Definition W.6a.6 with $H_0=0$ and $A=(\pi/2)(I-W_U)$. On a fresh tag $\langle W_U\rangle=0$ is conserved, so the mean action is $\pi\hbar/2$, and the integrated norm action is $\pi\hbar$ by (W.6a.6.3), independently of the data and reference. The complete support, waveform, table, physical time, power, programme, tag and all stored history are paid.

For the causal packet prepare an independent fair bit $B_0$ with retained model $D_0=B_0$. Commit the forecast $B_0$ before revealing the field comparison $Z$. At verification the task is $B_0\oplus Z$. Retain forecast/task/correctness/model-update/outcome records. Before verification the field settings, references, seed, the source of Theorem R.3.3b, controllers and all their accessible archives are independent of $B_0$. $Z$ is produced by the field experiment, not read from the private bit. The memory-null comparator has no access to $D_0$/$B_0$ through those independent inputs. The task information is exposed only at its verification boundary. The private task is freshly independent at every packet; late records do not reveal a future packet's bit.

The Gibbs meter of Proposition 20c with gap E has samplewise delivered work

$$
W_{\mathrm{delivered}}=W_{\mathrm{III}}-EZ,
$$

with expected $W_{\mathrm{III}}$ independent of Z and the center. All ramps, Gibbs bath swaps, lowering strokes, spent baths and work-copy bits remain in the response. Their heat can depend on Z. No reset or free erasure occurs.

Choose one fixed physical horizon H, long enough to include the finite source batch of Theorem T.42.1c and a reserved service window for the added operations. At each grade all finitely many added operations have positive padded slots inside that window; their norms and prices may grow without bound with grade under the admitted finite service without minimum latency. The supplier clocks and histories of Theorem T.42.1c are not rescaled. For this fixed H and the full finite support inventory $C_{\mathrm{stock}}$, the PCE potential of Definition D.1 is exactly

$$
\begin{aligned}
V_{\mathrm{record}}(X) &= C_{\mathrm{stock}}/H-\sum_jW_{\mathrm{III},j}/H+\sum_jE_jZ_j/H, \\
\mathbb{E}[V_{\mathrm{record}}(X)] &= C+\sum_j[E_j/(4HB_j)]\langle A_j\rangle.
\end{aligned}
$$

Every effect is thinned as printed. Choose $E_\mu/(4HB_\mu)=\kappa_\mu/2$ and $E_W/(4HB_W)=\Lambda_I$. Then

$$
\begin{aligned}
J_q(X) &= C_q+F(\ell_q(X))+\Lambda_IW(\ell_q(X)) \\
&\quad +(\kappa_C/2)\lVert Cq\rVert^{2}.
\end{aligned}
$$

Every physical constant, including the b shift and all supplied stock, is included in $C_{q}$. All units are calibrated; energy, work and action are not added without their specified conversion. This is the complete center-dependent objective for this declared service.

At a finite grade one fixed controlled table $U_{\mathrm{all}}=\sum_x|x\rangle\langle x|\otimes U_x$ implements each operation for all setting words. It is installed once with its entire table/support price. Off-domain words have declared padded failure programmes. All setting words are prepared with the inherited full-register price, never erased, and every controlled identity uses its full slot/support. Consequently programme, support, holding, controller, bath and archive charges are independent of the setting word under the declared tariff.

The source retains all thirteen candidate banks before the nomination, including all their center-comparison controls and outputs. Common nomination cost follows pathwise, separately from the just-proved equality of the center-independent tariff remainder.

#### R.5.2b Section 8. Explicit window, comparison controller and uniform normal errors

All following bounds are independent of the chosen admitted bank, role and empirical data. Put $\kappa=\kappa_\mu$ and write $V(v)=F(U_i\exp(A(v))p_0)+40000\kappa\,W(U_i\exp(A(v))p_0)$, excluding only the physically recorded constant. At $v=0$, $DV=0$ and $D^2V\ge\kappa I$.

Here are explicit derivative bounds. The Hilbert–Schmidt norm of $\Lambda$ is $\sqrt{42}$. Unitary conjugation and its Duhamel derivatives give $\lVert D^n\mu\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\le 2^n\sqrt{42}$ on unit coordinate directions. Therefore

$$
\begin{aligned}
&\lVert D^{3}F\rVert\le 1344\kappa,\quad \lVert D^{4}F\rVert\le 5376\kappa, \\
&\lVert D^{2}F\rVert\le 336\kappa.
\end{aligned}
$$

On the full 56-mode carrier the coordinate generator has norm at most $56|v|$. The trace-norm $n$-th derivative of its conjugated rank-one coherent projector is at most $112^n$. Leibniz applied to the three failure factors then gives $\lVert D^nW\rVert\le 336^n$. Thus take

$$
\begin{aligned}
M_3 &= \kappa(1344+40000\cdot 336^{3})=1517322241344\,\kappa, \\
M_4 &= \kappa(5376+40000\cdot 336^{4}), \\
L_2 &= \kappa(336+40000\cdot 336^{2})=4515840336\,\kappa.
\end{aligned}
$$

These are conservative full-support bounds. Define the exact rational radius

$$
r=1/12138577930752=1/[8(1344+40000\cdot 336^{3})].
$$

On $|v|\le r$, Hessian variation is at most $M_3r=\kappa/8$, hence $D^2V\ge\kappa/2\,I$. The actual compact window $K_{q}$ is the north-hemisphere image in $M_{q}$ of the closed cube $|v_j|\le r/8$. It lies strictly inside the smooth hemisphere chart and inside $|v|<r$ because $\sqrt{56}<8$. The full continuous comparison domain is this cube. Strict convexity proves that $v=0$ is its only critical point, its only minimum and an ambient nondegenerate minimum of $M_{q}$. Quantitatively,

$$
V(v)-V(0)\ge\kappa|v|^{2}/4.
$$

This verifies all premises of Theorem R.5.1 on $K_q$: $C_q=\{X_0\}$, positive normal Hessian, no other $V_0$ critical point in $K_{q}$, and the zero-dimensional Morse condition for $h_{q}=0$. The three sector configurations are distinct by $q$; their physical center images $p_{i}$ are separately distinct by the exact flag distances. The conclusion concerns these declared windows.

The rational window is fixed by the source derivative bound before any flavor comparison. Its location is a deterministic function of the bank record selected by Theorem T.42.1c, and its radius and scale use no mass or mixing data. The same recipe and radius are installed for every admitted bank.

##### R.5.2b Section 8.1 Finite setting alphabet and complete selector

At grade $n\ge 1$ set

$$
\begin{aligned}
\delta_n &= \kappa r^{2}2^{-8n-12}, \\
m_n &= 8n+48, \\
h_{\mathrm{grid}} &= (r/8)2^{-m_n}.
\end{aligned}
$$

Take the full rational Cartesian grid of the cube, including 0, with this spacing. Its cardinality is $(2^{m_n+1}+1)^{56}$. Every setting controls the actual $f_{q,i,X}$ above, where $x_j=1000v_j$ and $x_{57}=+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{56}x_j^2}$; these are ordinary computable functions with a uniform positive square-root margin on the window. The setting word and every retained table have their actual full finite price. No finite precision or hardware bound uniform in $n$ is claimed.

For each requested setting the ideal physical effects and preparations have computable matrix entries from the finite scaffold descriptor, exponentials, trigonometric functions and finite matrix square roots. Functional calculus square roots on [0,1] have effective uniform polynomial approximations even at 0; no unproved spectral gap is required. Finite control/compiler errors are chosen after the actual list of support sizes and meter gaps is known. The whole fixed controlled table, including off-domain failure words and every classical selection step, is delivered and paid once at that grade.

The controller calculates outward intervals for the actual source-defined expected center potential with absolute error at most $\delta_n/4$. These intervals are derived from the same physical preparation/effect formulas that define the experiment. A fixed padded reversible exhaustive scan selects the smallest interval midpoint, breaking ties by the predeclared lexicographic setting-word order. Its full inputs, partial comparisons, interval records, output and work bits remain; none is erased. Its support, duration and programme are identical for every compared setting. It runs for every bank and role before the nomination of Theorem T.42.1c. The source publishes all bank center packets; the nomination identifies one already present packet and triggers no selective physical operation.

After the mandatory scan, every bank/role performs a fresh full field preparation at its selected setting and fresh physical (R.12) comparison and meter packets. These final packets occur for all banks before the nomination of Theorem T.42.1c. Counterfactual choices of the final setting share the same completed scan history and its complete paid cost. The fixed-table final preparation/control tariff is also identical. Fresh private bits, comparison outcomes and meter supplies are independent of the scan history conditional on the requested setting. Thus the actual conditional expected final source invoice is $C_{\mathrm{scan}}(\text{history})+C_{\mathrm{final}}+V(v)$, with all constants and the sector offset retained. The response is this fresh field and meter response. The source comparison is over final settings of this fixed mandatory-scan programme. Every meter gap is calibrated against the actual fixed total-run horizon $H$, including the padded computation/scan and final packets. If final tests are repeated, split their weights so their sum is exactly $V$, rather than multiplying the objective by the repetition count. The fixed $H$ is unchanged across grades. Each grade fixes its complete finite padded slot schedule inside the reserved service window; the required finite meter gaps/control norms are delivered and paid under the admitted ideal finite service, with no refinement-uniform power claim. The default final meter count and finite lowering-stroke count are fixed, so their physical gaps and bath parameters do not drift with grade.

This is the exact-expected-functional convention of the finite alignment source of Theorem T.79.5g; a single noisy realization need not have the expected winner. If empirical estimation rather than source-known interval evaluation is used, it must meet the separate confidence bound below. The fields and comparison instruments are present and may be queried.

Allocate the error budget so that the single combined midpoint-to-ideal error is at most $\delta_{n}$, including physical implementation, setting approximation and numerical interval evaluation. In particular the physical expectation error is at most $3\delta_n/4$ and its computed midpoint adds at most $\delta_n/4$. After subtracting the retained setting-independent constant for comparison only, the selected grid point $v_{n}$ therefore satisfies

$$
\begin{aligned}
V(v_n) &\le V(0)+2\delta_n, \\
|v_n| &\le \sqrt{8\delta_n/\kappa}.
\end{aligned}
$$

The first inequality follows by comparison with the grid point 0 and the two error bounds; the second is the proved quadratic gap. The same conclusion holds for any certified approximate minimizer with its additional excess divided by the gap constant. If an interval comparison is unresolved, the fixed tie rule or full unresolved cluster is retained with this same quantitative location bound; no false exact finite winner is reported.

On the window the seven-projector metric derivative is at most 2, so the physical selected ideal center obeys

$$
d_{\mathrm{flag}}(\ell(v_n),p_i)\le 2\sqrt{8\delta_n/\kappa}.
$$

Physical field preparation and tomography add their separately certified errors to this bound. Thus the output is a prepared field with a CAR center readout converging to $p_{i}$. For two roles with center errors $e_i,e_j$, the finite squared-distance discrepancy is at most

$$
2d_{\mathrm{flag}}(p_i,p_j)(e_i+e_j)+(e_i+e_j)^{2}.
$$

Its ideal limit has exactly the E8 certificate of Section R.5.2 with $c_{\Sigma}=1/100$.

##### R.5.2b Section 8.2 Finite source and setting-rounding budget

Freeze the thinning convention to $A/(4B)$ for every added packet. For the moment effect $B_\mu=2b$ and weight $\kappa/2$, and $W$ effect $B_{W}=1$ and weight $40000\kappa$, the sum of physical meter gaps per horizon is

$$
S=\sum_jE_j/H=4\kappa b+160000\kappa=297984\kappa.
$$

The complete declared benefit is delivered work alone, in the benefit term of Definition D.1; prediction correctness is the causal packet and meter trigger. The tasks of Theorem T.42.1c keep their own utility, and no energy, action or accuracy term is counted twice.

For a fixed compiled finite word, let $\epsilon_l$ be the operator-norm approximation error of its $l$-th full-support unitary. Telescoping with arbitrary references bounds the final channel diamond error by $2\sum_l\epsilon_l$. Consequently its weighted expectation error is at most $2S\sum_l\epsilon_l$. Allocate $\sum_l\epsilon_l\le\delta_n/(16S)$, leaving margin for interval arithmetic, setting rounding and any measurement error. This budget includes every preparation, effect, pointer, reference and finite coefficient programme, each with its own native norm.

For a continuous requested setting $v$ in the cube, round to the nearest grid setting, with a fixed boundary convention. Since $\lVert DV\rVert\le L_2r$ there, the change of ideal value is at most $4L_2r\,h_{\mathrm{grid}}$. With the printed $m_{n}$ and $L_2/\kappa<2^{33}$, this is below $\delta_n/16$. The continuous response therefore has a uniform value approximation after all allocated budgets are included. This is a normal family with increasingly fine finite descriptors.

The fixed spatial scaffold and all candidate maps have effective uniform derivative bounds. Choose a spatial mesh fine enough for its interpolation error and the physical preparation/tomography error to meet the remaining declared center/topology tolerance. A finite spatial mesh is not needed to calculate the center effects, because the first-disk center is itself an actual sampled site, but it is needed for the claimed field reconstruction. All mesh sites, full CAR layers and independent tomography repetitions are paid. The elementary bump derivative bounds and finite chart bounds give a terminating mesh/refinement algorithm for each requested tolerance.

If a response value is estimated experimentally, estimate the underlying Bernoulli probabilities on independent complete repetitions. For N requested values, each weighted response is a sum of two bounded outcome means with total weight S. Taking each probability error at most $\eta$ gives total value error at most S $\eta$. A conservative union bound is

$$
\Pr(\text{any probability error}>\eta)\le 4N\exp(-2N_{\mathrm{rep}}\eta^{2}).
$$

Choose $\eta\le\delta_n/(8S)$ and $N_{\mathrm{rep}}$ at least $\log(4N/\alpha_n)/(2\eta^2)$, with a predeclared summable $\alpha_{n}$, for example $2^{-n-4}$. The full repeated preparations, raw histories, Gibbs baths, work bits, controls and failures are included. This is a confidence statement. The source-known interval controller does not estimate a private outcome or condition on a successful subset.

##### R.5.2b Section 8.3 Operational Hessian

The limiting action V is the explicit analytic matrix/probability function already proved smooth. Finite rounded setting tables are not asserted differentiable. Set the actual comparison step

$$
h_n=(r/16)2^{-n}.
$$

All needed points $0$, $\pm h_ne_j$ and $\pm h_ne_i\pm h_ne_j$ lie in the cube and in the finite grid, because $h_n/h_{\mathrm{grid}}=2^{7n+47}$ is an integer. Form the diagonal centered and mixed four-point differences from their actual physically calibrated response values. If each response value has absolute error at most $\delta_{n}$, every resulting Hessian entry has error bounded conservatively by

$$
2M_4h_n^{2}+4\delta_n/h_n^{2}.
$$

The first term is the fourth-derivative Taylor remainder; the second is propagation of finite source/readout error. Both tend to 0, since $\delta_n/h_n^2=\kappa\,2^{-6n-4}$. If sampling is used this holds on the declared good event, with all failures retained. The finite-difference matrix converges in operator norm because the dimension 56 is fixed; entry error $\eta$ gives operator error at most $56\eta$. Once below $\kappa/4$ it certifies a positive Hessian margin.

The same construction calibrates the actual center metric and coordinate pullback using the physical seven-projector response. It supplies the PCE-action localization Hessian on the declared controlled source; mechanical oscillator frequencies and the fixed-mark heat and VVM determinants of Theorems T.79.5c–T.79.5f are separate quantities.

#### R.5.2b Section 9. Join with the source of Theorem R.3.3b

The source of Theorem R.3.3b has its known branch $r=C_0$ and the map context $f_{q,i,X}$. Its complete charged oscillator carrier, preparation, gauge actions and all finite-history instruments remain. Adjoin the supplied frame and scaffold records and the realized CAR field and meter hardware on both sides of its response isometry $J_{q}$. Use the explicit product initial source, or its recorded classical-context-controlled product. Every observable of that source is $A\otimes I$ and every instrument of it acts as its operation tensor identity. Partial trace over the added outputs recovers that source exactly on every input, reference and history in the chosen branch. The enlarged intertwiner is $J_q\otimes I_{\mathrm{new}}$, with identical added records and hardware on both sides.

The known C0 word is independently supplied before private inputs, and its preparation, storage and controls are paid; no hidden preparation frame is read. The CAR correlations make the map-context fiber of Theorem R.3.3b physically observable in the enlarged source; only the augmented equivalence applies to these added observables.

The physical gauge action of Theorem R.3.3b is $G_{\mathrm{phys}}$ tensor $I_{\mathrm{new}}$, which acts trivially on the f readout. Separately, the geometry registers admit programmed operations representing the marked coordinate algebra and any explicitly owned geometric frame transports. A diagonal joint geometry and gauge transport requires transporting and paying for the field, reference, scaffold, pinning and controller data; this fixed-frame branch does not include it. The geometric $T_{\alpha}$ are distinct from the Noether currents of the charged oscillator.

All private bits of the added packets are independent of the private-message tasks of Theorem T.42.1c and of the seed and field settings before their verification boundaries. The antisymmetric utility and allowed output of Theorem T.42.1c remain fixed. Full source graphs retain every experiment of the joined sources together with every added controlled-field and meter experiment; no response-active factor, failure, phase or context record or controller is erased.

The complete source thus selects the bank of Theorem T.42.1c without changing its nomination objective, and within that bank its continuous field-center comparison returns, in each registered q window, the reference flag of that window. The normal finite controllers, readout and Hessian bounds give finite source realizations of this comparison. All costs may grow with precision, and every finite member has finite paid resources.

##### R.5.2b Section 9.1 Common normal response interface

Declare fixed finite labelled prefixes of actual field-site correlation tests, positive-effect instruments, private P–V–U packets, final Gibbs-meter/bath/work outputs and instruments of the joined sources as the common response port. Every named spatial query site is included at all sufficiently fine grades; the source uses a nested site enumeration or explicitly retains the requested finite sites in its mesh. Labels name physical sites, layers, source roles, effects and response stages.

On each such prefix, $q_n(v)$ converges to $v$, the smooth prepared local field states converge in trace norm, and the complete implemented instruments converge in diamond norm by the stated uniform unitary budget. The fixed finite meter count, gaps, bath temperatures and lowering-stroke conventions give the same finite physical meter channels at every grade. Tensor-product preparation and finite composition then bound every prefix history distribution and bounded response expectation by the sum of these errors. The bounds retain arbitrary external references on the source of Theorem R.3.3b and on the added instruments. The selected settings themselves converge to the source-derived $p_{i}$ by the proved gap estimate, so the final selected-field prefixes have the same normal convergence.

The finite lookup tables, all tags, growing archives, instantaneous control peaks and complete absolute invoices remain exact grade-specific records, without a finite-hardware or finite-cost limit. The additive source constants are retained physically even when removed for comparison of center settings; only the center contrasts and fixed labelled responses have the stated normal limit. Optional growing repetition scans have their stated confidence and error interface.

##### R.5.2b Section 9.2 Relation to the fixed-mark kernel sources

The centers are points of the full flag and satisfy its exact finite-distance E8 certificate. Their rank-two plane is constant on the chosen CP1, so they differ from the rank-two Bures heat marks of Theorems T.79.5c–T.79.5f. The Higgs, current, fixed-mark heat-kernel, VVM and flavor-mass data of those theorems are attached to their own marks; a consumer that identifies them with these centers must supply its own map and error certificate. Current and kernel consumers keep their own interfaces.

The output packet contains: source/label bank identifier; all actual q labels; complete fixed frame and constructor records; the compact $M_{q}$ and window; physical field-to-flag map and readout; the full actual objective and invoice; limiting centers; exact $c_{\Sigma}$ and ordered root-distance tuple; positive Hessian and its operational finite error bounds; all remaining symmetry/frame ties; and the explicit downstream restrictions above.

#### R.5.2b Section 10. Verification and falsifiers

The construction fails if any of the following occurs: the charged context of the source of Theorem R.3.3b is replaced by an unrelated q label; a frame is read without an owned source; an omitted comparison, bath or control term depends on the center; the utility differs from the printed meter law; a future or private bit leaks through a setting declared independent; the utility of Theorem T.42.1c is enlarged; a nomination triggers an uncounted actuator; a moment variance is substituted for the two-copy square; a flag fiber is omitted from the readout; the group-exponential chart leaves its certified window; a field leaves q under its printed homotopy; uniform finite value errors fail to beat the stated gap; lookup derivatives are used as Hessians; or the rank-two heat and current responses of Theorems T.79.5c–T.79.5f are transferred to these centers without their own join.

The mathematical predictions are checkable from finite matrices and the printed analytic inequalities. The source assumes the declared supplied-ready service and the supplier scope of Theorems T.42.1c and R.3.3b. On that source and interface, the construction supplies the flavor seed, the reference flags with their exact E8 distances, one nondegenerate center minimum per registered window at its reference flag, and paid controllers whose prepared fields converge to these centers. ∎
