### R.5.2c Localization by a Root-Register Comparison

<a id="theorem-r51f"></a>

<a id="theorem-r51f-three-sector-full-flag-localization-from-a-common-root-register-law"></a>**Theorem R.5.1f (Three-Sector Full-Flag Localization from a Common Root-Register Law).** Take the three registered full-singlet sectors of Section R.5.2 and the all-thirteen root and physical-label source of Theorem T.42.1c. On an externally controlled, known-frame source with the full-flag preparation, readout and tariff of Theorem R.5.1e, a single root-register comparison law gives one nondegenerate localization minimum per registered sector window. The windows and geometric effects are common to the entire bank. On the nominated bank the minimizing full flags satisfy $d_\Sigma(p_i,p_j)^2=q_{ij}/100$ with $(q_{32},q_{31},q_{21})=(2,4,4)$. All thirteen banks use the same rule, retaining the seed's nomination and physical labels. The inputs to the objective are the source's root-register responses and candidate flag measurements. The common Ramsey coupling fixes the overall length scale $1/100$ in squared distance. The distance ratios, orientation-selected flags and sector Hessians follow from minimization. No measured mass, mixing angle or other fitted observable enters the construction.

*Common coordinates and actual field windows.* Use the simple-root $\mathbb{CP}^1$ of R.5.2b Section 3, with first-line Bloch vector $n\in S^2$, and write $p(n)$ for its full flag. Denote the common upper $k$-plane projectors by $E_k$, $2\le k\le7$. Let $Z$ run over the 54-dimensional real space of off-diagonal anti-Hermitian $8\times8$ matrices with $Z_{12}=Z_{21}=0$, in the Frobenius norm. The physical center map in each sector is $(n,Z)\mapsto e^Zp(n)$. It is a chart on the product of a hemisphere and $\|Z\|_F<1/100$. The higher projectors $e^ZE_ke^{-Z}$ recover $Z$. The derivative at zero of their ordered tuple has norm at least $\|Z\|_F$, since every allowed matrix entry crosses at least one cut $k\ge2$. The tuple's second derivative has norm at most $4\sqrt{27}$ by differentiating unitary conjugation. Integration along a segment gives an inverse Lipschitz bound on this ball. Once $Z$ is known, undoing it recovers $n$.

For role $i$, use the hemisphere $n\cdot e_i>0$, where $e_i$ are the three fixed Bloch-coordinate axes. Take the two constant disks and the smooth degree scaffold of R.5.2b Section 4. Apply a fixed singlet-plane unitary $W_i$ to the scaffold, sending the base line to the axis $e_i$. Write $z\in\mathbb R^2$, $|z|<1$, for stereographic coordinates from $-e_i$ on that hemisphere and use the smooth two-mode orbital lift $U(z)$ of the spinor $(1,z_1+iz_2)/\sqrt{1+|z|^2}$ in the $W_i$ frame. On the first disk the field is $e^{\chi Z} W_i U(\chi z)p_0$ with the radial cutoff $\chi$ of R.5.2b. In the second disk use $W_i e^{\chi w A_{12}^R}p_0$. To obtain a compact smooth carrier, take $X=(x_Z,x_z,x_{57})\in S^{56}$, set $Z=x_Z/100$, $z=4x_z$ using the same stereographic lift on the larger finite disk, and set $w=10^{-3}(1-x_{57})$. First- and second-disk evaluations recover all three coordinates and their tangent vectors by the chart estimate above, valid also on its closed $Z$-ball. Hence this is an embedded smooth $S^{56}$, with the first-disk map a chart on its north hemisphere. Scaling the two disk controls to zero gives a same-sector homotopy. The full-flag CAR readout, all its Fock complements, and the charged context and current of Theorem R.3.3b on the supplied-frame branch apply to these actual fields. Geometry auxiliaries are neutral as in R.5.2b Section 5.

Use a setting for three flags at once. A bank-independent setting space is the compact product

$$
\begin{split}
\Omega={}&\{C=C^T\in\mathbb R^{3\times3}: C_{ii}=1,\ C\succeq\gamma I\}
  \times\{A\in\mathfrak{so}(3):\|A\|_F\le\gamma/100\}\\
 &\times\prod_{i=1}^{3}\{Z_i:\|Z_i\|_F\le 1/1024\},\qquad \gamma=2^{-20}.
\end{split}
$$

Set $N=e^A C^{1/2}$ and let $n_i$ be its columns. They are unit vectors and $N_{ii}\ge\sqrt\gamma-\|A\|_F>\sqrt\gamma/2$. Each $(n_i,Z_i)$ therefore determines a point $X_i$ on the north chart of its sector, with $|x_z|\le1/4$ and $\|x_Z\|_F\le100/1024$. Write $\pi_i:\Omega\to S^{56}$ for this map and take the registered sector window $K_i=\pi_i(\Omega)$. These compact windows lie in the smooth north charts. The tuple of first-disk flags determines the $Z_i$ and $N$. The latter determines $C=N^TN$ and the small principal logarithm $A$. The same $\Omega$, sector maps and control frames apply to every bank.

*Root-register and flag comparisons.* For each bank use the retained classical candidate-coordinate words $r_i$ of Theorem T.42.1c. A separate paid coefficient compiler subtracts their signed entries and loads the resulting pulse; the ray preparations and their original comparator are unchanged. Any representative in the bank gives the same comparison law. For a fixed finite implementation use the first representative in the seed's source ordering. For an edge let $d=r_i-r_j$ act as a vector in the eight-dimensional complement of $|0\rangle$ on a nine-level register. With the single coupling $a=\sqrt2/10$, run the Ramsey pulse with integrated generator

$$
\tfrac{a}{2}\bigl(|0\rangle\langle d|+|d\rangle\langle0|+\sqrt8 I\bigr).
$$

It is positive on all nine levels. Its action starting at $|0\rangle$, followed by the complete observable $R=2|0\rangle\langle0|-I$, gives expectation $\beta_{ij}=\cos(a\|r_i-r_j\|)$ by exponentiating its two-dimensional active block. This pulse uses raw seed coordinates and one common coupling.

On an independent copy of the full first-layer CAR carrier in each of two sectors, use the declared setting's $e^{-Z_i}$ control on the auxiliary register. Take the three Pauli observables on the first-line singlet plane, extended by zero on its one-body complement and used as $d\Gamma$ observables. Their norms on the entire eight-mode layer are at most one, since their single-particle spectra are $1,-1,0,\ldots,0$. Their expectation vector is exactly $n_i$. The readout control uses the setting word, so it works for every candidate flag. On independent preparations the bond contrast $L_{ij}=\sum_b \sigma_i^b\otimes\sigma_j^b-R_{ij}$, with identities on the omitted factors, has expectation $C_{ij}-\beta_{ij}$ and norm at most four. The positive operator $16I+L_{ij}\otimes L_{ij}$ on two independent preparations has expectation $16+(C_{ij}-\beta_{ij})^2$. For $i<j$ the contrast $M_{ij}=\sigma_j^i-\sigma_i^j$ has expectation $N_{ij}-N_{ji}$, norm at most two, and $4I+M_{ij}\otimes M_{ij}$ gives the shifted square. The latter contrast uses the fixed Cartesian axes to fix orientation for every bank. On untouched preparations, the operator $k-d\Gamma(E_k)$ in layer $k$ is positive on the full Fock space and gives $k-\operatorname{Tr}(E_k e^{Z_i}E_k e^{-Z_i})$. It vanishes when the higher planes equal the common ones. The complete meter effects come from these positive bounded operators by the thinning rule of R.5.2b Section 7.

Use the full supplied-ready, full-support physical comparison service of R.5.2b Section 7. Give each squared bond and normal effect unit objective coefficient and each orientation effect coefficient $1/2$, in a common positive rate unit. For a positive operator $A_j$ bounded by $B_j I$ with coefficient $b_j$, set the Gibbs gap to $4H B_j b_j$ in that unit, use the full pointer with outcome probability $\langle A_j\rangle/(4B_j)$, and retain both outcomes and every work and bath record. The contribution is $\sum_j b_j\langle A_j\rangle$, including its constant shifts in the physical invoice. The fresh private-bit causal packet, zero bare Hamiltonian in the added control windows and fixed-horizon tariff are those of R.5.2b Section 7. Each orbital correction, root-pulse program, field preparation, effect and computation has a full-support tagged pulse on a padded setting-independent slot. The fixed controlled tables price all settings, including all inactive words and failed outcomes. The constant stock, baseline, work, holding, program and meter terms are included in the comparison. Keep the three registered charge-sector responses, their separate source invoice and their constant sector terms as in Theorem R.5.1e. Every bank's comparison and field preparation belongs to the all-thirteen batch before nomination. The antisymmetric score, tie rule and label record read their original data. Thus the center-dependent objective in the declared rate unit is

$$
J(C,A,Z)=\sum_{i<j}(C_{ij}-\beta_{ij})^2
  +\tfrac12\sum_{i<j}(N_{ij}-N_{ji})^2
  +\sum_{i,k\ge2}\bigl(k-\operatorname{Tr}(E_k e^{Z_i}E_k e^{-Z_i})\bigr).
$$

*Unique interior joint minimum.* Let $B$ have diagonal one and off-diagonal $\beta_{ij}$. Write $u\le v\le w$ for the three angular sides $a\sqrt{q_{ij}}$, and $s=(u+v+w)/2$. Distinct equal-norm roots give strict triangle inequalities. The sorted unscaled distances have smallest possible triangle margin $2\sqrt2-\sqrt6>1/3$. If the smallest is at least two, the margin is at least $4-\sqrt8$. If only the smallest is $\sqrt2$, it is at least $2-\sqrt2$. If the smallest two are $\sqrt2$, strictness bounds the largest by $\sqrt6$. Thus $s-w>1/50$, $s-u,s-v\ge1/10$, $s\ge3/10$, and $s\le3/5$. The determinant identity from Theorem R.5.1b, now with these angular sides, gives

$$
\det B=4\sin s\sin(s-u)\sin(s-v)\sin(s-w)\ge3/200000.
$$

Here $\sin t\ge t/2$ on $[0,3/5]$. The two-by-two principal minors are positive. With $\operatorname{Tr}B=3$, its smallest eigenvalue is at least $\det B/9\ge1/600000>\gamma$. This holds uniformly for all thirteen banks, including the singular original root-Gram cases.

For fixed $C$, write $S=C^{1/2}$ and $Q(A)=\tfrac14\|e^A S-S e^{-A}\|_F^2$ for the orientation term. At zero, $DQ=0$, and $D^2Q(0)[T,T]=\tfrac12\|TS+ST\|_F^2\ge2\gamma\|T\|_F^2$, by diagonalizing $S$. On the real skew space, the Frobenius norm of every derivative through order three of $e^A S-S e^{-A}$ on unit directions is at most $2\sqrt3$. This follows from the simplex formula for derivatives of unitary exponentials and $\|S\|_F=\sqrt3$. Differentiating the squared norm gives $\|D^3Q\|\le24$. The chosen ball thus has $D^2Q\succeq\gamma I$. For the one-role normal term $G(Z)=\sum_{k=2}^7(k-\operatorname{Tr}(E_k e^ZE_ke^{-Z}))$, the Hessian at zero is $2\sum_k\|(I-E_k)ZE_k\|_F^2\ge\|Z\|_F^2$. Its third derivative is bounded by $8\sum_{k=2}^7 k=216$ by differentiating the conjugation and taking the trace against $E_k$. Hence $D^2G\succeq I/2$ throughout its ball.

The orientation and normal variables strictly decrease the objective along the segment toward zero unless already zero, including at the boundary. With those variables zero, the segment from $C$ to $B$ strictly decreases the bond sum unless $C=B$. The segment is inside the convex Gram window. Thus the joint minimum $C=B,A=0,Z_i=0$ is unique, including among local minima relative to the compact domain. Its Hessian is positive definite. At $A=0$ the orientation term and its $A$-differential vanish for every $C$, so the bond Hessian, orientation Hessian and flag-normal Hessians are its separate positive blocks.

*Individual sector objectives and the distance certificate.* For each role use the profiled objective $V_i(x)=\min\{J(y):y\in\Omega,\ \pi_i(y)=x\}$ on $K_i$. This compares all compatible settings of the other physical lanes with lane $i$ held fixed. A constrained local minimum of $V_i$ lifts to a joint local minimum of $J$ at any minimizing point in its fiber, since $J\ge V_i\circ\pi_i$ and the projection is continuous. The preceding result gives uniqueness. At the minimizing joint point $\pi_i$ is a submersion. Varying $A$ spans the two Bloch tangents via $Tn_i$, and varying $Z_i$ supplies all 54 transverse flag tangents. The joint minimum is interior. The implicit function theorem in product coordinates adapted to $\pi_i$ gives the smooth minimizing fiber branch near it. This branch is the global fiber minimum nearby. Any sequence of different minimizing settings on approaching fibers would, by compactness and comparison with the smooth branch, converge along a subsequence to the same unique joint minimizer. The implicit function theorem rules out a second fiber critical point there. The sector Hessian is the positive Schur complement of the joint Hessian. Each sector therefore has exactly one window minimum and it is ambient and nondegenerate.

At the minimum $n_i\cdot n_j=B_{ij}$, so the angle between the two Bloch vectors is $a\sqrt{q_{ij}}<\pi$. The exact flag distance on this $\mathbb{CP}^1$ is its spherical distance at radius $1/\sqrt2$, as proved in R.5.2b Section 3. Consequently $d_\Sigma(p_i,p_j)^2=(a^2/2)q_{ij}=q_{ij}/100$. In the normalized squared-distance convention of Section R.5.2, $D_{ij}=100d_\Sigma(p_i,p_j)^2=q_{ij}$. The nominated T.42.1c bank has $D_{31}=4>D_{32}=2$ and $D_{21}=4$. Its generation labels and the family-charge conditions are those of the source.

*Finite realization.* Exhaust rational setting meshes in the fixed domain and select the smallest measured $J$, recording all trials and ties. Rational points in the shape window can be made effectively dense by first mixing with $I$, then rounding the three off-diagonal entries within the resulting explicit eigenvalue margin. Balls admit the corresponding inward-rounding meshes. All needed operations are finite matrix operations or smooth bounded field controls on these compacts. For example, evaluating $C^{1/2}$ by its convergent binomial series after scaling by $3I$ has a uniform geometric tail, since $\|I-C/3\|\le1-\gamma/3$. The positive-pulse, bounded-expectation and full-inventory estimates of R.5.2b Sections 7–8 apply to the displayed effects. Uniform preparation and meter errors can be made arbitrarily small with finite grade and arbitrarily high declared confidence, using bounded variables and a union bound over the finite settings and effects. At grade $n$, set the failure bound to $2^{-n-4}$ and retain every outcome. On the product of the grade histories, summability and the first Borel–Cantelli lemma give eventual simultaneous accuracy almost surely. The uniform estimates below then imply convergence of the selected settings and of bounded continuous normalized responses in expectation. A convergence gap follows from

$$
J-J_*\ge\sum_{i<j}|C_{ij}-B_{ij}|^2+\tfrac\gamma2\|A\|_F^2+\tfrac14\sum_i\|Z_i\|_F^2.
$$

The bound follows by integrating the fiber Hessians. Compare the measured optimizer with a nearest setting mesh point and use this bound to control the output distance. For a profile value at fixed $x\in K_i$, retain the whole measured mesh and use settings whose projected readout is within a declared tolerance of $x$ in the ordered-projector norm. Choose the tolerance to tend to zero more slowly than the uniform mesh and readout errors. A setting nearest a fiber minimizer supplies the upper limit, by the uniform continuity of $\pi_i$ and $J$. Compactness gives the lower limit, because any accumulation point of selected settings lies in the exact fiber. This implements the conditioned comparison with the same full retained inventory. For an operational Hessian, evaluate the actual expectation at finite-difference stencil settings in an interior neighborhood of the selected joint setting. Setting and expectation errors $o(h^2)$ at step $h$, with the bounded third derivatives there, give convergent central second differences. The sector Hessians follow by the submersion coordinate change and Schur complements. Their inverted blocks are positive at the limit. This is a normal finite-source limit with separately paid precision grades. ∎

### R.5.3 Yukawa Couplings from Gaussian Overlaps

**Gaussian Localization:**

Near each generation vacuum, the reduced potential has a quadratic Hessian. The overlap model below separately registers Gaussian profiles. On an isotropic local branch write
$$
\psi_g(r)\propto\exp\!\left(-\frac{\kappa}{2}\|r-r_g\|^2\right)
=\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|r-r_g\|^2}{2\xi^2}\right),
\qquad
\xi^2=\kappa^{-1}.
$$
Here $\kappa$ is an inverse-width parameter. It is not determined by the potential Hessian without a kinetic normalization, mass scale, and $\hbar$ convention. The Gaussian maximum-entropy property is compatible motivation for this ansatz, not a derivation of its quantum ground-state width.

**Higgs Alignment:**

This model convention places the Higgs profile at the point labelled $r_3$ and uses the corresponding diagonal kernel as the normalization anchor. The empirical top Yukawa motivates that convention but does not derive the label or prove that the physical heaviest singular vector is aligned with it. Such an identification requires Theorem T.39's independent full-matrix, label, and matching certificate.

**Overlap Integrals:**

On the registered flat eight-dimensional flavor-coordinate branch, take equally normalized generation profiles and a Higgs profile
$$
\psi_g(r)=N_a\exp\left(-\frac a2\|r-r_g\|^2\right),
\qquad
\phi_H(r)=N_b\exp\left(-\frac b2\|r-r_3\|^2\right),
$$
with $a,b>0$. Completing the square in
$$
K_{gg'}
:=
y_0\int_{\mathbb R^8}d^8r\,\psi_g(r)\phi_H(r)\psi_{g'}(r)
$$
gives the exact pre-realization model kernel
$$
K_{gg'}
=
K_0
\exp\left[
-\frac{ab}{2(2a+b)}\bigl(D_{g3}+D_{g'3}\bigr)
-\frac{a^2}{2(2a+b)}D_{gg'}
\right].
\tag{R.15}
$$
The symbol $Y_f$ is introduced only by Theorem T.39's accepted matrix assembly.
Hence the two coefficients are not independent:
$$
\alpha_1=\frac{ab}{2(2a+b)},
\qquad
\alpha_2=\frac{a^2}{2(2a+b)},
\qquad
\frac{\alpha_2}{\alpha_1}=\frac ab.
$$
For wavepackets transplanted to the curved flag target, write the certified result as the right-hand side of (R.15) multiplied by $(1+\rho_{gg'})$, with a certified bound independent of the compared mass data $|\rho_{gg'}|\le\epsilon_G<1$. Lemma T.41.4 gives an exact distance conversion only for root pairs explicitly listed in its single-block certificate $\mathfrak C_{B/E_8}$. The flag-target transplantation, finite-triad comparison, and residual bound remain the separate obligations of $\mathfrak C_{E_8}$; neither certificate may be substituted for the other.

### R.5.4 The ξ-Free Mass Invariant

<a id="definition-r51-diagonal-model-kernel-invariant-and-physical-gate"></a>**Definition R.5.1 (Diagonal Model-Kernel Invariant and Physical Gate).** Assume the registered Higgs anchor of Section R.5.3, $A=ab/(2a+b)>0$, $K_0\ne0$, and $D_{31},D_{32}>0$. Let the diagonal curved-target residuals be real and satisfy $|\rho_{gg}|\le\epsilon_G<1$. On the branch where the denominator is nonzero, define
$$
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{model}}
:=\frac{\log(K_{33}/K_{11})}{\log(K_{33}/K_{22})}.
\tag{R.16}
$$
Approximate diagonal dominance does not identify this ratio with a physical mass invariant. That identification requires an accepted Theorem-T.39 certificate for the same kernel verifying exact diagonal alignment after matching, pairwise ordered singular values, physical labels and the residual intervals.

The diagonal specialization of (R.15) is
$$
K_{gg}=K_0e^{-AD_{g3}}(1+\rho_{gg}),
\qquad K_{33}=K_0(1+\rho_{33}).
$$
All kernel ratios in (R.16) are positive because the common nonzero factor cancels and $1+\rho_{gg}>0$. Put
$$
\varepsilon_g:=\log\frac{1+\rho_{33}}{1+\rho_{gg}},
\qquad g=1,2.
$$
Taking real logarithms gives
$$
\log\frac{K_{33}}{K_{gg}}=AD_{3g}+\varepsilon_g,
\qquad
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{model}}
=\frac{AD_{31}+\varepsilon_1}{AD_{32}+\varepsilon_2}.
\tag{R.17}
$$
On the accepted physical gate, this equals $\log(m_3/m_1)/\log(m_3/m_2)$.

For a uniform error estimate define
$$
E_G:=\log\frac{1+\epsilon_G}{1-\epsilon_G},
\qquad x:=AD_{31}>0,\qquad y:=AD_{32}>0.
$$
The residual bounds imply $|\varepsilon_g|\le E_G$. If $y>E_G$, every allowed denominator is positive and
$$
\left|\mathcal R_{\mathrm{model}}-\frac{x}{y}\right|
=\frac{|y\varepsilon_1-x\varepsilon_2|}
{y|y+\varepsilon_2|}
\le\frac{E_G(x+y)}{y(y-E_G)}.
$$
This provides a bounded interval around $D_{31}/D_{32}$. A merely nonzero realized denominator does not supply a uniform interval: when $y\le E_G$, the residual bounds permit denominators arbitrarily close to zero. A separate denominator-separation certificate or more restrictive residual correlations are then required.

**Scope.** Exact equality to the distance ratio holds precisely when
$$
D_{32}\varepsilon_1=D_{31}\varepsilon_2,
$$
with a nonzero denominator. This includes the flat Gaussian branch and the case of vanishing logarithmic residuals, and is the proportional-residual reading for a physically accepted Dirac sector. The Majorana neutrino triad $T_\nu=(2,6,6)$ uses the separate Takagi--Weyl/alignment and anchored construction of Theorem T.24.11; its $1\leftrightarrow2$ $A_2$ edge belongs to PMNS mixing geometry.

**Finite model set on the exact triad branch.** If $\mathfrak C_{E_8}$ is accepted, $\varepsilon_1=\varepsilon_2=0$ and $D_{31}>D_{32}>0$, then the root-distance values $\{2,4,6,8\}$ give
$$
\boxed{\mathcal R_{\mathrm{model}}\in
\left\{\frac43,\frac32,2,3,4\right\}}.
\tag{R.18}
$$
The common metric scale, Gaussian normalization and coefficient $A$ cancel. Nonzero residuals replace this finite set by a certified bounded interval only when the denominator is uniformly separated from zero as above. Membership of a physical mass invariant in the finite set additionally requires the accepted Definition-R.5.1/Theorem-T.39 gate.

### R.6.1 Observational Data and Conditional Model Comparisons

The discrete distance-ratio set is a mathematical candidate set. Assigning one element to charged leptons or quarks is a model-selection step, and the fourth-order flavor equations retain the open coefficient, effective-dimension, scheme, and remainder entries of Appendix T. Numerical proximity on the data used to select a pair measures calibration of the chosen model.

This section compares conditional flavor diagnostics with quoted experimental inputs. The charged-lepton calculation below uses the historical central mass inputs in Workman et al. (2022, lepton summary, pp. 1–2). Its invariant $\mathcal{R}$ is compared with the discrete $E_8$ candidate set under the model and physical-realization qualifications of Section R.6.1.

**Charged Lepton Sector:**

For this charged-lepton model comparison, use the Particle Data Group 2022 central mass inputs:
- $m_e = 0.51099895$ MeV
- $m_\mu = 105.6583755$ MeV  
- $m_\tau = 1776.86$ MeV

Computing the invariant:
$$
\frac{m_\tau}{m_e}=3477.228280\ldots,
\qquad
\ln(m_\tau/m_e)=8.153991\ldots,
$$
$$
\frac{m_\tau}{m_\mu}=16.817029\ldots,
\qquad
\ln(m_\tau/m_\mu)=2.822392\ldots,
$$
and therefore
$$
\mathcal R_\ell
=\frac{8.153991\ldots}{2.822392\ldots}
=2.889036\ldots.
\tag{R.19}
$$

**Comparison with the $E_8$ discrete candidate set:**
- Closest candidate value: $\mathcal R=3$
- Fractional difference: $|2.8890355-3|/3\approx3.70\%$

An explicit $E_8$ triad realizes $(d_{31}^2,d_{32}^2)=(6,2)$: choosing a half-integral root $r_3$, flipping six signs gives inner product $-1$ and distance $6$, while flipping two signs gives inner product $+1$ and distance $2$. Thus the candidate ratio $6/2=3$ is geometrically realizable.

**Assessment:** The observed value is close to one member of a five-value candidate set. Because the member, triad assignment, response law, and higher-order flavor entries are not selected independently of the comparison in this section, the result is calibration evidence rather than a zero-parameter first-principles prediction.

**Quark Sector Predictions:**

For up-type quarks: $\mathcal{R}_u = \ln(y_t/y_u)/\ln(y_t/y_c)$ should match a discrete value from the set $\{4/3, 3/2, 2, 3, 4\}$.

For down-type quarks: $\mathcal{R}_d = \ln(y_b/y_d)/\ln(y_b/y_s)$ should match a discrete value from the same set.

The quark sector analysis is complicated by QCD running effects and the difficulty of extracting Yukawa couplings from physical masses. Preliminary estimates suggest:
- Up quarks: $\mathcal{R}_u \approx 2-3$ (between discrete values 2 and 3)
- Down quarks: $\mathcal{R}_d \approx 1.5-2$ (near discrete value 3/2 or 2)

Further precision requires detailed analysis of renormalization group evolution and threshold corrections.

### R.6.2 Mixing Angles and CKM Matrix

Within a fully specified mass-matrix texture, off-diagonal entries affect the left diagonalizers and hence the physical mixing angles. On the flat Gaussian branch of (R.15) with $K_0\ne0$, the common kernel prefactor gives the exact leading entry-ratio proxy
$$
r_{12}^{(0)}:=\left|\frac{K_{12}}{K_{22}}\right|
=e^{-E_{12}},
\qquad
E_{12}:=\alpha_1(D_{13}-D_{23})+\alpha_2D_{12}.
$$
Here $D_{ij}$ are the normalized squared root distances used in (R.15). The exponent follows by subtracting the diagonal exponent $2\alpha_1D_{23}$ from the off-diagonal exponent $\alpha_1(D_{13}+D_{23})+\alpha_2D_{12}$. Thus $r_{12}^{(0)}<1$ exactly when $E_{12}>0$, and
$$
0.01\le r_{12}^{(0)}\le0.1
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\ln10\le E_{12}\le\ln100.
$$
Order-one distances and coefficients alone determine neither the sign nor this interval. Of the illustrative CKM magnitudes $0.22$, $0.004$ and $0.04$, only $0.04$ lies in $[0.01,0.1]$. Identifying a physical angle with this entry-ratio proxy requires the accepted matrix texture, its diagonalization and a quantitative approximation-error certificate.

For three declared proxy laws $r_{ij}^{(0)}=e^{-E_{ij}}$, the inequalities $E_{13}>E_{23}>E_{12}>0$ imply $0<r_{13}^{(0)}<r_{23}^{(0)}<r_{12}^{(0)}<1$. Ordering selected pairwise distances does not establish that exponent ordering when each exponent contains several distances and coefficients. A physical CKM hierarchy additionally requires normalization, labels and errors controlled strongly enough to preserve the ordering after full matrix diagonalization.

**PMNS vs. CKM:**

The same entry model can accommodate larger PMNS than CKM mixing if the complete lepton and quark textures, labels, phases, and response coefficients yield the required singular-vector rotations. Smaller selected separations or coefficients are compatible mechanisms for that contrast.

## R.8 Discussion and Limitations

### R.8.1 What the Framework Establishes and What It Models

Theorem R.3.4 establishes the minimal admissible three-generation pattern on its branch-resolved SM15 and primitive-minimal-norm SM16 classes, and Proposition R.3.5.1a supplies the exact pre-flavor realization on its stated additive-monotone branch. The $D_4$, $E_8$, and Leech records are compatibility structures.

The flavor layer is conditional. Root distances provide candidate discrete diagnostics only after physical labels are selected. Exponential hierarchy, CKM/PMNS separation, CP phases, and the coefficient $\alpha=3/2$ require the particular overlap, potential, Schur, holonomy, scale, and remainder certificates stated in Appendix T. They are mechanisms or calibrated model outputs.

The model-independent statement about CP is that, within the ordinary three-family mixing formalism, a nonzero Jarlskog invariant requires at least three generations. Its magnitude and phase are flavor-model inputs.

The theorem/model status is:

1. **Family count:** Theorem R.3.4 proves minimal admissibility on the SM15 anomaly branch and the primitive-minimal-norm SM16 branch, both with a CP-active realization; Proposition R.3.5.1a adds the additive-monotone realized-count branch.
2. **Triality and lattice records:** $D_4$, $E_8$, and Leech structures are compatibility or realizability statements.
3. **Mass hierarchy and ratio catalog:** These follow within a selected localization and Gaussian-response model.
4. **Mixing angles and CKM/PMNS contrast:** These require sector labels, mass matrices, response maps, and normalization certificates.
5. **CP:** Three families permit a physical Jarlskog phase; nonzero magnitude requires nonzero phase and mixing factors.
6. **Coefficient $\alpha=3/2$:** This is conditional on the packet-width and Schur-normalization branch of Corollary T.41.3.

The framework does not yet fully determine:

Absolute values additionally require the full heat-kernel normalization, Van Vleck and remainder records, physical labels, common-scale matching, and the full-matrix realization certificate of Theorem T.39

4. **Running to low energies:** Requires RG evolution (standard QFT)

Item 3 remains conditional on the assigned lift, response map, and accepted realization certificates. Items 1–2 require an explicit PCE potential together with the stated localization, embedding, kernel, scale, and full-matrix certificates.
