<a id="theorem-r82a-covariant-quadratic-curvature-response-kernel"></a>**Theorem R.8.2a (Covariant Quadratic-Curvature Response Kernel).** Let $g$ be a smooth Lorentzian metric of signature $(-+++)$, let $G_N>0$ and $\beta\in\mathbb R$ be spacetime constants, and use the local quadratic-curvature action
$$
\Gamma_{\mathrm{grav}}[g]
=\frac1{16\pi}\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}
\left(\frac R{G_N}+\beta R^2\right).
$$
Use compactly supported metric variations, or boundary data that remove the variation's boundary terms. Let $T_{\mu\nu}$ be the retained matter stress tensor with its usual metric-variation normalization and with no independent non-conserved gravitational source. The metric equation is
$$
\left(\frac1{G_N}+2\beta R\right)R_{\mu\nu}
-\frac12\left(\frac R{G_N}+\beta R^2\right)g_{\mu\nu}
-2\beta(\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu-g_{\mu\nu}\Box_g)R
=8\pi T_{\mu\nu}.
\tag{R.8.2a.1}
$$
Its geometric left side has identically vanishing covariant divergence. In four dimensions its trace is
$$
-\frac R{G_N}+6\beta\Box_gR=8\pi T,
\qquad T:=g^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}.
\tag{R.8.2a.2}
$$
For a perfect fluid $T_{\mu\nu}=(\rho+p)u_\mu u_\nu+pg_{\mu\nu}$ with $g(u,u)=-1$, this specializes to $T=-\rho+3p$.

For a specified metric, register a linear scalar domain $\mathcal D$ and range $\mathcal E$, including the boundary or initial-data conditions, such that
$$
\mathcal A_g:=I-6\beta G_N\Box_g:\mathcal D\longrightarrow\mathcal E
$$
is bijective, and require the retained curvature scalar to lie in $\mathcal D$. For $T\in\mathcal E$ the trace response on this branch is
$$
R=-8\pi G_N\mathcal A_g^{-1}T.
\tag{R.8.2a.3}
$$
The scalar response operator and its inverse on these domains are
$$
G_{\mathrm{eff}}(\Box_g)=G_N\mathcal A_g^{-1},
\qquad
G_{\mathrm{eff}}(\Box_g)^{-1}=G_N^{-1}-6\beta\Box_g.
\tag{R.8.2a.4}
$$
If additionally $T\in\mathcal D$ and $\Box_gT=0$, then $R=-8\pi G_NT$. This exact harmonic-source statement requires compatibility with the chosen scalar domain; $\Box_gT=0$ alone does not eliminate homogeneous response modes or supply a controlled slow-variation approximation. An algebraic matter-dependent correction to an inverse coupling requires a separate nonlinear response certificate.

*Proof.* Put $f(R)=R/G_N+\beta R^2$. The metric variation of $R$ and integration by parts give
$$
f'(R)R_{\mu\nu}-\tfrac12f(R)g_{\mu\nu}
+(g_{\mu\nu}\Box_g-\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu)f'(R)
=8\pi T_{\mu\nu}.
$$
Since $f'(R)=G_N^{-1}+2\beta R$, this is (R.8.2a.1). Contracted Bianchi gives $\nabla^\mu R_{\mu\nu}=\tfrac12\nabla_\nu R$. The divergence of the first two terms is therefore $R_{\mu\nu}\nabla^\mu f'(R)$, while the scalar-derivative commutator gives the opposite divergence for the last term. Thus the geometric tensor is conserved, and the metric equation requires $\nabla^\mu T_{\mu\nu}=0$.

Taking the trace gives
$$
(G_N^{-1}+2\beta R)R
-2(G_N^{-1}R+\beta R^2)+6\beta\Box_gR=8\pi T.
$$
The quadratic terms cancel, yielding (R.8.2a.2) and $\mathcal A_gR=-8\pi G_NT$. The registered bijection gives (R.8.2a.3), and multiplication by the constant $G_N$ gives (R.8.2a.4). If $T\in\mathcal D\cap\mathcal E$ and $\Box_gT=0$, then $\mathcal A_gT=T$; uniqueness of the inverse yields $\mathcal A_g^{-1}T=T$. Without the inverse-domain certificate, the trace equation can admit additional scalar solutions $R_h$ of $\mathcal A_gR_h=0$ consistent with the chosen data. The response formula is a scalar trace relation for the specified metric and domains, not a solution of the coupled metric-matter equations by itself. ∎

<a id="theorem-r82b-local-nonlinear-retarded-response-of-the-original-quadratic-curvature-action"></a>**Theorem R.8.2b (Local Nonlinear Retarded Response of the Original Quadratic-Curvature Action).** Fix $G>0$ and $\beta>0$, retain the $R/G+\beta R^2$ action of Theorem R.8.2a, and use the complete autonomous scalar/relational-clock material source below. On an actual open fixed-support small constitutive-source ball, there is a common finite Cauchy window with Lorentzian metric, $F=1+2\beta GR>0$ and nondegenerate timelike material clock. The constrained coupled source/metric solution has an exact finite-amplitude causal retarded kernel, controlled source derivatives and remainders, covariance modulo initial-data-preserving diffeomorphisms and the explicit relational readout contacts. Its homogeneous restriction and quantitative fixed-unit weak-source limit are those of the same action/source, including the response-active massive scalaron. A positive-source member has a nonzero intrinsic curvature response.

The constants, source law, state/input data, graph domains, unit/clock conventions and all retained contacts are specified in parts A–C. This is the direct original effective-action branch with finite-energy constrained Cauchy input; no W18 derivation, microscopic finite quantum substrate, arbitrary-sign coupling, global stability or common Einstein-only quantum–gravity realization is inferred.

*Proof.*

**A.1. Fixed constants, fields, units and complete action**

Take signature $(-+++)$, physical units $c=\hbar=1$, and fixed constants

$$
\begin{aligned}
&G>0,\;\beta>0,\;\alpha=\beta G,\;\kappa=8\pi G,\;m>0,\;v>0,\;L>0,\;q>0, \\
&k=2\pi v/L,\;M_s^{2}=1/(6\alpha).
\end{aligned}
$$

$G$, $\beta$, $m$, the torus period $L$ and the physical time unit do not change in the weak-source limit. The spacetime is a short globally hyperbolic slab with Cauchy surface $T^3_L$. There is no spatial boundary. Metric variations used to derive the bulk equations are compactly supported in the slab. No assertion that fixing only the endpoint metric removes the higher-derivative boundary variation is needed.

The retained fields are the Jordan metric $g$, a real scalar $y$, one real clock $X^0$, and three circle-valued material coordinates $X^j$ with circumference $2\pi v$. Their target is $\mathbb{R}\times(S^1_v)^3$, with its flat positive target metric. Each spatial clock has winding one. The kinetic derivative of an angular field is the pullback of the circle's standard one-form; local lifts give identical action and equations.

A program $J$ is a fixed smooth scalar function on that target, periodic in the angular coordinates, vanishing on an open target-time neighborhood of the initial slice. It is a constitutive parameter, constant as a function of coordinate time, not an externally imposed spacetime force. The source chart below permits arbitrary small smooth $J$ with common target support. One-probe notation $J=\lambda f$ is a specialization, with $\lambda$ dimensionless.

For $0<\varepsilon\le 1$ define the actual action

$$
\begin{aligned}
S_{\varepsilon,J}[g,y,X] \\
&= (1/(2\kappa))\int\sqrt{-g}\,(R+\alpha R^{2}) \\
&\quad -\varepsilon^{2}\int\sqrt{-g}\,\bigl\{(1/2)g^{\mu\nu}[\partial_\mu y\,\partial_\nu y+\sum_A\partial_\mu X^{A}\partial_\nu X^{A}] \\
&\qquad +U_J(y,X)\bigr\}, \\
U_J &= (m^{2}/2)[y+J(X)/m^{2}]^{2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.1}
$$

Thus the gravitational part is exactly $(16\pi)^{-1}\int\sqrt{-g}\,(R/G+\beta R^2)$, the original Appendix R action. The parameter $\varepsilon$ weakens the entire matter/clock material source, including kinetic energy, interaction and contacts. It does not rescale time, $G$, $\beta$, the response observable or energy units. The physical $\varepsilon=1$ branch is included. The $\varepsilon=0$ equations used for limits are defined by first dividing the matter equations by $\varepsilon^{2}$; they are the smooth limiting equations, not an assertion that a zero matter action determines test-field dynamics.

Dimensions: $[G]=\text{mass}^{-2}$, $[\beta]=1$, $[\alpha]=\text{mass}^{-2}$, $[y]=[X]=[v]=\text{mass}$, $[q]=[k]=\text{mass}^2$, $[J]=\text{mass}^3$ and $[U]=\text{mass}^4$. The initial operational calibration $X^0/q$ has the dimension of proper time. This calibration remains fixed as $\varepsilon$ varies.

**A.2. Exhaustive variation and all source contacts**

Set $Y=(y,X^0,X^1,X^2,X^3)$. The full retained matter stress is

$$
\begin{aligned}
T_{\mu\nu} &= \varepsilon^{2}T^{*}_{\mu\nu}, \\
T^{*}_{\mu\nu} &= \partial_\mu y\,\partial_\nu y+\sum_A\partial_\mu X^{A}\partial_\nu X^{A} \\
&\quad -g_{\mu\nu}\bigl\{(1/2)[(\partial y)^{2}+\sum_A(\partial X^{A})^{2}]+U_J\bigr\}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.2}
$$

There is no additional prescribed T, gravitational force or uncounted clock stress. The scalar equations are

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Box_gy-m^{2}y-J(X) &= 0, \\
\Box_gX^{A}-[y+J(X)/m^{2}]\,J_A(X) &= 0,
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.3}
$$

where $J_{A}$ is the target derivative. Their exact Ward identity is

$$
\nabla^{\mu}T_{\mu\nu}=\varepsilon^{2}\bigl\{(\Box y-U_y)\partial_\nu y+\sum_A(\Box X^{A}-U_A)\partial_\nu X^{A}\bigr\}=0.
\tag{R.8.2b.A.4}
$$

The second equation in (R.8.2b.A.3) is the clock/material reaction to the apparent y-driving term. Holding X fixed would discard that reaction and destroy the source-exhaustive certificate. Here X is always varied and evolved.

For $J=\lambda f$, the potential and its important mixed/contact vertices are

$$
\begin{aligned}
U &= (1/2)m^{2}y^{2}+\lambda yf+\lambda^{2}f^{2}/(2m^{2}), \\
U_y &= m^{2}y+\lambda f,\qquad U_{yy}=m^{2}, \\
U_A &= \lambda yf_A+\lambda^{2}ff_A/m^{2},\qquad U_{yA}=\lambda f_A, \\
U_{AB} &= \lambda yf_{AB}+\lambda^{2}(f_Af_B+ff_{AB})/m^{2}, \\
U_\lambda &= yf+\lambda f^{2}/m^{2},\qquad U_{\lambda\lambda}=f^{2}/m^{2}, \\
U_{y\lambda} &= f,\qquad U_{A\lambda}=yf_A+2\lambda ff_A/m^{2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.5}
$$

The direct fixed-field stress contacts are $\partial_\lambda T_{\mu\nu}=-\varepsilon^2g_{\mu\nu}U_\lambda$ and $\partial_\lambda^2T_{\mu\nu}=-\varepsilon^2g_{\mu\nu}f^2/m^2$. Changes of metric, fields and relational readout add their ordinary derivatives; none is subtracted. For arbitrary program directions $f,h$,

$$
D_JU[f]=(y+J/m^{2})f(X),\quad D_J^{2}U[f,h]=f(X)h(X)/m^{2}.
\tag{R.8.2b.A.6}
$$

Equations (R.8.2b.A.1), (R.8.2b.A.2) and (R.8.2b.A.6) define every higher/mixed contact by differentiation of one fixed action. No truncation to the displayed vertices defines the nonlinear source.

For every future unit normal n and induced spatial metric h, the actual material energy density is

$$
\begin{aligned}
T(n,n) &= \varepsilon^{2}\bigl\{(1/2)[(ny)^{2}+|Dy|^{2}+\sum_A((nX^{A})^{2}+|DX^{A}|^{2})] \\
&\qquad +U_J\bigr\}\ge 0.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.7}
$$

This positivity concerns the full material inventory. It is not a claim of a positive unconstrained gravitational Hamiltonian on a compact universe. The local theorem below supplies a stable hyperbolic branch and a positive scalaron mass, rather than a global-in-time stability assertion.

**A.3. Exact metric equation and registered retarded trace domain**

Varying (R.8.2b.A.1) yields

$$
\begin{aligned}
&FR_{\mu\nu}-(1/2)(R+\alpha R^{2})g_{\mu\nu} \\
&\quad +(g_{\mu\nu}\Box-\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu)F=\kappa\varepsilon^{2}T^{*}_{\mu\nu}, \\
&F=1+2\alpha R.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.8}
$$

Its trace is exactly

$$
\begin{aligned}
(I-6\alpha\Box_g)R &= -\kappa\varepsilon^{2}T^{*}, \\
(\Box_g-M_s^{2})R &= \kappa M_s^{2}\varepsilon^{2}T^{*}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.9}
$$

For each solved globally hyperbolic metric in the chart, choose an integer $2\le r\le m_0-2$, where $m_0$ is the high Sobolev index in the local source-chart theorem of Part B below. Set $E^r_T=C_tH^r\cap C^1_tH^{r-1}$, $A_g=I-6\alpha\Box_g$, and register the zero-Cauchy-data graph domain

$$
\begin{aligned}
E_g &= L^{1}([0,T];H^{r-1}(T^{3})), \\
D_g &= \{u\in E^{r}_T: u|_\Sigma=0,\; n\,u|_\Sigma=0,\; A_gu\in E_g\}.
\end{aligned}
$$

Give $D_{g}$ the graph norm $\lVert u\rVert_{E^r_T}+\lVert A_gu\rVert_{E_g}$. The normally hyperbolic massive scalar Cauchy energy estimate makes $A_g:D_g\to E_g$ bijective with bounded inverse. In particular this specifies both domain and range, including the spatial zero mode and every admissible time-dependent forcing. Its inverse is the zero-data retarded inverse, not an arbitrary frequency prescription. The constructed initial data have $R=nR=0$, so

$$
R=-\kappa\varepsilon^{2}(I-6\alpha\Box_g)^{-1}_{\mathrm{ret}}T^{*}.
\tag{R.8.2b.A.10}
$$

This is precisely the original R.8.2a branch with the complete retained stress. There are no undetermined homogeneous scalar response modes. Equation (R.8.2b.A.10) alone is not offered as the coupled solution theorem; that theorem is given in part B.

**A.4. Exact Einstein-frame equivalence and positivity**

On $F>0$ introduce

$$
\varphi=\sqrt{3/(2\kappa)}\log F,\quad b=\sqrt{2\kappa/3},\quad F=e^{b\varphi},\quad g_E=Fg.
$$

The auxiliary action $(2\kappa)^{-1}\int\sqrt{-g}\,[FR-(F-1)^2/(4\alpha)]$ is exactly equivalent to (R.8.2b.A.1)'s gravitational action: its $F$ equation is $R=(F-1)/(2\alpha)$, since $\alpha>0$. After conformal transformation and the stated boundary convention, the full action becomes

$$
\begin{aligned}
S_E &= \int\sqrt{-g_E}\,\bigl\{R_E/(2\kappa)-(1/2)(\partial\varphi)^{2}-V(\varphi) \\
&\qquad -(\varepsilon^{2}/2)F^{-1}\sum_I(\partial Y^{I})^{2}-\varepsilon^{2}F^{-2}U_J(Y)\bigr\}, \\
V(\varphi) &= (1-e^{-b\varphi})^{2}/(8\kappa\alpha).
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.11}
$$

All scalar kinetic coefficients are positive for $\varepsilon>0$ and $F>0$. Moreover $V\ge 0$ and $V''(0)=M_s^2>0$. This is an exact field redefinition of the original $R+\alpha R^2$ theory, retaining its scalaron. It adds no independent gravitational source and cannot be read as an Einstein-only branch.

Let $A_E=\sum_Ig_E^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu Y^I\partial_\nu Y^I$. The normalized equations are

$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{Ric}_{E,\mu\nu} &= \kappa\bigl\{\partial_\mu\varphi\,\partial_\nu\varphi+\varepsilon^{2}F^{-1}\sum_I\partial_\mu Y^{I}\partial_\nu Y^{I} \\
&\qquad +g_{E,\mu\nu}[V+\varepsilon^{2}F^{-2}U_J]\bigr\}, \\
\Box_E\varphi-V'(\varphi) &= (b\varepsilon^{2}/2)T^{*}_E, \\
T^{*}_E &= -F^{-1}A_E-4F^{-2}U_J, \\
\Box_EY^{I}-b\,\partial\varphi\cdot\partial Y^{I}-F^{-1}U_I &= 0.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.12}
$$

Dividing the matter equations by $\varepsilon^{2}$ is legitimate for every positive $\varepsilon$ and exposes a regular hyperbolic limit. Unweighted Sobolev energies for these normalized equations control the limit; the vanishing physical material energy is not used as a uniform analytic norm.

**A.5. Explicit nonempty constraint data, independent of program**

At the initial torus choose Einstein-frame data

$$
\begin{aligned}
&h_{ij}=a_0^{2}\delta_{ij},\quad K_{ij}=-H_\varepsilon h_{ij}, \\
&\varphi=n\varphi=y=ny=0, \\
&X^{0}=0,\quad nX^{0}=q,\quad X^{j}=kx^{j}\bmod 2\pi v,\quad nX^{j}=0, \\
&H_\varepsilon=\varepsilon\sqrt{\kappa(q^{2}+3k^{2}/a_0^{2})/6}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.A.13}
$$

Programs vanish in a neighborhood of $X^0=0$, including all target derivatives. Hence $U_{J}$ and all program-dependent source vertices vanish on $\Sigma$ for every program, and exactly the same initial data work for the entire source chart.

The momentum density is zero. The Einstein Hamiltonian constraint is

$$
\begin{aligned}
R(h)+(\operatorname{tr}K)^{2}-|K|^{2} &= 6H_\varepsilon^{2} \\
&= 2\kappa\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{*}_0,\quad \rho^{*}_0=(q^{2}+3k^{2}/a_0^{2})/2.
\end{aligned}
$$

The momentum constraint also vanishes. Thus (R.8.2b.A.13) is an explicit, nonempty constraint-satisfying family, not an assumed solution of the constraint equations. Since $F=1$ and $nF=0$, the Jordan and Einstein initial $h,K$ coincide, and $R=nR=0$. The evolution fixes the higher Jordan metric derivatives consistently through the scalar equation.

For Einstein harmonic coordinates, initially choose lapse 1, shift 0, $g_{E,00}=-1$, $g_{E,0i}=0$, $\partial_tg_{E,ij}=2H_\varepsilon h_{ij}$, $\partial_tg_{E,00}=-6H_\varepsilon$ and $\partial_tg_{E,0i}=0$. These give $\Gamma_E^\mu=0$ initially. The constraints imply its vanishing normal derivative. No independent incompatible gauge data are appended.

The clock differential initially has determinant $qk^3\ne 0$ and timelike $X^0$ gradient. On a sufficiently short common solution window those properties persist. Spatial windings cannot disappear under that small smooth evolution, giving a relational readout chart. This chart is a physical supplier/readout field, not a substitute for the hyperbolic evolution gauge.

**A.6. Source-exhaustion and scope ledger**

The declared effective-action system contains exactly: the original metric plus its scalaron; y; four physical clock/material fields; their kinetic energies; the full positive square $U_{J}$; all metric/program/field contacts; and the specified constrained initial data. Clock recoil/work is carried by (R.8.2b.A.3)-(R.8.2b.A.4). Space is periodic, so there is no omitted wall or boundary work. J and the constants are fixed material laws, not time-dependent external services. No prescribed nonconserved stress, silent vacuum subtraction, external pump holding X fixed, or omitted $\lambda^{2}$ energy is used.

Preparing the initial data is not claimed as a microscopic finite quantum protocol. At the original effective-action scope, (R.8.2b.A.13) is the explicit retained finite-energy Cauchy input. A separate substrate, fabrication, or cross-owner horizon/quantum certificate would remain a different claim. In particular the scalaron is response-active at fixed $\beta>0$ and mass $M_{s}$; this branch is not imported into K15's Einstein-only exclusion gate.

Primary mathematical references: Fourès-Bruhat's quasilinear hyperbolic Cauchy construction (Fourès-Bruhat 1953); Salgado's scalar-tensor wave-gauge and constraint analysis (Salgado 2005, Appendix A), whose cited vacuum result is not silently used as a theorem for arbitrary matter; Bär et al. (2007, Chapter 3) for normally hyperbolic retarded Cauchy inverses. The particular matter system and its estimates are established explicitly in Part B of Theorem R.8.2b.

**B.1. Source and solution domains**

Fix an integer $s\ge 8$, set $m_0=s+16$ and $N=s+24$. (The symbol $m_0$ is a regularity index, distinct from the matter mass $m$.) Source functions $J$ belong to the Banach space $C^N_K$ of target-smooth functions supported in a fixed compact subset $K$ of $\mathbb{R}\times(S^1_v)^3$, vanishing in a fixed open neighborhood of $X^0=0$. Norms use the fixed target radii and physical scales from the source contract. Choose a ball

$$
B_\delta=\{J\in C^{N}_K:\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}<\delta\}.
\tag{R.8.2b.B.1}
$$

The ball is open in this common-support source space and includes arbitrary spatially inhomogeneous programs; it is not a single selected $\lambda$ probe. Smooth functions in it supply distribution-kernel tests. For finite-regularity programs the conclusions hold at the Sobolev orders below.

Let $E^r_T=C([0,T];H^r(T^3))\cap C^1([0,T];H^{r-1}(T^3))$, with the sum of the two supremum norms. Angular fields are represented by periodic perturbations of their fixed affine winding lifts. Put $U=(g_E,\phi,Y)$, and solve in Einstein harmonic coordinates with the initial data (R.8.2b.A.13) and the specified harmonic lapse/shift derivatives of part A. The Jordan metric is always $g=\exp(-b\phi)g_E$.

**Local source-chart theorem.** There are $\delta>0$, $T>0$ and finite constants $C_{j}$, uniform for $0\le\varepsilon\le 1$ and $J\in B_\delta$, such that:

1. The normalized equations (R.8.2b.A.12) have a unique solution $U_{\varepsilon,J}\in E^{m_0}_T$, uniformly bounded there. For $\varepsilon>0$ it solves the full Euler–Lagrange equations and constraints of the declared original action. At $\varepsilon=0$ it solves the regular limiting equations.
2. $g$ and $g_{E}$ are Lorentzian, $F$ stays in a fixed compact positive interval, and $X$ is a local diffeomorphism with timelike $dX^0$ throughout this slab. The initial winding gives an actual relational chart after the short-time restriction.
3. The source/solution map is six times differentiable into successively lower energy spaces. In particular, for $j\le 6$,

   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   \lVert D_J^j U_{\varepsilon,J}[f_1,\dots,f_j]\rVert_{E^{m_0-j}_T} \\
   &\le C_j \prod_l \lVert f_l\rVert_{C^N}.
   \end{aligned}
   \tag{R.8.2b.B.2}
   $$

   Mixed derivatives with respect to $\varepsilon$ and $J$ satisfy the same estimate with one Sobolev order reserved per total derivative. No differentiability assertion in the top energy norm is made.
4. Every source variation has zero Cauchy data and is obtained from the exact retarded inverse of the linearized coupled system. The first/second kernels, the exact finite-amplitude path kernel, and quantitative Taylor remainders are given below. They are covariant modulo diffeomorphisms fixing the Cauchy data; scalar relational readouts are genuinely covariant.
5. All gravitational source derivatives carry the actual $\varepsilon^{2}$ factor. On the same chart, with $G_{\mathrm{var}}=(g_E,\phi)$ or equivalently $(g,\phi)$,

   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   \lVert D_J^j \mathrm{Gvar}_{\varepsilon,J}[f_1,\dots,f_j]\rVert_{E^{m_0-j-1}_T} \\
   &\le C_j \varepsilon^2 \prod_l \lVert f_l\rVert_{C^N},\quad 1\le j\le 6.
   \end{aligned}
   \tag{R.8.2b.B.3}
   $$

   In particular the nonzero-amplitude gravitational response is controlled without dividing physical energy units or changing the clock.

**B.2. Hyperbolic construction and a quantitative common time**

In harmonic coordinates the first equation (R.8.2b.A.12) has principal part $-(1/2)g_E^{ab}\partial_a\partial_bg_{E,\mu\nu}$. Multiplying it by $-2$, all components of $U$ have the common principal operator $g_E^{ab}\partial_a\partial_b$. The remaining terms are smooth functions of $U$, its first derivatives, $J(X)$ and $J_A(X)$. No second derivative of a matter field enters the metric right side. The scalar equations were divided by $\varepsilon^{2}$ before forming this system, so these coefficients and their required derivatives are bounded uniformly down to $\varepsilon=0$.

Choose a compact coefficient chart with: lapse and spatial-metric eigenvalues bounded away from zero; bounded inverse metric; $|\phi|\le\Phi$ with $\exp(-b\Phi)\le F\le\exp(b\Phi)$; bounded fields and first derivatives; $dX^0$ timelike with a strict margin; and $\det(DX)$ separated from zero. The initial values lie strictly inside this chart. The derivative bounds on $J$ in (R.8.2b.B.1), together with the explicit smooth functions $F,V,U$, give a finite coefficient bound $M_{N}$ on this chart.

For a linear wave system with these coefficients and lower-order terms, commuting spatial derivatives through the equation, multiplying by the normal time derivative, and integrating on the torus gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
E_r(w;t)&\le C_E \exp(C_r t) \\
&\quad \{E_r(w;0)+\int_0^t \lVert P_U w(\tau)\rVert_{H^{r-1}}\,d\tau\}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.B.4}
$$

Here $E_{r}$ is the usual positive wave energy equivalent to $\lVert w\rVert_{H^r}+\lVert\partial_tw\rVert_{H^{r-1}}$; one may use the unit normal and induced positive spatial metric to handle shift terms. The constants depend on the stated chart bounds and the corresponding $H^{m_0}$ bound for $U$. Commutator terms are controlled by the product inequality

$$
\lVert ab\rVert_{H^{r}}\le C_r(\lVert a\rVert_{L^\infty}\lVert b\rVert_{H^{r}}+\lVert a\rVert_{H^{r}}\lVert b\rVert_{L^\infty})
$$

and Sobolev embedding in three dimensions. For the linearized system, the only apparently high-order coefficient is $w_g\partial^2U$; for $r\le m_0-1$, $\partial^2U\in H^{m_0-2}$ supplies precisely the $H^{r-1}$ product bound required by (R.8.2b.B.4).

For completeness, fix an energy radius $R$ larger than twice the common initial $H^{m_0}\times H^{m_0-1}$ norm, after subtracting the affine winding lifts. Let $d_{\mathrm{ch}}$ be a positive lower bound on the initial distance from the boundary of the coefficient/clock chart. On the radius-$R$ ball let $C_{\mathrm{en}}$ bound the commuted nonlinear energy inequality, $C_{\mathrm{lip}}$ the one-lower-order difference estimate, and $C_{S}$ the Sobolev embedding constant. Solving the normalized wave system for its second time derivative, using the uniform nonzero $g_{E}^{00}$, gives

$$
\lVert\partial_t^{2}U\rVert_{H^{m_0-2}}\le C_{\mathrm{acc}}(\mathrm{chart},R,\delta),
$$

where $C_{\mathrm{acc}}$ is a finite expression in the coefficient maxima and $R$ (it can contain $R^2$ and is not replaced by $R$). The same bound applies to the iterates. These constants are obtained from finite maxima of the coefficient derivatives bounded by $M_{N}$ and the displayed product estimate. The construction works with any positive $T$ satisfying

$$
\begin{aligned}
T\le\min\{&1,\,d_{\mathrm{ch}}/[4C_S(R+C_{\mathrm{acc}})], \\
&1/[4C_{\mathrm{en}}(1+R)],\,1/(4C_{\mathrm{lip}})\}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.B.5}
$$

Increasing $R$ once if necessary absorbs the fixed initial coefficients. Thus $T$ is bounded below by a specified function of finite source/data/chart bounds, not by an unknown lifespan of the desired solution. One can additionally impose $T<\pi/(2M_s)$ for the later nonzero homogeneous witness.

Picard iteration freezes the principal coefficient at the preceding iterate and uses the preceding lower-order terms. Inequality (R.8.2b.B.4) preserves the radius-$R$ ball by (R.8.2b.B.5). For successive differences, the only additional principal term is $(g_i^{ab}-g_{i-1}^{ab})\partial_a\partial_bU_i$, controlled in $H^{m_0-2}$; hence the iteration contracts in $E^{m_0-1}$ when $C_{\mathrm{lip}}T\le 1/4$. Its strong lower-order limit, uniform high-order bound and the energy estimate give a solution in $E^{m_0}$. Smooth approximation gives strong high-order continuity, and the same difference estimate proves uniqueness. Differentiating the equation recovers the stated temporal regularity. This is the standard quasilinear-wave construction, with its hypotheses checked here for the actual scalar/clock source rather than for unspecified conserved matter.

For the full first-jet chart, spatial first derivatives obey $\lVert U(t)-U(0)\rVert_{C^1_x}\le C_STR$, while $\lVert\partial_tU(t)-\partial_tU(0)\rVert_{C^0}\le C_ST\,C_{\mathrm{acc}}$ follows by integrating the acceleration bound. Together they preserve the clock margins, including timelike $dX^0$ and $\det(DX)$, by (R.8.2b.B.5). Their purpose is readout and relational source localization, not analytic coercivity. The uniform PDE norm is unweighted: physical matter energy proportional to $\varepsilon^{2}$ would not control these norms as $\varepsilon\to 0$.

There is no circular choice of an inactive program. First obtain (R.8.2b.B.5) for a source-norm ball whose bound does not depend on the support location. Then choose $K$ in a target-time interval reached before $T$, for example inside $qT/8<X^0<qT/4$ after reducing the clock chart so $\partial_tX^0\ge q/2$. Choose any nonzero smooth bump $f$ there and restrict $|\lambda|<\delta/\lVert f\rVert_{C^N}$ for $J=\lambda f$. This guarantees a genuinely active program on the common window while retaining zero source data near $\Sigma$.

**B.3. Constraint propagation and geometric uniqueness**

The reduced Einstein equations differ from the full equations by symmetrized derivatives of $C^\mu=\Gamma_E^\mu$. The complete scalar equations imply the exact Einstein-frame stress conservation identity. Taking the divergence of the reduced metric equations therefore gives a homogeneous linear wave equation for $C$,

$$
\Box_EC_\mu+\mathrm{Ric}_{E,\mu\nu}C^{\nu}=0
\tag{R.8.2b.B.6}
$$

(up to the equivalent lower-order form associated with the chosen reduced convention). Initial $C=0$ follows from the explicit gauge data. The Hamiltonian and momentum constraints imply its vanishing initial normal derivative. Uniqueness in (R.8.2b.B.4) gives $C=0$. Thus the solution is an actual constrained solution of the full coupled equations, and the exact invertible $F>0$ transformation gives the original Jordan equations.

A change of spacetime coordinates fixing the Cauchy data yields the same geometric development. The source function $J$ is a scalar law on the material target and $J(X)$ transforms as a spacetime scalar, so this argument applies to the entire source family. No preferred external spacetime source is installed by the gauge choice.

**B.4. Retarded inverse and differentiated equations**

Write the normalized reduced equations as $E(U,J;\varepsilon)=0$ and set

$$
P_{\varepsilon,J}=D_UE(U_{\varepsilon,J},J;\varepsilon).
$$

Its principal symbol is $g_E^{ab}\xi_a\xi_b$ times the identity on the finite vector of metric/scalar variables. Lower-order mixing includes all metric, scalaron, matter and clock vertices. Hence it is a normally hyperbolic vector-bundle operator on the solved slab. Equation (R.8.2b.B.4), with zero initial data, defines its unique retarded Cauchy inverse $G_{\varepsilon,J}^{\mathrm{ret}}$. Its distribution kernel has causal support. This conclusion also follows from the normally hyperbolic Cauchy theory in Bär–Ginoux–Pfäffle, Chapter 3; no inverse is asserted on an unspecified domain.

For source directions f,h the exact first and second variations are

$$
u_f=-G^{\mathrm{ret}}E_J[f],
\tag{R.8.2b.B.7}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
u_{fh} &= -G^{\mathrm{ret}}\bigl\{E_{UU}[u_f,u_h]+E_{UJ}[u_f,h] \\
&\qquad +E_{UJ}[u_h,f]+E_{JJ}[f,h]\bigr\}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.B.8}
$$

Every derivative on the right is evaluated at the actual background $(U_{\varepsilon,J},J)$. In particular $E_{JJ}$ contains the square-completion contact from part A (R.8.2b.A.6), and $E_{UJ}$ contains the clock reaction and source-evaluation derivatives. Equations (R.8.2b.B.7)-(R.8.2b.B.8) are not formulas with the physical source held fixed while only $y$ is varied.

The source support in (R.8.2b.B.7) is contained in $X_J^{-1}(\operatorname{supp}f)$. A target derivative of $f$ has the same support. Finite propagation gives $\operatorname{supp}u_f\subset J_g^+(X_J^{-1}\operatorname{supp}f)$. In (R.8.2b.B.8) every source term is causally downstream of both input supports, so its kernel vanishes unless both input events can influence the output. The Jordan and Einstein cones agree because their metrics are positively conformal. These support statements concern variations about the actual finite background, not merely flat Fourier analyticity.

**B.5. Derivative estimates, actual differentiability and finite remainders**

For a $j$-th source derivative, keep the term $P_Uu_{1\dots j}$ on the left. Every remaining term is a finite product of lower variations, background coefficients and derivatives of the input programs. The worst spatial factor is $\partial^2u_I$ with $|I|\le j-1$. The inductive bound $u_I\in E^{m_0-|I|}$ places this factor in $H^{m_0-j-1}$, exactly the forcing space for an output in $E^{m_0-j}$. Since $m_0-j-1\ge s+9>3/2$ for $j\le 6$, all remaining products satisfy the above Sobolev estimates. Equation (R.8.2b.B.4) and induction prove (R.8.2b.B.2).

These are derivatives of the solution map, not merely formal candidate variations. For the first derivative, subtract the exact equations at $J+hf$ and $J$, divide by $h$, subtract (R.8.2b.B.7), and apply (R.8.2b.B.4) to the residual; Taylor's formula for the smooth coefficient maps and the one-order-lower difference bound make that residual tend to zero. Repeating this argument at each order, reserving one spatial derivative each time, proves existence and continuity through order six. The $C^N$ choice covers all source compositions and their required target derivatives. The identical argument applies to $\varepsilon$ and mixed derivatives; its equations have smooth polynomial $\varepsilon$-dependence and smooth initial $H_\varepsilon=\varepsilon H_*$ dependence. If desired, extend $\varepsilon$ to a small signed interval to apply ordinary two-sided parameter differentiation; the same constrained data use the opposite expansion sign and all coefficient bounds remain valid.

The block structure proves (R.8.2b.B.3). Write variations as gravitational $A_{j}$ and material $B_{j}$. Every term feeding material variations into a gravitational equation carries $\varepsilon^{2}$; no inverse $\varepsilon$ occurs in the normalized matter equations. The retarded estimates have the form

$$
\begin{aligned}
A_j(t) &\le C\int_0^tA_j+C\varepsilon^{2}\{B_j(t)+\text{known lower products}\}, \\
B_j(t) &\le C\{\prod\lVert f_l\rVert+\int_0^tA_j+\text{known lower products}\}.
\end{aligned}
$$

At $j=1$ the data vanish and the lower products are bounded. Gronwall (or the same short-time contraction) yields $A_1\le C\varepsilon^2\lVert f\rVert$ and $B_1\le C\lVert f\rVert$. Induction preserves the $\varepsilon^{2}$ factor at every $j\le 6$: a purely gravitational lower product already contains it, and every new matter forcing of gravity has it explicitly. This establishes the stronger source-controlled estimate without treating $\varepsilon^{2}$ physical energy as a Sobolev norm.

Banach-space Taylor integration now gives, for $\lVert J\rVert_{C^N}<\delta$,

$$
\lVert U_J-U_0-u_J-(1/2)u_{JJ}\rVert_{E^{s}}\le C\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}^{3}.
\tag{R.8.2b.B.9}
$$

The exact symmetry $(J,y)\to(-J,-y)$, with all other fields unchanged, and identical initial data implies $y$ is odd while $(g_E,\phi,X)$ and $g$ are even. Therefore for the gravitational and clock outputs their first and third derivatives at $J=0$ vanish. In particular

$$
\begin{aligned}
g_{\varepsilon,J}-g_{\varepsilon,0} &= (1/2)D_J^{2}g_{\varepsilon,0}[J,J]+R_{\varepsilon,J}, \\
\lVert R_{\varepsilon,J}\rVert_{E^{s}} &\le C\varepsilon^{2}\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}^{4}, \\
\lVert g_{\varepsilon,J}-g_{\varepsilon,0}\rVert_{E^{s}} &\le C\varepsilon^{2}\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}^{2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.B.10}
$$

These are fixed-positive-radius, nonzero-amplitude estimates. No regulator-dependent shrinking source disk is used.

**B.6. Exact nonlinear kernel, covariance and readout contacts**

The full map is not claimed to equal an unproved convergent Volterra series. There is instead an exact finite-amplitude response identity on the star-shaped source ball:

$$
\begin{aligned}
U_J-U_0 &= -\int_0^1G_{\varepsilon,\theta J}^{\mathrm{ret}} \\
&\qquad E_J(U_{\varepsilon,\theta J},\theta J;\varepsilon)[J]\,d\theta.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.B.11}
$$

This follows from the proved differentiability and the fundamental theorem of calculus. The distributional integral on the right is an exact nonlinear, background-dependent retarded kernel. It includes all orders through the actual intermediate solutions. Equations (R.8.2b.B.7)-(R.8.2b.B.10) give its first nonlinear coefficients and controlled finite remainders. Each constituent retarded inverse has the physical cone of its own solved background; domain of dependence and geometric uniqueness give the intrinsic nonlinear causality statement.

For a smooth observable $O(U,J)$, its exact response is

$$
O_J-O_0=\int_0^1\{O_U\,D_JS[\theta J]J+O_J[J]\}\,d\theta.
\tag{R.8.2b.B.12}
$$

The second term retains any direct observable/source contact. Its second derivative likewise contains $O_Uu_{fh}$, $O_{UU}[u_f,u_h]$, the two $O_{UJ}$ terms and $O_{JJ}$. Thus source and readout contacts are specified by one exact map rather than discarded by notation.

The dynamical clocks give a coordinate-independent scalar readout

$$
R_{\mathrm{rel}}[J](Y)=R[g_J](X_J^{-1}(Y)).
\tag{R.8.2b.B.13}
$$

Where the clock inverse is defined, its first variation is the pullback of $\delta R-\xi^\mu\partial_\mu R$, $\xi^\mu=((DX)^{-1})^\mu{}_A\,\delta X^A$. Higher derivatives are those of this exact composition, controlled after the available additional Sobolev losses. For a metric readout, replace the scalar composition by the tensor pullback; its first variation is $\delta g-L_\xi g$. These formulas explicitly retain clock-motion effects.

A weak-source metric comparison is made in the common hyperbolic gauge, or modulo diffeomorphism, not by dividing raw source-dependent clock-coordinate metric differences by $\varepsilon^{2}$. Clocks react even in flat test matter; that coordinate effect is not gravity. The nonzero curvature witness and the correctly transported relational statement are treated in part C.

**B.7. Scope of the constructed map**

This is a nonlinear coupled metric–scalaron–matter–clock solution map on an actual open source ball, with complete action contacts, compatible constraints, retarded kernels, physical characteristic cones and nonzero-amplitude remainders. It is local in time on the displayed regular branch. It does not assert global evolution through singularities, arbitrary $\beta\le 0$, loss of the $F>0$ chart, a quantized microscopic substrate, or an Einstein-only/K15 branch. Those claims are not used to obtain the original effective-action kernel here.

**C.1. Exact homogeneous restriction of the same nonlinear map**

Restrict the source law to $J(X)=\lambda f(X^0)$, with a smooth target-time bump satisfying the source-chart conditions. The three spatial clocks remain $X^j=kx^j$: their equations have $U_{j}=0$ and the affine winding lifts solve their wave equations. Combined spatial translations and target-circle shifts, together with simultaneous spatial/target permutations and sign changes, preserve the winding data and the homogeneous source law. These symmetries and uniqueness of the hyperbolic development preserve the spatially flat homogeneous isotropic ansatz. Equivalently, the displayed homogeneous ansatz solves every component of the full PDE and uniqueness identifies it with the constructed solution. Its cosmic-time form in the Jordan frame is

$$
\begin{aligned}
g &= -dt^{2}+a(t)^{2}\delta_{ij}\,dx^{i}dx^{j}, \\
y &= y(t),\quad X^{0}=X(t),\quad X^{j}=kx^{j}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.1}
$$

This is the same solution written in a different time coordinate. If $\tau$ denotes the harmonic time used for the PDE construction, symmetry sets its shift to zero and its Jordan lapse $N_J(\tau)>0$. Define $t(\tau)=\int_0^\tau N_J(u)\,du$. This strictly increasing $C^1$ change, with the higher regularity supplied by the solution, proves the conversion to (R.8.2b.C.1); it does not change initial normal derivatives or impose an additional source equation. Over a shorter common interval, $t$ and $\tau$ have uniformly equivalent lengths.

With $H=\dot{a}/a$, the retained material energy and pressure before the common $\varepsilon^{2}$ factor are exactly

$$
\begin{aligned}
\rho^{*} &= \frac{1}{2}\dot{y}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\dot{X}^{2}+3k^{2}/(2a^{2})+U_\lambda(y,X), \\
p^{*} &= \frac{1}{2}\dot{y}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\dot{X}^{2}-k^{2}/(2a^{2})-U_\lambda(y,X), \\
U_\lambda &= \frac{1}{2}m^{2}(y+\lambda f(X)/m^{2})^{2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.2}
$$

The equations obtained by restricting the full material Euler–Lagrange equations are

$$
\begin{aligned}
\ddot{y}+3H\dot{y}+m^{2}y+\lambda f(X) &= 0, \\
\ddot{X}+3H\dot{X}+\lambda(y+\lambda f(X)/m^{2})f'(X) &= 0.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.3}
$$

Differentiating (R.8.2b.C.2) and using both equations (R.8.2b.C.3), including the clock reaction, gives the exact balance

$$
\dot{\rho}^{*}+3H(\rho^{*}+p^{*})=0.
\tag{R.8.2b.C.4}
$$

There is no uncounted work reservoir in (R.8.2b.C.4).

The 00, spatial and trace equations of the original $R+\alpha R^2$ metric equation reduce to

$$
\begin{aligned}
3FH^{2} &= \kappa\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{*}+\alpha R^{2}/2-3H\dot{F}, \\
-2F\dot{H} &= \kappa\varepsilon^{2}(\rho^{*}+p^{*})+\ddot{F}-H\dot{F}, \\
\ddot{R}+3H\dot{R}+M_s^{2}R &= \kappa\varepsilon^{2}M_s^{2}(\rho^{*}-3p^{*}), \\
F &= 1+2\alpha R,\qquad R=6(\dot{H}+2H^{2}).
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.5}
$$

The initial data are $a(0)=a_0$, $H(0)=H_\varepsilon$, $R(0)=\dot{R}(0)=0$, $y(0)=\dot{y}(0)=X(0)=0$ and $\dot{X}(0)=q$. They satisfy the first equation by the explicitly solved Hamiltonian constraint. A nonzero initial matter trace is compatible with $R(0)=\dot{R}(0)=0$: it determines $\ddot{R}(0)$, rather than invalidating the retarded scalar data. Equation (R.8.2b.C.4) and the trace equation propagate the first equation in (R.8.2b.C.5), as also follows from the full constraint propagation theorem.

For an independent sign check, in Einstein cosmic time the same restriction obeys

$$
\begin{aligned}
3H_E^{2} &= \kappa\bigl\{\frac{1}{2}\dot{\varphi}^{2}+V+\varepsilon^{2}[\frac{1}{2}F^{-1}(\dot{y}^{2}+\dot{X}^{2}+3k^{2}/a_E^{2})+F^{-2}U]\bigr\}, \\
\dot{H}_E &= -(\kappa/2)\bigl\{\dot{\varphi}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}F^{-1}(\dot{y}^{2}+\dot{X}^{2}+k^{2}/a_E^{2})\bigr\}, \\
\ddot{\varphi}+3H_E\dot{\varphi}+V' &= -(b\varepsilon^{2}/2)T^{*}_E, \\
\ddot{y}+(3H_E-b\dot{\varphi})\dot{y}+F^{-1}U_y &= 0, \\
\ddot{X}+(3H_E-b\dot{\varphi})\dot{X}+F^{-1}U_X &= 0,
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.6}
$$

where $b=\sqrt{2\kappa/3}$, $a_E=\sqrt{F}\,a$, $dt_E=\sqrt{F}\,dt$, and dots in (R.8.2b.C.6) refer only to $t_{E}$. These formulas follow by direct substitution into the full Einstein-frame equations; the scalar force sign is fixed by signature $-+++$.

Equations (R.8.2b.C.2)–(R.8.2b.C.6) prove homogeneous matching by exact restriction and uniqueness, rather than by fitting a separate homogeneous model. Their source inventory is identical to that of the inhomogeneous theorem.

**C.2. The regular weak-source family**

Set $a_0=1$ for this section. The physical family is the declared action with the entire material/clock sector multiplied by $\varepsilon^2$, $0<\varepsilon\le 1$. Its initial expansion is $H_\varepsilon=\varepsilon H_*$ with fixed $H_*=\sqrt{\kappa(q^2+3k^2)/6}$. No gravitational coupling, clock normalization, target radius, mass or physical unit is retuned.

At $\varepsilon=0$ use the normalized limiting field equations from the local theorem. Their metric is exactly $\eta$, their scalaron is zero, and their material fields $Y_{J}$ solve

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Box_\eta y-m^{2}y-J(X) &= 0, \\
\Box_\eta X^{A}-(y+J(X)/m^{2})J_A(X) &= 0
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.7}
$$

with the original fixed material initial data. These are genuinely nonlinear flat equations, including clock recoil and every $J^2$ contact. Let $T^{\mathrm{flat}}_J$ be their exact stress computed with $\eta$ and $U_{J}$, and put

$$
\tau_J=T^{\mathrm{flat}}_J-T^{\mathrm{flat}}_0.
$$

Both flat solutions satisfy their equations, so $\partial^\mu\tau_{J,\mu\nu}=0$ exactly. The source is not an arbitrarily prescribed nonconserved tensor. Its initial jets vanish on the common inactive-program neighborhood. In particular $\tau_{J}$ has zero initial energy and momentum difference.

All comparisons below first use the common Einstein harmonic gauge with the fixed gauge initial data. Define $\Delta g_{\varepsilon,J}=g_{\varepsilon,J}-g_{\varepsilon,0}$, and similarly for $g_E$, $\phi$ and $R$. There are fields $k_J,h_J,R_J^{(1)}$, with zero relative Cauchy data, and a constant $C$ uniform on a smaller fixed source ball, such that

$$
\begin{aligned}
\lVert\varepsilon^{-2}\Delta g_{E,\varepsilon,J}-k_J\rVert_{E^{s+2}} &\le C\varepsilon\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}^{2}, \\
\lVert\varepsilon^{-2}\Delta g_{\varepsilon,J}-h_J\rVert_{E^{s+2}} &\le C\varepsilon\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}^{2}, \\
\lVert\varepsilon^{-2}\Delta R_{\varepsilon,J}-R_J^{(1)}\rVert_{E^{s}} &\le C\varepsilon\lVert J\rVert_{C^{N}}^{2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.8}
$$

Also $\lVert g_{\varepsilon,0}-\eta\rVert_{E^{s+2}}\le C\varepsilon$ and $\lVert\phi_{\varepsilon,0}\rVert_{E^{s+2}}\le C\varepsilon^2$. Thus the reference spacetime approaches flat space but is not falsely called flat at positive $\varepsilon$. The clock stress and the common expanding initial data are fully retained.

Here is the parameter proof of (R.8.2b.C.8). At $\varepsilon=0$ the gravitational solution is independent of $J$. Its first $\varepsilon$ derivative is the vacuum linearized metric solution with the common initial expansion and gauge derivatives proportional to $H_*$; the first scalaron derivative is zero. It too is independent of $J$, since matter enters the gravitational equations only as $\varepsilon^{2}$. Therefore the relative gravitational map $D(\varepsilon,J)$ has $D(0,J)=\partial_\varepsilon D(0,J)=0$. Taylor's formula gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
D(\varepsilon,J) &= \frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_\varepsilon^{2}D(0,J) \\
&\quad +(\varepsilon^{3}/2)\int_0^1(1-\theta)^{2}\partial_\varepsilon^{3}D(\theta\varepsilon,J)\,d\theta.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.9}
$$

The exact evenness in $J$ and the mixed $D_\varepsilon^3D_J^2$ estimates of the local theorem imply $\lVert\partial_\varepsilon^3D(\varepsilon,J)\rVert_{E^{s+2}}\le C\lVert J\rVert_{C^N}^2$. These are derivatives of total order five, within the six-order theorem and its ample Sobolev reserve. The same estimate for $R$ uses the exact solved identity $R=(\exp(b\phi)-1)/(2\alpha)$, not an unproved interchange of second spacetime derivatives. This proves (R.8.2b.C.8) with its quantitative remainder. The first $\varepsilon$ metric term common to both backgrounds and all its quadratic gravitational contributions cancel in the subtraction.

The $\varepsilon=0$ matter dynamics used in (R.8.2b.C.7) is a smooth limit of normalized equations for $\varepsilon>0$. It is not asserted to be determined by varying the identically zero matter part of an $\varepsilon=0$ action.

**C.3. Exact weak-field operator and its retarded domain**

Taking the second $\varepsilon$ coefficient of the Einstein equations and subtracting $J=0$ gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
G^{L}_\eta(k_J) &= \kappa\tau_J, \\
\partial^{\mu}\bar{k}_{J,\mu\nu} &= 0, \\
\Box_\eta\bar{k}_{J,\mu\nu} &= -2\kappa\tau_{J,\mu\nu},
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.10}
$$

with zero Cauchy data. The quadratic terms from the common first $\varepsilon$ metric derivative cancel. The harmonic condition linearizes to the second equation because its common first-order quadratic terms cancel as well. Conservation of $\tau_{J}$ ensures the retarded solution satisfies the divergence condition.

The corresponding Jordan scalar curvature and metric obey

$$
\begin{aligned}
(\Box_\eta-M_s^{2})R_J^{(1)} &= \kappa M_s^{2}\tau_J{}^{\mu}{}_{\mu}, \\
h_J &= k_J-2\alpha\eta R_J^{(1)},
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.11}
$$

and hence

$$
G^{L}_\eta(h_J)+2\alpha(\eta\Box_\eta-\partial\partial)R_J^{(1)}=\kappa\tau_J.
\tag{R.8.2b.C.12}
$$

To verify the normalization without relying on a named scalar-tensor equivalence, the exact trace equation gives the first equation of (R.8.2b.C.11), while $b\phi^{(1)}=2\alpha R_J^{(1)}$ and $g=\exp(-b\phi)g_E$ give the second. For a scalar $\psi$, $G^L_\eta(\psi\eta)=(\eta\Box_\eta-\partial\partial)\psi$, which then proves (R.8.2b.C.12).

On the flat torus slab let $G_0^{\mathrm{ret}}$ and $G_M^{\mathrm{ret}}$ be the unique zero-Cauchy inverses of $\Box_\eta$ and $\Box_\eta-M_s^2$. Precisely, their range is $L^1([0,T];H^{r-1})$ and their zero-Cauchy graph domain consists of $u\in E^r_T$ whose displayed wave operator belongs to that range, as registered for $A_{g}$ in part A §3. Fix, for example, $r=s+2$. These inverses include the spatial zero modes and leave no free homogeneous solution. Then

$$
\begin{aligned}
\bar{k}_J &= -2\kappa G_0^{\mathrm{ret}}\tau_J, \\
R_J^{(1)} &= \kappa M_s^{2}G_M^{\mathrm{ret}}(\tau_J{}^{\mu}{}_{\mu}), \\
h_J &= k_J-2\alpha\eta R_J^{(1)}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.13}
$$

All operators act on the same actual conserved flat limit source. This is the retained massless tensor plus massive scalar response of the original gravitational action, with the scalaron mass $M_{s}$ fixed throughout the weak-source limit.

The Jordan metric $h_{J}$ in (R.8.2b.C.13) is not itself in Lorenz gauge: $\partial^\mu\bar{h}_{J,\mu\nu}=2\alpha\partial_\nu R_J^{(1)}$. If that gauge is desired, solve the additional zero-data retarded equation $\Box_\eta\xi_\nu=2\alpha\partial_\nu R_J^{(1)}$ and set $h'_J=h_J-\partial_\mu\xi_\nu-\partial_\nu\xi_\mu$. Then $\partial^\mu\bar{h}'_{J,\mu\nu}=0$, with unchanged physical source and curvature, and

$$
\begin{aligned}
\bar{h}'_J &= -2\kappa G_0^{\mathrm{ret}}\tau_J \\
&\quad +4\alpha G_0^{\mathrm{ret}}(\eta\Box_\eta-\partial\partial)R_J^{(1)}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.14}
$$

The initially inactive source ensures the stated gauge transformation has zero relevant initial jets. Equation (R.8.2b.C.14) is a gauge conversion of (R.8.2b.C.13), not an alternative source prescription.

Equivalently the trace response is the declared retarded inverse of the original operator $A_\eta=I-6\alpha\Box_\eta$: $R_J^{(1)}=-\kappa(A_\eta)^{-1}_{\mathrm{ret}}\operatorname{trace}(\tau_J)$. The exact nonlinear map uses $A_{g}$ on each solved curved background with the same zero-data convention. Thus both the nonlinear inverse domain and its flat specialization are explicitly registered.

**C.4. A nonzero, genuinely gravitational retained response**

Choose the common lifetime before selecting the witness bump, as in part B §2. Shrink it, if necessary, so that $T<\pi/(2M_s)$. Place a nonzero homogeneous smooth $f$ in a target-time interval reached strictly before $T$, and choose nonzero $\lambda$ small enough that $J=\lambda f$ lies in the fixed source ball. The baseline flat clock is $X_0(t)=qt$. The source therefore activates on the actual guaranteed interval, rather than only after it.

For the exact flat homogeneous solution of (R.8.2b.C.7), the spatial winding is unchanged. Equation (R.8.2b.C.4) with $H=0$ says that the full material energy $\rho^*_\lambda$ is constant. It equals the baseline value because the initial data and initially zero potential agree. Consequently, exactly for every admitted $\lambda$,

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Delta\rho^{*} &= 0, \\
\Delta p^{*} &= -2U_\lambda, \\
\operatorname{trace}(\tau_\lambda) &= -6U_\lambda.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.15}
$$

This cancellation uses the clock's kinetic-energy change. Retaining only the y energy would fail (R.8.2b.C.15) and stress conservation.

The exact sign symmetry and the source differentiability give, uniformly on the window,

$$
\begin{aligned}
&y_\lambda=\lambda y_1+O(\lambda^{3}),\qquad X_\lambda=qt+O(\lambda^{2}), \\
&\ddot{y}_1+m^{2}y_1=-f(qt),\qquad y_1(0)=\dot{y}_1(0)=0, \\
&U_\lambda=\lambda^{2}/2\,[m\,y_1+f(qt)/m]^{2}+O(\lambda^{4}).
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.16}
$$

The displayed leading square is not identically zero. Otherwise $y_1=-f(qt)/m^2$ and its forced equation would imply $d^2f(qt)/dt^2=0$ throughout the interval; vanishing near the initial time would then force $f(qt)=0$, contradicting the active nonzero bump. Thus for sufficiently small nonzero $\lambda$ the exact nonnegative $U_\lambda$ is positive somewhere before $T$. An equivalent exact argument is that $U_\lambda\equiv 0$ would force $\ddot{y}_\lambda=0$ and, from zero $y$ data, $y_\lambda=0$; the clock equation would then give $X_\lambda=qt$, forcing $f(qt)=0$.

Equations (R.8.2b.C.11) and (R.8.2b.C.15) now give

$$
\begin{aligned}
\ddot{R}_\lambda^{(1)}+M_s^{2}R_\lambda^{(1)} &= 6\kappa M_s^{2}U_\lambda, \\
R_\lambda^{(1)}(t) &= 6\kappa M_s\int_0^t\sin[M_s(t-u)]\,U_\lambda(u)\,du.
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.17}
$$

On the chosen interval the sine is positive whenever $0<u<t$ and the source is active. Hence $R_\lambda^{(1)}>0$ at some later time strictly inside the window. It begins at order $\lambda^{2}$ by (R.8.2b.C.16). It is a nonzero scalar-curvature response, so it cannot be removed by a linearized diffeomorphism of the flat limiting geometry. The massive scalar response is physically retained, not a merely re-labelled metric component.

For each such fixed nonzero $\lambda$, (R.8.2b.C.8) implies $\Delta R_{\varepsilon,\lambda}/\varepsilon^2$ converges uniformly to this nonzero function. Choosing $\varepsilon>0$ small enough yields an actual positive-$\varepsilon$ coupled solution with a nonzero retained curvature difference on the common slab. The positivity proof and the quantitative estimate establish a nonempty family of genuine nonlinear gravitational responses, not just a formal coefficient. They do not assert that every conceivable source direction produces a nonzero response.

This witness also admits an intrinsic two-development comparison, beyond a fixed-coordinate subtraction. For each homogeneous Jordan solution use proper cosmic time elapsed from the common initial slice, as in §1. The relative harmonic-gauge lapse is $O(\varepsilon^2\lVert J\rVert^2)$, so the difference of the two proper-time conversion maps is $O(\varepsilon^2\lVert J\rVert^2)$. Baseline curvature and its first time derivative are $O(\varepsilon^2)$. Evaluating the baseline at those two slightly different harmonic times therefore changes the curvature comparison by $O(\varepsilon^4\lVert J\rVert^2)$. The common conversion from harmonic to proper time is $t=\tau+O(\varepsilon)$, so it changes the normalized leading function only by $O(\varepsilon)$. Consequently the nonzero coefficient (R.8.2b.C.17) survives the comparison at equal elapsed Jordan proper time. Proper time and scalar curvature in this homogeneous initial-value construction are invariant under independent coordinate changes of either development.

**C.5. Readout, covariance and the source-exhaustion boundary**

The metric comparison (R.8.2b.C.8) uses the common wave-gauge identification fixed by identical initial coordinate data. It is geometrically meaningful modulo diffeomorphisms fixing those data. The dynamical clock maps react to J even when $\varepsilon=0$. Therefore the raw difference between their two separate clock-coordinate representations of flat metric is not to be divided by $\varepsilon^{2}$.

Scalar relational readout is nevertheless available without dropping clock motion. On a common clock image, define $R_{\mathrm{rel},\varepsilon,J}=R_{\varepsilon,J}\circ X_{\varepsilon,J}^{-1}$. The transported comparison at the same identified spacetime event is exactly

$$
\begin{aligned}
R_{\mathrm{rel},\varepsilon,J}(Y) \\
&\quad -R_{\mathrm{rel},\varepsilon,0}(X_{\varepsilon,0}(X_{\varepsilon,J}^{-1}(Y))) \\
&= [R_{\varepsilon,J}-R_{\varepsilon,0}](X_{\varepsilon,J}^{-1}(Y)).
\end{aligned}
\tag{R.8.2b.C.18}
$$

The transition map appearing here is the actual solved clock map, so its variation and Jacobian are retained. Simultaneous spacetime diffeomorphisms of both developments leave (R.8.2b.C.18) unchanged. By the uniform clock inverse bounds, the normalized right side converges to $R_J^{(1)}\circ X_{\mathrm{flat},J}^{-1}$ with the corresponding lower-norm $O(\varepsilon)$ bound. Its nonzero homogeneous witness is preserved. An untransported difference at equal material labels is a different legitimate observable; its extra coordinate-composition terms must be retained and are already covered by the exact observable map in the local theorem.

Every limit and restriction in Theorem R.8.2b retains $y$, all four clocks, their kinetic stress, the square-completion potential, the $J^2$ contact, and the scalaron of the original action. The independent source variables are the constitutive functions $J$ in the declared open target-function chart; they are not arbitrary externally conserved stress tensors. The coupled source solution produces those tensors and proves their conservation.

This proves the homogeneous and fixed-unit weak-field matching clauses for the direct original-action local nonlinear kernel. It does not claim an Einstein-only scalar-free weak-field branch, K15 compatibility, arbitrary-sign $\beta$, global singularity-free evolution, or a microscopic finite-substrate construction. None is silently substituted for the original effective-action statement.

∎

### R.8.3 Epistemological Status

This appendix separates one conditional family-count result from later flavor models:

- On SM15, the linear and cubic anomaly equations plus a CP-active realization give the smallest admissible count $N=3$; on SM16, the linear equation additionally requires primitive charge-norm minimization.
- The additive-monotone objective selects that smallest count on the effective R.3.5.1a branch.
- $D_4$ triality, $E_8$/Leech scaffolds, and QFI mode counts are compatibility records.
- Root-distance hierarchies, mixings, and phases require the independent flavor certificates described above.

### R.8.4 Conditional Connection to Baryogenesis

Within the Standard Model three-family mixing formalism, a physical CKM phase requires at least three families. A baryon yield additionally requires baryon-number violation, a CP-odd source, nonequilibrium transport, freeze-out, washout, normalization, and uncertainty certificates. The Berry-holonomy and sphaleron entries of Appendix Y are therefore conditional source-model data; they do not by themselves derive the observed $\eta_B$.

The family-count theorem and baryogenesis model are compatible on their common branch. Baryogenesis is not an independent proof of the generation count, because alternative CP sources or enlarged family sectors are outside the theorem's declared class.

### R.8.5 Minimal Family-Charge Selection Audit

<a id="definition-r85a-admissible-minimal-family-charge-selection-problem"></a>**Definition R.8.5a (Admissible Minimal Family-Charge Selection Problem).** Let $b\in\{15,16\}$ denote the SM15 or SM16 spectrum branch of Theorem R.3.4. A candidate is a finite nonzero integer charge multiset
$$
\mathcal Q=\{F_1,\ldots,F_N\}\subset\mathbb Z.
$$
Set
$$
g(\mathcal Q):=\gcd(|F_1|,\ldots,|F_N|),
\qquad
f_i:=\frac{F_i}{g(\mathcal Q)}.
$$
Its primitive representative is $f=(f_1,\ldots,f_N)$. Two candidates are in the same $U(1)_F$ charge orbit exactly when, after a permutation, their primitive representatives differ by one common sign. Equivalently, their integer vectors differ by a nonzero rational scalar and a permutation. This is an equivalence relation; every nonzero orbit has one primitive representative up to sign and permutation.

An admissible branch record satisfies:

1. **(P1) Linear anomaly cancellation:**
   $$
   \sum_{i=1}^N f_i=0.
   $$

2. **(P2) Spectrum-resolved anomaly condition:** on the SM15 branch,
   $$
   \sum_{i=1}^N f_i^3=0;
   $$
   on the SM16 branch, one uniformly charged $\nu_R^i$ per family is included and cancels the gravitational and cubic rows, so no cubic equation is imposed.

3. **(P3) Nontrivial primitive normalization:** $f\ne0$ and $\gcd(|f_1|,\ldots,|f_N|)=1$.

4. **(P4) CP-active realization:** a registered Yukawa/spurion record on these $N$ families has a nonzero rephasing invariant. This condition implies $N\ge3$ but is not equivalent to requiring three distinct numerical charge values. Lemma R.3.4a supplies an existence certificate for the selected orbit.

5. **(P5) PCE objective:** among records satisfying (P1)–(P4), minimize
   $$
   L_b(\mathcal Q)
   =
   L_0+N L_{\mathrm{block}}+L_{\mathrm{mix}}(N)
   +\lambda_F C_F(f),
   \qquad
   C_F(f):=\sum_{i=1}^N f_i^2,
   $$
   where $L_{\mathrm{block}}>0$, $L_{\mathrm{mix}}$ is nondecreasing, and $\lambda_F>0$.

<a id="theorem-r85b-pce-selection-of-n_g-3-in-the-family-charge-class"></a>**Theorem R.8.5b (PCE Selection of $N_g=3$ in the Family-Charge Class).** On either spectrum branch of Definition R.8.5a, the unique minimal-cost charge orbit is represented by
$$
f=(1,-1,0),
$$
equivalently
$$
\mathcal Q=\{a,-a,0\},
\qquad a\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\},
$$
and the selected generation count is $N_g=3$.

*Proof.* For $N=1$, (P1) forces the zero assignment, contradicting (P3). For $N=2$, (P1) gives a vector proportional to $(1,-1)$, but no two-family mixing matrix has a nonzero rephasing-invariant CKM phase, so (P4) fails.

Let $N=3$. On the SM15 branch, write $f_3=-f_1-f_2$. Then
$$
f_1^3+f_2^3+f_3^3=-3f_1f_2(f_1+f_2).
$$
Condition (P2) therefore forces one entry to vanish and the other two to be opposite. Primitive normalization gives $(1,-1,0)$ up to sign and permutation. On the SM16 branch, every nonzero integral vector satisfying (P1) obeys
$$
C_F(f)\ge2,
$$
because at least one positive and one negative integer occur. Equality holds exactly when one entry is $1$, one is $-1$, and all remaining entries vanish. Thus at $N=3$ the unique charge-norm minimizer is again the orbit of $(1,-1,0)$. Lemma R.3.4a supplies the required CP-active realization on this orbit.

For any admissible $N>3$, the same sign argument gives $C_F(f)\ge2$. Hence, relative to the selected three-family representative $f_*=(1,-1,0)$,
$$
L_b(\mathcal Q)-L_b(f_*)
=
(N-3)L_{\mathrm{block}}
+
L_{\mathrm{mix}}(N)-L_{\mathrm{mix}}(3)
+
\lambda_F\bigl(C_F(f)-2\bigr) >0.
$$
Therefore no larger admissible multiset has equal or lower cost, proving both uniqueness of the selected orbit and $N_g=3$. ∎

<a id="remark-r85c-no-exhaustive-classification-of-all-larger-charge-multisets"></a>**Remark R.8.5c (No Exhaustive Classification of All Larger Charge Multisets).** Theorem R.8.5b is a minimal-selection theorem, not an exhaustive classification. For example,
$$
\{-20,-14,-1,17,18\}
$$
has sum $0$ and cube-sum $0$, so it satisfies the arithmetic SM15 equations; it also satisfies the SM16 linear equation. A CP-active Yukawa realization remains a separate (P4) record. If such a record is supplied, this primitive five-family candidate is still PCE-demoted because $N=5$ and $C_F=1210$ exceed the selected values $N=3$ and $C_F=2$.

<a id="corollary-r85d-scope-of-the-u1_f-selection"></a>**Corollary R.8.5d (Scope of the $U(1)_F$ Selection).** Theorem R.8.5b is internal to the two integer $U(1)_F$ spectrum branches of Definition R.8.5a. The SM16 branch already includes one uniformly charged right-handed neutrino per family and is compatible with the broken-$U(1)_F$ type-I-seesaw operators of Lemma R.3.4a. Non-Abelian family symmetries, additional sterile states, response-active extra generations, boundary/interface completions, and defect-fusion labels are different candidate classes with their own anomaly, embedding, threshold, flavor, and PCE ledgers.

*Proof.* Definition R.8.5a fixes the candidate object, its SM15/SM16 spectrum flag, its primitive orbit, and its objective. The listed extensions change at least one of those data and therefore belong to separate comparison classes. ∎

<a id="theorem-r85e-generation-branch-catalog-under-premise-relaxation"></a>**Theorem R.8.5e (Generation-Branch Catalog under Premise Relaxation).** The generation-count result has the following premise-sensitive status.

1. If $U(1)_F$ is a predictive-frame redundancy, either the SM15 or registered SM16 spectrum branch is fixed, a CP-active realization is required, and the PCE objective of Definition R.8.5a is retained, then Theorem R.8.5b selects
   $$
   \{a,-a,0\},
   \qquad
   N_g=3.
   \tag{R.8.5e.1}
   $$

2. If $U(1)_F$ is instead a physical global update channel, Theorem X.8d does not impose quotient descent. Its anomaly/current consistency conditions and its family count must then be recorded on a separate physical-symmetry branch.

3. If CP-active mixing is not required, the anomaly equations alone do not select three families. The only one-family solution of the linear equation is the trivial charge, while the two-family orbit $(1,-1)$ is anomaly-admissible but has no physical CKM phase.

4. If the PCE objective is relaxed, larger arithmetic solutions survive whenever their additional threshold, flavor, neutrino, or CP responses are retained. Response-null duplicates are removed only by the separate PPI/PCE response quotient.

5. A registered SM16 right-handed-neutrino and broken-spurion seesaw is already inside item 1. Non-Abelian family groups, extra sterile states, boundary/interface anomaly inflow, and defect-fusion labels lie outside this integer-$U(1)_F$ catalog and require their own finite anomaly-bordism, determinant-orientation, flavor, and threshold ledgers.

Thus failure of a later flavor texture, CKM/PMNS fit, neutrino mass model, or baryogenesis certificate identifies a downstream branch failure; it refutes the structural family-count result only if it also invalidates one of the premises used in item 1.

*Proof.* Item 1 is Theorem R.8.5b. Item 2 is the redundancy/physical-channel distinction of Theorem X.8d. Item 3 follows from (P1) and the two-family rephasing theorem. Item 4 follows from the explicit objective comparison in Theorem R.8.5b and the independent response quotient. Item 5 is Corollary R.8.5d together with Lemma R.3.4a. ∎

## R.9 Summary

The appendix gives a conditional three-family selection and keeps it separate from the geometric structures used to model masses and mixing. Triality, coding, and lattice geometry support compatibility checks, while physical labels, scales, and response laws remain distinct inputs.

### Technical results ledger

*   **Topology:** On the $d_0=8$ flag-manifold branch, $\pi_2(\Sigma_8)\cong\mathbb Z^7$.
*   **$E_8$ Geometry:** A registered $E_8$ root model supplies candidate squared distances $\{2,4,6,8\}$; its physical embedding and labels are branch data.
*   **Gauge–Topology Map:** The marked embedding induces the stated homomorphism to $X^*(T_{\mathrm{SM}})$; Cartan neutrality gives a candidate sector and full singletness requires the root-action certificate.
*   **Family Minimality:** On SM15, the linear and cubic anomaly equations plus CP capability give the smallest primitive pattern $\{a,-a,0\}$; on SM16, the linear anomaly equation plus primitive charge-norm minimization and CP capability gives the same pattern. Larger admissible patterns exist.
*   **Family Selection:** Effective Proposition R.3.5.1a selects $N_g=3$ under its additive-monotone objective. Response-null removal is supplied separately by Corollary P.6.1b.8.
*   **Compatibility Records:** Triality, the scaled $E_8^{\oplus3}$ scaffold, interface mode count, and Golay code are cross-branch compatibility facts, not further proofs of $N_g=3$.
*   **Flavor Diagnostics:** A registered triad and common leading response give candidate ratios. Physical labels, coefficients, effective dimensions, scales, phases, schemes, and remainders remain Appendix-T certificate data.
*   **Phenomenological Status:** Charged-lepton and quark comparisons are calibration evidence until the flavor certificate is specified independently and evaluated on held-out data.
*   **Gauge/Family Relation:** Gauge and family constructions share some MPU scaffolding but follow different conditional inference chains; neither theorem derives the other.
